| Evidence | EXECUTED. `uniqcount` counts distinct decimal numbers on stdin using four exports from three instances, and matches `reference_uniqcount` on four inputs (including the table-full exit) on Linux x86-64 natively and on Linux AArch64 under qemu-aarch64. MEASURED (`oi36_stdlib_evidence.json`): x86-64 code per used export is 130 to 326 bytes (entry 1,145; artifact 2,306 bytes). The AArch64 frame path is 5 to 9× larger (artifact 13,748 bytes). The store bytes each instance contributes are 2,112 to 4,077, and the unused exports and package objects are absent from the store. Packages are built in 11 ms, verified in 1.6 ms, and compiled in 60 ms (x86-64) and 37 ms (AArch64). Offline tokens (o200k_base): a 268-token interface view, then 1,360 tokens for the application's 73 nodes with 4 package calls, against 3,256 tokens (190 nodes) with the bodies written inline. Vectors: `test_xax_stdlib.py` (4). |
| Limits | One machine scalar return per function on the native backends, so multi-value results are packed (`find_digits` returns a span in one `bits<64>`). Generic instantiation is Python-side construction; there is no in-graph type parameter. Versioning beyond the identity string (compatibility ranges, deprecation) is not decided. The token figures are offline counts, with no model trial (R5). |

## ADR-132 — Self-hosting step S4: scalar operation typing rules are checked by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | One XAX function (`xax_selfhost_typing.py`, store `bootstrap/xax_op_typing.xax`) decides the arity and operand/result type rules of the scalar operation families. These are integer binary operations, `int.truncate`/`int.zero_extend`, `rotate_right`, float arithmetic, `float.compare`, int/float conversions, `float.convert`, and `int.compare` (including link equality). It decodes the type objects itself from their kind, reference count, and body bytes. Its input is the graph's distinct operand and result types plus each covered node's operation, counts, first attribute, and type indices; it returns, per node, outside, proven, or not proven. It runs once per graph, and only after S3e proved every value use valid (so collecting operand types cannot raise). A proven node skips the bootstrap typing checks in every memory-facts pass. A node that is not proven, or that is outside these families, takes the bootstrap checks unchanged, so diagnostics are identical. Default on Linux x86-64; `XAX_TYPING_PYTHON=1` forces the bootstrap. |
| Rationale | S4 is the next rung of the migration ladder: after hashing, store and graph decoding, control flow, and value dominance (S0 to S3e), the verifier's per-node typing is the next pure decision procedure. Soundness is one-sided by construction. The type decoder accepts only single-byte forms and one- or two-byte canonical values, so it can only be stricter than the bootstrap, never looser. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_typing.py`, 4 tests). On 600 random nodes (random families, arities, attributes, and types, including a tuple and a non-canonical type body), the nodes XAX proves are exactly the nodes the bootstrap accepts. Each family proves its well-typed node, and 13 negative vectors are not proven and are rejected by the bootstrap. Accept/reject results with the native path on are identical to the bootstrap's. The committed store equals a fresh build. The full suite passes with the path on by default (868 tests, 17 skipped). MEASURED (`s4_typing_evidence.json`): all 2,419 covered nodes of `jsonmin`, `uniqcount`, and the S2 and S3d self-host stores are proven. `verify_store` time moves by −8% to +7% (marshalling costs about what the skipped checks cost). Store 14,956 bytes; native code 4,763 bytes. |
| Limits | Aggregate, sum, call, memory, atomic, resource, meta, and target operations still type in Python (S4b). Memory-fact tracking (`_end_heap_views` and the pointer/owner/effect facts) is unchanged and still Python. The Python side still marshals the type and node tables. |

## ADR-133 — Self-hosting step S4b: aggregate and sum typing rules are checked by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The S4 typing function (ADR-132) also decides `aggregate.make`, `aggregate.get`, `sum.make`, `sum.tag`, and `sum.get`. Its input now lists each type's reference table, and a second pass decodes tuple, array, and sum type bodies with canonical ULEBs of at most three bytes and an exact end. Reference indices must be in range and every reference used. Elements and variants must be scalars the first pass decoded (`bits`, float, link; never proof types). The node rules mirror the bootstrap: `make` operands equal the tuple elements in order (or `count` copies of the array element), `get` and `sum.make/get` indices are in range and select that element or variant, and `sum.tag`'s `bits<W>` result has `2^W` at least the variant count (the bit length of `count - 1` is at most `W`). Aggregates of aggregates, and any type the decoder does not accept, are left to the bootstrap. |
| Rationale | Aggregate and sum typing are, after the scalar families, the remaining typing rules that are pure checks. The call, memory, atomic, and resource branches update the pointer, owner, and effect facts as they check, so they move with that fact tracking (S4c). |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_typing.py`, 4 tests). 1,000 random nodes across all covered families: every proven node is accepted by the bootstrap, and every accepted node is proven unless it involves a nested aggregate. Nine well-typed and seven ill-typed aggregate/sum vectors are added to the family test. Accept/reject results are unchanged on the production path. Full suite: 868 tests, 17 skipped. MEASURED (`s4_typing_evidence.json`): 2,444/2,444 covered nodes of the four corpus stores are proven (the corpus has only 25 aggregate nodes, so the random corpus is the main aggregate evidence). The store grows to 29,664 bytes and the native code to 12,852 bytes. `verify_store` medians move by −30% to −3% against the bootstrap-only path; host timing noise is of the same order. |
| Limits | Typing for call, function-address, foreign, indirect, constant, memory, atomic, resource, meta, and target nodes, and all memory-fact tracking, remain Python (S4c). |

## ADR-134 — Self-hosting step S4c: resource, effect, and meta typing rules are checked by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The XAX typing function (ADR-132, ADR-133) also decides the resource/effect operations and the meta operations. (1) A third type pass decodes effect, resource, and opaque type bodies with the bootstrap's canonical rules: a domain in 1..11 and an optional positive instance; the stack-owner short form, or kind, state, flags (known bits only), instance, and a strictly increasing transition list without the type's own state, rejecting the stack owner's long form; and an opaque kind in 1..7. Values longer than three ULEB bytes are left to the bootstrap. (2) `effect.step`: matching effect operands and results, pairwise-distinct effect types, and pairwise-distinct operand values (Python now marshals the values' identities as extra node words). `resource.acquire/transfer/transition/release/discard/split/join`: operand and result counts, a matching effect continuation, and the flag, kind, affinity, instance, and transition-list rules. (3) Meta operations: arity, opaque operand kinds or `bits` operands, and the result form, including `meta.target_supports`' operation attribute. `call.*`, `constant`, memory, atomic, and target nodes keep their Python checks because those branches update the pointer, owner, and effect facts (S4d). |
| Rationale | These are the remaining node checks that read only types. The checks that also run for these nodes outside the typing chain (linear flow, resource-join siblings, `_end_heap_views`) are not skipped. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_typing.py`, 4 tests). The agreement test now uses 2,000 random nodes over every covered family. For every proven node, the store's outcome (accepted, or the exact diagnostic code, rule, and entity) is identical with the XAX path on and off, and any rejection comes from a later pass, never a typing rule. Every node the bootstrap accepts is proven, except nested aggregates. Nine well-typed and eleven ill-typed resource/effect/meta vectors are added. On 400 further samples, outcomes are identical with the path on and off. Full suite: 868 tests, 17 skipped. MEASURED (`s4_typing_evidence.json`): 2,447/2,447 covered nodes across five corpus stores are proven (including the M14 self-hosted compiler's meta nodes). Store 68,547 bytes; native code 32,519 bytes; `verify_store` times are unchanged within host noise. |
| Limits | The corpus has few resource and meta nodes, so the random and vector tests carry most of that evidence. Typing for `call.direct/group_member/foreign/indirect`, `function.address`, `constant`, memory, atomic, and target nodes, and all memory-fact tracking, remain Python (S4d). |

## ADR-135 — Self-hosting step S4d.1: constants and block terminators are typed by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | S4d is split. The call, memory, atomic, and target branches check contracts and update the pointer, owner, and effect facts in one pass, and the fact system is about 2,000 lines of Python, so it moves in stages. S4d.1 moves the two remaining typing rules that need no facts. (1) `constant`: XAX decodes the constant object (kind, one type reference, a length-prefixed value with an exact end) and validates the value against its scalar type: `ceil(W/8)` bytes with the unused high bits clear for `bits<W>`; 4 or 8 bytes with only the canonical quiet NaN for floats; eight zero bytes for links. It also checks the contract: no operands, one result of the constant's type. A proven constant still records its null-link fact in Python. (2) Terminators: a conditional branch's condition is `bits<1>`, and every edge's argument types equal the target block's parameters. Python re-checked these in every memory-fact pass; a proven block now skips them. The node stream's extra word carries the constant's object index, and a block section (parameters, terminator kind, condition type, edges with argument types) follows the nodes. |
| Rationale | These are the last checks in the fact loop that read only types and objects. Call contracts are not moved yet, because Python decodes the same callee interfaces for its fact updates; they move with the fact system (S4d.2). |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_typing.py`, 6 tests). The random agreement corpus now draws constants from nine valid and six invalid objects (a high bit above the width, a wrong length, a non-canonical NaN, a wrong float length, a tuple type, a non-null link). Outcomes and exact diagnostics are identical with the path on and off. Nine valid constants are proven, and six invalid constants plus one wrong-result constant are not proven and are rejected by the bootstrap. Terminator vectors: a well-typed branch is proven; a non-`bits<1>` condition, mismatched argument types or counts, and an undecodable condition type are not. 150 random branching graphs give identical outcomes with the path on and off. Full suite: 870 tests, 17 skipped. MEASURED (`s4_typing_evidence.json`): across five corpus stores, 3,374/3,374 covered nodes (927 constants) and 427/427 terminators are proven. Store 80,461 bytes; native code 38,479 bytes; `verify_store` times are unchanged within host noise (one run measured one store 27% slower, the rerun 10% faster). |
| Limits | Python still marshals types, nodes, and blocks, and still checks call contracts, `function.address`, and memory, atomic, and target nodes, along with all pointer/owner/effect fact tracking and linear flow (S4d.2 onward). |

## ADR-136 — Self-hosting step S4d.2a: memory-free graphs skip the fact system

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The first stage of moving the memory-fact system decides when it has nothing to do. (1) The XAX typing function checks `call.direct` contracts. It decodes the callee function object (a graph reference to a `GRAPH_FRAGMENT`, not a recursion group, then parameter and return reference lists with an exact end) and requires every interface type to be a type its decoders validated and equal to the node's operand or result type in order. (2) It reports whether the graph is memory-free: every type object it was given is decoded and validated, and none is a type the fact system tracks (links, memory effects, and resources of the stack-storage, heap-owner, or heap-view kinds; pointers and other undecoded forms already fail the "decoded" condition). (3) When a graph is memory-free and XAX proved every node (including calls and constants) and every terminator, `_parse_graph_uncached` runs zero memory-fact passes. It records operand and return types directly, and linear flow (`_verify_linear_flow`) still runs. Elsewhere a proven call contract does not skip its branch, because that branch also borrows views and resources. |
| Rationale | In a memory-free graph every fact table stays empty: there are no pointers, owners, memory effects, links, allocations, or view triples, and resource call contracts need a stack owner or pointer. So the passes record nothing and can reject nothing beyond the typing XAX already proved, and loops no longer cost repeated passes. This also fixes the interface for the next stages: each fact family that moves to XAX widens the set of graphs that skip the Python passes. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_typing.py`, 7 tests). The random agreement corpus adds `call.direct` against five callees (scalar, multi-result, sum, tuple, and memory-frontier interfaces), with outcomes and exact diagnostics identical with the path on and off. 150 random branching graphs (memory-free, so on the skip path) give identical outcomes. A pure caller of a pure callee is fully proven and memory-free; a caller with a memory frontier, or of a callee with one, is not. Full suite: 870 tests, 17 skipped. MEASURED (`s4_typing_evidence.json`): 5 of 20 corpus graphs are memory-free (one BLAKE3 graph and the four M14 compiler graphs); the memory-heavy programs (`jsonmin`, `uniqcount`, the CFG analysis) correctly never skip, and their calls with pointer interfaces stay with Python. Timings move from −30% to +27% between runs on this host, so no speedup is claimed; the tiny M14 stores pay about 0.2–0.3 ms of fixed overhead. Store 95,971 bytes; native code 46,118 bytes. |
| Limits | Any graph that uses memory still runs the full Python fact passes. Next (S4d.2b): stack storage facts (allocation, owner, effect, initialized intervals, end of lifetime, leaks), then heap views, links, and pointer windows. |

## ADR-137 — Self-hosting step S4d.2b: the memory-fact passes run as an XAX engine (stack storage)

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) A facts engine (`xax_selfhost_facts.py`) reimplements the bootstrap's memory-fact passes as XAX functions, called by the typing function over a facts section of the same input. It keeps per-value pointer facts (storage, element, permission, offset, extent, alignment, alias class, window, record, link target and record) stamped with the pass, and owner and effect facts stamped with the block visit, mirroring the bootstrap's pass-wide `global_pointers` and per-block owner and effect maps. It also keeps per-site live and ended flags, consumer stamps, block-fact records (per-parameter facts plus ended and live site sets) for edge exits and block seeds, and immutable initialized-interval lists. It runs the bootstrap's algorithm: blocks in reverse postorder, arriving edge facts from this pass or the previous one (stale otherwise), the same meet (equal facts, joined links, intersected intervals, unioned sets), and passes until no edge is stale or the seeds repeat, within `2B + 2` passes. (2) The engine either accepts or declines. It accepts only when every node is typing-proven or modelled, every check holds, and the passes converge, and it outputs each value's pointer extent. On acceptance (with every terminator proven) the verifier skips its Python fact passes and takes `pointer_extents` from the engine; on decline the Python passes run and raise the exact diagnostic. (3) Stage S4d.2b models `stack.alloc`, `stack.end`, `address.offset`, `load.bits.le`, `store.bits.le`, `pointer.cast`, and direct calls whose memory frontier passes through without stack resources. Graphs with links, heap views, heap owners, records as pointer elements, windows, a stack-owner entry, or any other memory, atomic, foreign, indirect, group, or target node decline. (4) The typing tables now decode pointer types (`_decode_pointer_type`'s rules, pointer elements to depth two) and the exact stack-owner and memory-effect forms. Helpers take at most two register arguments (the native ABI passes four machine values, two of them the views); further arguments travel in header words. |
| Rationale | The fact passes are the largest remaining Python part of the verifier. Accept-or-decline keeps every stage sound: the engine may decline anything it does not model, but it must never accept what the bootstrap rejects, and its outputs (pointer extents) must be identical. The full suite exercises this, because every negative memory test still runs with the engine on. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_facts.py`). On 300 random stack programs (straight-line and looping, with pointers, owners, and frontiers carried through block parameters) and 14 mutation kinds, outcomes, exact diagnostics, pointer extents, and return types are identical with the engine on and off. The engine accepted all 73 valid programs and the 9 mutated ones that are still valid, and declined every program the bootstrap rejects. Across the compiler, general-language, and OI-06 suites it accepted 43 graphs with every test passing. Full suite: 872 tests, 17 skipped. MEASURED: the typing-plus-facts store is 378,841 bytes, with 170,907 bytes of native code, compiled in about 1.8 s once per process. The full suite takes 138 s, up from about 100 s, mostly that per-process compile. The corpus programs use heap views, so the engine accepts only the 5 memory-free corpus graphs at this stage. |
| Limits | Heap views, checked accesses, foreign calls and allocators, pointer address/rebase windows, links and records, atomics, indirect and group calls, function addresses, and target operations still decline to the Python passes (S4d.2c onward). |

## ADR-138 — Self-hosting step S4d.2c: the facts engine models heap views, foreign calls, and view-passing calls

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The XAX facts engine (ADR-137) now also models, with the bootstrap's rules: (1) borrowed entry views (`_entry_heap_view_facts`: each pointer/view/effect triple seeds storage `(-2, index)`, initialized when the view's state says so) and the triple shape check; (2) `_end_heap_views` after every node except `call.direct`, `call.foreign`, and `heap.view`; (3) `checked.load/store.bits.le`; (4) `heap.view` over an allocator result (allocation, owner, and allocator-effect provenance, static size, alignment, token type); (5) `pointer.address` and `pointer.rebase` windows, with windowed loads requiring the whole window initialized and windowed stores proving nothing new; (6) `call.foreign`: the engine decodes the foreign-function carrier itself (prefix, ABI from `FOREIGN_ABIS`, library, name, input and output CIDs matched against a CID table of the stream's types, allocator and deallocator contracts), checks the call contract, ended operands, deallocation of a heap view or heap owner, frontier hand-over to memory outputs, and allocator results (sizes from `constant` operands of at most eight bytes); (7) `call.direct` and `call.group_member` view borrowing (`_verify_heap_view_call`: whole live views and frontiers, initialized views, views given back in order by type, unreturned views ended); (8) returned views (`_verify_heap_view_return`: whole, live, initialized, and borrowed views in order). (9) Typing now decodes recursion-group member functions for `call.direct` (the group decoded whole, as `_decode_recursion_group` does), and both ULEB decoders accept five bytes (view extents past 2^21). Each decline records the check that caused it (`NativeTyping.decline_reason`) for diagnosis. The native code is cached on disk, keyed by the store and backend sources. |
| Rationale | These are the operations the real programs use: views from `mmap`, checked parsing loops, `pointer_rebase` read windows, and parser functions that borrow views and return them. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_facts.py`, 2 tests). 300 random borrowed-view programs (checked accesses, rebase windows, view-passing calls, loops) and 9 mutation kinds give identical outcomes, diagnostics, extents, and returns with the engine on and off, and the engine accepts every valid one. The 300 stack programs (ADR-137) still agree. On the corpus, the engine accepts all 20 graphs (`jsonmin` 8, `uniqcount` 5, the S2 BLAKE3 store 2, the S3d CFG store 1, the M14 compiler 4), with all 643 pointer extents identical. Full suite: 872 tests, 17 skipped, in 131 s (about 100 s before the engine). MEASURED (`s4_typing_evidence.json`): `verify_store` with the XAX passes takes 85 against 113 ms for `jsonmin`, 23 against 31 for `uniqcount`, and 34 against 44 for the CFG store. The typing-plus-facts store is 1,031,986 bytes, with 454,135 bytes of native code. A first compile takes about 15 s; cached starts take 5 ms. |
| Limits | Still declined to the Python passes: links and records (`link.*`, record elements, `heap.view` with a link target), atomics, `raw.load`, stack resource contracts (calls and entries with stack owners), lend entries, `call.indirect`, `function.address`, and target operations (S4d.2d). |

## ADR-139 — Self-hosting step S4d.2d: the facts engine decides every remaining family (S4 complete)

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The XAX facts engine (ADR-137, ADR-138) now models, with the bootstrap's rules: (1) atomics (`atomic.load/store/rmw/cmpxchg/fence`) and `raw.load.bits.le`; (2) stack resource call and entry contracts (pass, store, load, and mixed shapes), with each direct call's callee summary passed as aux words; (3) `function.address` (opaque function, code-entry, and lend-entry types, the entry interface decoded from the callee), `call.indirect`, and foreign lend entries (`_verify_lend_entries`); (4) `target.op` in a new module, `xax_selfhost_target.py`: it decodes and validates the native target package field by field as `decode_native_target` does (identity, machine profile rules for every architecture, register lists, operation and terminator sets, concurrency and handler entries, accelerator topology and memory spaces, operation contracts), then checks the node against its contract (attributes, scope, memory spaces, arity, operand and result constraints, resource transitions, effect continuation); (5) records and links: record layouts and field offsets (`_record_layout`), view-record strides, field addresses, rebase strides, padding-aware window initialization (`_window_initialized`), `link.make/follow/target`, null-link constants, loaded and stored links, `heap.view` link targets, link dependents at every release, foreign calls touching link-bearing storage, callee `link_target` declarations at view calls, self-linked borrowed and returned views, and entry-block declarations. New typing tables: `OPID` (opaque identity types) and `EINST` (effect instance). The typing views are 16 MB in and 256 MB out, and the CFG views 16 MB in and 32 MB out. Both are allocated once per process and touched only as far as a graph needs. Type bodies up to 4 KB are marshalled (identity types embed CIDs). |
| Rationale | S4 is complete when the XAX engine decides every graph the bootstrap accepts, with Python left only to produce the diagnostic on a decline or a rejection. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED. `test_xax_selfhost_s4d2d.py` (2 tests): every program of the link suites (valid, and each forged step: wrong field offset, bits into a link, record stride, foreign table link, untargeted table, non-root follow, a target ending first, a foreign write, cross-storage calls) and 240 `target.op` mutations (target body bytes, attributes, result types, dropped operands) give identical outcomes and diagnostics with the engine on and off, and the engine accepts every valid one. The stack and view differentials (ADR-137, ADR-138) still agree. A gap analysis instruments `_parse_graph_uncached` over the whole suite and counts every graph the bootstrap accepts that the engine declines. What remains is only the trusted seed: 45 graphs of the typing program itself, parsed while it is being built, and 3 graphs parsed while the CFG and decoder helpers are themselves loading. Every graph of the self-hosting stores is accepted, up to 98,122 values and 1,594 blocks. All 20 corpus graphs are accepted. Full suite: 875 tests, 17 skipped. MEASURED (`s4_typing_evidence.json`): `verify_store` takes 98 ms with the XAX passes against 122 ms bootstrap-only for `jsonmin`, 24 against 32 for `uniqcount`, 36 against 42 for the CFG store, and 67 against 74 for the BLAKE3 store. The typing-plus-facts store is 2,718,650 bytes, with 1,030,596 bytes of native code. The largest store's graph peaked at about 760 MB of process memory. |
| Limits | A view call's callee `link_target` declarations and a resource call's callee summary are read structurally by Python (no verification, as the bootstrap's own call-site checks read them) and passed to the engine as aux words. The callee's own verification checks them. The XAX helper programs cannot check their own graphs before they exist; those graphs are verified by the bootstrap (trusted seed). Accelerator target packages are modelled only for the accelerator architecture. |

## ADR-140 — Self-hosting step S5a: the RISC-V backend's code generation is an XAX program

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The RV64IM backend (ADR-113) now generates code with an XAX program (`xax_selfhost_riscv64_backend.py`, store `bootstrap/xax_riscv64_backend.xax`), run natively in-process like the typing program. Its stages: (1) block-level liveness over bitsets to a fixpoint, with values' interval hulls (definitions, uses, live-in and live-out boundaries); (2) a linear scan over s1-s11 in (start, end, value key) order, spilling the furthest-ending interval; (3) the frame layout (ra, saved registers, spill slots, edge temporaries, 16-byte alignment); (4) the lowering of every operation in the integer subset (wrapping arithmetic with width masks, bitwise ops, `divu`/`remu` with the zero-divisor trap, rotates, width changes, constants, signed and unsigned compares, direct calls, resource and effect no-ops) and of the four terminators, with parallel edge copies through temporaries when a destination is also a source; (5) the `li` planner; (6) frame accesses past the 12-bit range; (7) jumps patched at the end with the ±1 MiB check. `compile_riscv64` uses it by default (`backend="auto"`); `"python"` forces the bootstrap generator, and `"xax"` fails if the program cannot run or declines. The input is a structural stream of the closure: per function, the blocks, value widths, nodes, operands, attributes, and terminators. Python marshals it after the bootstrap's closure check. On a decline (over 8 machine arguments, over 1 machine result, a jump out of range, an operation outside the subset), the bootstrap generator runs and raises the exact diagnostic. |
| Rationale | S5 moves the backend from store bytes to image bytes into XAX. Code generation is the larger part; the input side, feeding the program from the XAX store and graph decoders instead of the marshal, is S5b. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_riscv64_backend.py`, 6 tests). These cases give identical `Riscv64Image` values (code, function offsets, semantic ranges) with `backend="xax"` and `backend="python"`: 60 random programs with calls at widths 8, 13, 32, 47, and 64; 98 `li` edge and random constants (including `0x7FFF…FFFF`, where the bootstrap's unbounded `v - low` would overflow 64 bits, so the program computes `(v >> 12) + bit 11`); loops with 12, 24, and 300 live values (frames past 2047 bytes); and traps with division. A rejected program raises the same diagnostic either way, and the committed store is the built program. The existing RISC-V suites (emulator differential, traps, self-compilation fixed point) pass on the XAX path. Full suite: 881 tests, 17 skipped. MEASURED (`s5_backend_evidence.json`): `compile_riscv64` takes 44 ms against 102 ms for the 300-value loop (73,540-byte image), 2.5 against 5.9 ms for the 24-value loop, and 1.5 against 1.6 ms for a small random function. |
| Limits | Python still marshals the closure: it parses each graph, classifies erased proof callees, decodes constant values, and reads the value widths. These are structural reads after verification. The image metadata (offsets in bytes, semantic ranges, parameter and return widths) is assembled in Python from the program's output. |

## ADR-141 — Self-hosting step S5b: the RISC-V backend runs from store objects to image bytes in XAX (S5 complete)

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The XAX backend program (ADR-140) now has its own front end. Python no longer parses, classifies, or orders anything for it. It copies every store object in store order: kind, reference indices, CID (four big-endian words), and payload. A graph fragment's payload is the S3c XAX graph-decoder stream; any other object's is its body bytes. From these the program decides: (1) the target's operation and terminator sets, and that the architecture is RISC-V; (2) each function's interface (graph, parameter and return types; group members decline); (3) value widths and proof types (`_machine_width`); (4) constant values (little-endian, up to eight bytes); (5) the call closure from the entry, skipping erased proof callees (`_is_erased_proof_function`: a proof-only interface and one block of resource/effect nodes that returns) and checking every operation and terminator against the target; (6) the function order, entry first and then by CID; (7) value numbering from the decoder stream's value references. It then writes the internal stream the code generator consumes and reports the function order and the entry's machine parameter and return widths. The S5a marshal is removed. |
| Rationale | With S5b, the step from store bytes to image bytes is XAX: the store decoder (S3), the graph decoder (S3c), verification (S3d–S4), and this backend program. Python moves buffers between them and produces diagnostics. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_riscv64_backend.py`, 7 tests, `backend="xax"` failing on any decline). The S5a differentials still give identical images, offsets, and ranges: 60 random programs with calls at five widths, 98 constants, 12/24/300-value spills, and traps. A new case checks that an erased proof callee and an unreachable function in the store are left out of the closure exactly as the bootstrap does. The RISC-V emulator, trap, and self-compilation suites pass on this path. Full suite: 882 tests, 17 skipped. MEASURED (`s5_backend_evidence.json`): `compile_riscv64` takes 16.7 ms against 107.5 ms for the 300-value loop (6.4×), 1.5 against 6.1 ms for the 24-value loop, and 0.6 against 1.6 ms for a small random function. |
| Limits | A graph body over the decoder's 1 MiB capacity, or one it defers, runs on the bootstrap generator; so does any store that does not fit the 16 MiB input view. Recursion-group member functions decline. The image object (`Riscv64Image`: bytes, CID-keyed offsets, semantic ranges) is assembled in Python from the program's output words. |

## ADR-142 — Self-hosting step S6a: resource and effect linearity is decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The facts engine (ADR-137 to ADR-139) now runs `_verify_linear_flow` as an XAX pass (`_linear_flow`) right after it lays out the graph, and reports the result in header word `H_LINEAR`. The pass covers four rules. (1) A proof parameter (effect or resource) of a non-entry block has predecessors, and every incoming edge passes it. (2) Every proof value is consumed once: by exactly one node of its own block, or by its own block's terminator only. Under a conditional branch, each edge passes it once; after a trap, an effect is not continued; otherwise it has exactly one continuation. (3) For `resource.join`, both pieces must be results 0 and 1 of one `resource.split`, reached through transfers and transitions. (4) The walk from those pieces, and from the split's operand through splits, transfers, and transitions, must not pass a non-entry block parameter; on such paths the bootstrap's origin comparison depends on the walk, so XAX does not prove them. When the flag is set, `_parse_graph` skips the bootstrap pass. Otherwise the bootstrap pass runs and raises the exact diagnostic. |
| Rationale | Linear flow was the last whole-graph verifier pass still decided only in Python. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_linear.py`). 400 random resource lifecycles (acquire, transition, split, transfer, join, release; straight-line and branching) and eight mutation kinds (duplicate use, dropped resource, one conditional edge, cross-block node use, cross-block terminator use, stranger joins, same-piece joins, joins through a block parameter) give identical outcomes and diagnostics with the XAX proof used and not used. The rules reached include `RESOURCE-LINEAR-CONTINUATION`, `RESOURCE-EACH-CONTROL-PATH`, `RESOURCE-EXPLICIT-BLOCK-PARAMETER`, and `RESOURCE-JOIN-SIBLINGS`. The proof never holds for a rejected graph, and it holds for every valid program except the joins through a parameter. Across the whole suite, every graph was checked with the bootstrap pass forced on: XAX proved 6,518 graphs, with no graph proven that the bootstrap rejects. The only valid graphs it did not prove are the join-through-parameter programs. Full suite: 883 tests, 17 skipped. |
| Limits | Joins whose pieces or split operand are reached through a non-entry block parameter are left to the bootstrap pass. |

## ADR-143 — Self-hosting step S6b.1: type and constant objects are decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The typing program (ADR-132) gains a table, `OBJOK`. For each listed entry it holds 1 when the entry is either a type that the XAX decoders fully validate (`_known`: bits, float, link, pointer, effect, resource, opaque, opaque identity, tuple, array, sum) or a constant that `_decode_constant` accepts. The constant check uses the constant rule's object core: one reference to a scalar type, the exact body, canonical bits width with zero spare bits, float width with the canonical quiet NaN, and an all-zero null link. `marshal(..., objects=...)` lists objects without any graph. At the start of `verify_store`, one native call gives verdicts for every type and constant not yet proven. The proven CIDs go into a process-wide set, which is sound because validity is a function of the content-addressed CID. `_verify_type` returns early for a proven CID, and `verify_object` skips `_decode_constant` for one. Every other object, and every rejection, takes the bootstrap path and its exact diagnostic. |
| Rationale | Types and constants are the most numerous verified objects after graphs. With S6b.1, the corpus stores verify with no Python type or constant decoding. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_objects.py`). Random type objects of all eleven forms (nested up to depth two), bits, float, and null-link constants, and 45% of them mutated (body bytes changed, appended, or truncated; references dropped) give 600 objects: 379 valid and 221 rejected by the bootstrap. XAX proves all 379 valid ones and none of the rejected ones. On `jsonmin` (12 types, 90 constants), `uniqcount` (14 types, 23 constants), and the M14 compiler store, `verify_store` runs no Python `_verify_type` or `_decode_constant` at all. Full suite: 884 tests, 17 skipped. |
| Limits | Bits widths above 16,383 and kind-only entries (bodies over 4 KB) are left to the bootstrap. Function, recursion-group, call-contract, module and root, build, and target objects are S6b.2. |

## ADR-144 — Self-hosting step S6b.2: functions, reference lists, call contracts, and rootedness are decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | A new XAX program, the store verifier (`xax_selfhost_verify.py`, store `bootstrap/xax_store_verifier.xax`), runs natively at the start of `verify_store`. It reads the S5b object table: every object's kind, reference indices, CID, and payload, plus a flag per type that ADR-143 proved. With the bootstrap's rules, it decides four things. (1) Ordinary functions (`_verify_function`): the exact interface body, a graph-fragment carrier, parameter and return types that are proven types, exact reference use, and no `call.group_member` nodes. It also checks the graph contract from the S3c graph-decoder stream: the entry block's parameters are the interface's, and every `return` returns values of the interface's return types. (2) Module and program-root reference lists (`_verify_reference_list`). (3) Call contracts (`_decode_call_contract`). (4) The store as a whole: every object reachable from the root, with no reference cycle. `verify_object` skips the Python checks for a proven object. For a function it still parses the graph (cached and XAX-backed), so an invalid graph fails exactly where and how the bootstrap would. Recursion groups, group member functions, targets, and build objects stay with the bootstrap. Two related changes: the E DSL's `var` now binds a value another variable already holds as a fresh copy (`value + 0`), and a helper program that is still importing makes the store verifier stand aside, as the other helpers do. |
| Rationale | With S6b.2, verification of the corpus stores runs almost no Python object-level checks. The exceptions are the recursion-group member functions and the per-graph glue around the XAX passes. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_verify.py`). Random stores of one to three functions (straight-line and branching graphs returning parameters, node results, or constants) with a call contract, and their mutations (function body bytes, a return type of another type, module body bytes, contract boolean bytes, an unreachable object), give the same outcome and exact diagnostic with the verifier's verdicts used and not used. No verdict holds for the object the bootstrap rejects, and every function, list, contract, and store-level check of the 65 valid stores is proven. On the corpus, every ordinary function (17 of 17: `jsonmin`, `uniqcount`, the BLAKE3, CFG, and M14 stores) and every module, root, and store-level check is proven. The one function left to Python is a recursion-group member. The verifier adds about 0.2 ms to `verify_store` on a one-function store and 2.5 ms on `jsonmin`. Full suite: 885 tests, 17 skipped. |
| Limits | While building this program, a variable bound to another variable's current SSA value lost that value across later control flow in the native code. That was the x86-64 register-resident lowering of a large function: placing the snapshot in memory, or binding a fresh value, made it correct. It did not reproduce in small programs (see the handoff), so the root cause in `xax_x86_64_regalloc` is open. The `E.var` fresh-copy rule keeps every E-DSL program (typing, facts, store verifier, RISC-V backend) out of the pattern, and their stores were rebuilt. Recursion groups, group member functions, targets, and build objects remain bootstrap-verified. |

## ADR-145 — B1–B4 for the XAX RISC-V backend: it compiles itself to a fixed point (views profile)

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | A RISC-V *views profile* target (`riscv64_views_target()`, identity `riscv64-baremetal-views-v1`) adds the checked heap-view load and store (operations 38 and 39) to the S5 RISC-V operations. That is enough to compile the XAX RISC-V backend program itself, whose functions take and return lent input and output views. In this profile, the bootstrap generator (`xax_riscv64.py`) and the XAX backend program (`xax_selfhost_riscv64_backend.py`) lower the same five things. (1) A pointer is one 64-bit value. (2) A checked access traps unless `offset + size <= extent`. The extent is the `heap_view<N>` instance that follows the pointer in its parameter or result list (`_pointer_extents`). After the check comes `add` and the sized load or store. (3) Each borrowed view a direct call gives back (`borrowed_view_returns`) is the pointer passed in. Uses read that operand, and neither the call result nor the function's own matching returns are machine values. (4) Jumps are `auipc` + `jalr` pairs with a ±2 GiB range, because the backend's largest function is about 1 MB of code, past `jal`'s ±1 MiB. (5) In-bounds skip branches skip 12 bytes instead of 8. In the XAX program, the front end gains a third pass (extents and borrowed-view aliases per value id, through a new `borrowed` helper) and a far-jump flag taken from the target's operations. The generations are: gen1, the backend program run natively (x86-64); gen2, the views-profile image gen1 makes of the backend's own store; and gen3, the image gen2 makes of that store, run in the Unicorn RV64 emulator. `image_from_backend_output` builds an image from the program's output wherever it ran. |
| Rationale | This is the self-hosting S-ladder's first B1–B4 evidence for real compiler code rather than the M14 semantic-image closure. The XAX backend, gen1, compiles itself (B1). Its RISC-V image, gen2, is itself a working compiler (B2). gen2 agrees with gen1 on other programs (B3). Compiling the compiler again reproduces it byte for byte (B4). |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_fixed_point.py`, `benchmarks/bench_selfhost_fixed_point.py`, `selfhost_fixed_point_evidence.json`). B1/B2: the backend store has 17 functions and is 704,433 bytes. gen1 compiles it to a 1,959,876-byte image (sha256 `8ac6d3c5…`), identical (code, offsets, semantic ranges) to the bootstrap generator's image. B3: on 12 random integer programs with calls (widths 8, 32, and 64), gen2 under emulation produces exactly gen1's images. B4: gen2 compiling the backend store gives gen3, byte-identical to gen2, in at most 22 billion emulated instructions (338 s under Unicorn, against 0.57 s for gen1 natively and 4.1 s for the bootstrap generator). The S5 RISC-V suites are unchanged and pass. Full suite: 888 tests, 18 skipped (B4 is opt-in). |
| Limits | The fixed point is for one component (the RISC-V backend) on one target (RV64 under emulation). The verifier, typing, and fact programs are not yet compiled this way. B4 runs for minutes, so its test is opt-in (`XAX_FIXED_POINT=1`) and its result is recorded as evidence. The views profile supports 1-, 2-, 4-, and 8-byte checked accesses through pointers with a statically known extent; others are declined (the bootstrap gives `RISCV64-CHECKED-ACCESS`). |

## ADR-146 — Self-hosting step S6b.3: recursion groups, group member functions, and targets are decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The store verifier (ADR-144) now also decides three kinds of object with the bootstrap's rules. (1) Recursion groups (`_verify_recursion_group`). It checks the member list (`_decode_recursion_group`: a nonempty count, graph carriers, proven types, exact end and reference use) and each member graph's contract. Every `call.group_member` must name a member and match that member's parameter and result types. Breadth-first discovery from every member must reach all members (one recursive strongly connected component). The stored order must be canonical: member 0's discovery order is the identity, and no other start gives a smaller descriptor. Descriptors compare member keys (the graph's reference CIDs, the graph bytes without member-call spans, then parameter and return types) and then renumbered callees. This uses the bootstrap's own key, so groups whose members share every reference, or are byte-for-byte equal, are decided too. For that, the verifier's object table now carries each graph's body bytes after its decoder stream. (2) Group member functions: `[group, member]` with one reference, a proven group, and a member it has. (3) Targets (`decode_native_target(allow_carrier=True)`): identity-only carriers, and profile-1 (general) and profile-2 (concurrency: atomic widths, scopes, families, handler entries) targets on every architecture, with the bootstrap's machine, register, and canonical-list rules. Groups are decided first, because member functions read their verdicts. A proven group's member graphs, and a proven ordinary function's graph, are still parsed in the bootstrap's order, so an invalid graph fails exactly as before. A proven member function parses nothing: its graphs go with its group. |
| Rationale | With S6b.3, every object a corpus program store holds, except build and package objects, is decided by XAX. That includes the jsonmin recursion-group member that S6b.2 left to Python. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`test_xax_selfhost_groups.py`). 220 random stores were tested: groups of one to three members in canonical order, with ring and extra calls and members that tie on their references, plus their member functions and a random built-in target. Mutations covered group, member-function, and target body bytes, a member index past the group, group calls with another interface, and a member no call reaches. All 220 give the same outcome and exact diagnostic with the verdicts used and not used, across 21 distinct rejection rules (among them `GRAPH-RECURSION-ORDER`, `GRAPH-RECURSION-SCC`, `GRAPH-CALL-CONTRACT`, and `GRAPH-RECURSION-MEMBER`). No verdict holds for the object the bootstrap rejects. Every group (92: 37 with one member, 28 with two, 27 with three), member function (174), and general or concurrency target (80) of the 92 valid stores is proven. Every built-in target the bootstrap accepts in those profiles is proven. On the corpus (jsonmin and the eleven helper and M11/M14 stores the S3c decoder can stream), no function, group, list, contract, or target is left to Python. The remaining Python object checks are the M11 bundle's build and package objects. The verifier now takes about 5 ms on jsonmin, including building the table. Full suite: 890 tests, 18 skipped. |
| Limits | Accelerator (profile 3), platform (4), and board (5) targets, and build and package objects, stay with the bootstrap. So does the per-graph glue around the XAX passes (reference resolution and trap payloads in `_parse_graph`). The op-typing store is not decided: its graph bodies exceed what the S3c decoder streams, so no object table is built, as before. |


## ADR-147 — Multi-language runtime rule: XAX within 1.05× of the fastest valid implementation

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Every CPU/native runtime benchmark used to judge performance compares XAX with an optimized C or C++ baseline **and** at least one implementation outside C/C++ (Rust here: `compiler/benchmarks/rust_twins/`, `rustc -C opt-level=3 -C panic=abort -C codegen-units=1`), all with the same observable contract, input, host, harness, warmup, and repetitions. The fastest valid implementation by median decides: XAX meets the primary target at ≤ 1.05× of it, is *competitive below target* at ≤ 1.10×, and is *unmet* above that, in which case XAX is profiled and optimized and the full comparison re-run; workloads are never weakened and faster competitors never dropped. The shared harness (`linux_harness.py`) builds the Rust twin, records `time_ratio_vs_fastest` and `performance_class` per XAX arm, and since this ADR interleaves repetitions: each round runs every arm once, rotating the order, so no arm owns a time slice of a drifting shared host. Performance-relevant compiler changes re-run all three Linux comparisons. Each implementation may choose its own data representation for the same contract (the Rust `chains` twin uses a struct of arrays with `u32` links; XAX gains the same layout as the `soa` arm). `XAX_BENCHMARKS.md` §15.0 is the normative text; `XAX_SPEC.md` §21.2 defines "competitive" for R4 through it. |
| Rationale | The work order (2026-10-03) replaced "competitive with gcc/clang" by a stricter, multi-language target. A C-only comparison can hide a gap to another established toolchain, and sequential arm timing on a shared host can move a result by several percent. |
| Evidence | MEASURED (`u1_linux_filestat_evidence.json`, `oi37_chains_evidence.json`, `jsonmin_evidence.json`, 2026-10-03, Intel Xeon @ 2.10 GHz (Emerald Rapids, model 207), 4 vCPUs, Linux 6.18.44; gcc 13.3.0, clang 18.1.3, rustc 1.97.0; 3 warmup rounds, 31 rounds). Before ADR-148 the first multi-language run gave XAX/fastest 1.566 (`filestat`, clang fastest), 1.183 (`chains`, record links; Rust fastest), and 1.208 (`jsonmin`, Rust fastest): all unmet. After: `filestat` 1.017 (clang 63.3 ms, XAX 64.4 ms, Rust 70.0 ms), `chains` 1.000 (XAX `soa` 201.9 ms is fastest; Rust 209.9 ms, gcc -O2 228.9 ms), `jsonmin` 1.029 (clang 24.1 ms, XAX 24.8 ms, gcc -O3 25.6 ms, Rust 27.0 ms): all meet the primary target. XAX also has the smallest peak RSS in all three and the smallest binary in `filestat` and `chains`. |
| Limits | One host, three workloads, x86-64 Linux only. The AArch64, RISC-V, and SPIR-V comparisons are emulated or software-device measurements (conformance §23.17) and do not judge performance; the Windows PE comparison runs under Wine; the JVM comparison is against `javac` on HotSpot. None of these has a non-C arm yet. On this host the run-to-run stdev is 4–15% of the median, so a single run's ratio near 1.05 is not decisive; the committed JSON is one complete interleaved run. |

## ADR-148 — x86-64 register path: range-proven checks, lowering view, and call-aware allocation (OI-38)

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Code-generation changes, all exact, that took the three Linux workloads to the ADR-147 target. **Range facts** (`xax_x86_64_regalloc`): every `bits<w>` value is bounded by its width; adds, ands, multiplies by constants, divisions, truncations, and zero extensions propagate upper bounds; block parameters get the maximum over incoming edges, refined by an unsigned compare whose branch selects the edge (taken `x < y` bounds x by max(y) − 1), with widening after three rounds and a narrowing pass after the fixed point. A checked access whose index bound fits the view needs no check; a no-loss `x*2^k` index folds into the SIB scale; `store(p,i, load(p,i)+c)` with a proven index is one `add [m], c`; a 4/8-byte load used only by its block's fused compare becomes the compare's memory operand; a truncation that cannot lose bits emits nothing; a 32-bit add that cannot carry is a 64-bit add (cheaper as an induction step on this core). **Selection:** an OR of `x == c` tests used as a value is one clamped bit test plus `setc`; as a branch, the span check jumps straight to the false edge and one `bt` decides; compares xor-zero their result first; group-1 and `imul` immediates use imm8 forms. **Allocation:** loop pass-through block parameters under register pressure share one frame slot per closed class (no in-loop copy); a copy of a pinned value shares its register; 64-bit constants without an imm32 form that are used in a loop are loaded once into a free pinnable register when the loop's estimated demand allows; values in rbx/rbp/r12–r15 survive calls and values live across a call prefer them; Linux functions save only rbx/rbp/r12–r15 (SysV callee-saved), Win64 also rsi/rdi; edge copies into parameters the target never reads are skipped; loop headers up to 16 pure/load nodes rotate; loop alignment padding is not executed by a fall-through from the same loop. **Lowering view** (`xax_inline.py`): for each function the backend compiles a view of its graph, not the canonical graph: leaf `call.direct` callees (no calls, ≤ 256 nodes) are inlined; single-predecessor blocks merge; edges skip blocks that only forward their parameters; `bits<32>` halves of a `zext(lo) | ror(zext(hi),32)` pack fold to `lo`/`hi`; a `w`-bit store of `trunc_w(x)` stores `x`; unused pure nodes are deleted; blocks are laid out innermost-loop first, then non-returning, then joins. |
| Rationale | Profiling (perf sampling, callgrind instruction counts, llvm-mca, and an isolated loop harness) located the gaps: per-iteration spills and bounds checks (`filestat`), a 10-byte `movabs` per byte and a 32-bit induction add (`filestat`), loop-carried layout (`chains`), and call overhead, cold-path layout, and register shuffles (`jsonmin`). Each change removes one measured cost without a runtime mechanism. |
| Evidence | MEASURED and EXECUTED. Results in ADR-147. `filestat` 1.566× → 1.017× fastest (code 3,808 → 4,048 bytes), `jsonmin` 1.208× → 1.029× (instructions on a 1 MiB document 27.7M → 23.8M; C 18.5M), `chains` record-link arm 1.63× → 1.19× after the layout fix, `soa` arm fastest. `tests/test_x86_64_optimizations.py`: the loop whose bound proves the store keeps no check and runs; the same loop one element longer still traps (SIGILL); the value bit test matches every byte 0–255; jsonmin built through the lowering view inlines its helpers and matches `reference_jsonmin` on valid, invalid, deep, and whitespace-control inputs. Full suite: 1,059 passed, 4 skipped (Windows-host and Android-device only). |
| Limits | The lowering view is not re-verified as a canonical graph; its rewrites are checked by differential execution, not by translation validation. Range facts are upper bounds only. Allocation is still per block with fixed parameter registers, so register shuffles and home-slot reloads remain (jsonmin executes 1.29× C's instructions). Floats and aggregates still fall back to the older paths (OI-38). |

## ADR-149 — Self-hosting step S6b.4: packages, build objects, every target profile, and the per-graph glue are decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The store verifier (ADR-144/146) now decides, with the bootstrap's rules, every object kind it did not. (a) **Packages** (`decode_package`): nonempty identity, nonempty modules in CID order, dependencies ordered by key (exact roots by CID before logical identities, which compare as encoded strings), named build entries, feature and configuration schemas (nonempty names in byte order, of the right kinds), capabilities ordered by (kind, scope), exact end, exact reference use. (b) **Leaf build forms**: profiles (mode, levels, grants ordered by (identity, kind, scope), nonempty identities), trust policies (canonical booleans, ordered nonempty algorithms and signers, both sets when anything is required), signatures (a resolved signed object, three nonempty strings), and optimization policies. (c) In later passes, each reading the verdicts and records of the passes before it: **requests** (a proven package declaring the entry, target, profile form, bindings equal to the package's schemas name for name with constants of the schema types, ordered artifacts), **snapshots** (header forms, CID-ordered packages and signatures, ordered 32-byte digests, a proven request whose package is listed, the dependency closure from it by exact root and unique logical identity equal to the listed packages, every grant naming listed packages that all declare it, signatures over listed packages, coverage when the policy requires it), and **provenance** (forms, three digests, a producer, a proven snapshot of the proven request with that target and profile). (d) **Targets**: platform (4) contracts, accelerator (3) targets of the accelerator architecture (topology, canonical scopes and memory spaces, power-of-two alignment, contracts with byte opcodes, scope subsets, known spaces, value constraints, runtime-dependency identities), and AArch64 board (5) targets, with the Android, Linux, bare-metal, and board profile rules. Accelerator fields on another architecture give 0. (e) **Per-graph glue** of every graph fragment the S3c decoder streams: type references naming proven types, entity references that resolve, every reference used, and canonical trap payloads. `_graph_syntax_from_stream` then skips `_verify_type`, the trap-payload decode, and the reference-use comparison for those graphs (`_XAX_GLUE_GRAPHS`, by CID). Two XAX helpers were added: byte-string and CID order. |
| Rationale | S6b.3 left build and package objects, three target profiles, and the glue around the XAX passes in Python. With S6b.4 every object-level and per-graph check in `verify_store` has an XAX decision path; the bootstrap runs only for declines and for the exact diagnostic of a rejection. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_selfhost_build.py`). 260 random packages and leaf build objects, half of them mutated (bytes changed, swapped, truncated, appended, inserted): the outcome and exact diagnostic agree with and without the verdicts, 159 of 159 valid packages and leaf objects are proven, and none of 141 rejected subjects is (16 rejection rules). 176 request-snapshot-provenance stores with deliberately broken entries, binding types, closures (missing, ambiguous, extra), grants, signature coverage, foreign signatures, and provenance closure, and mutated bodies: identical outcomes; every proven build object passes the bootstrap's own `verify_object`; every request, snapshot, and provenance object of the valid stores is proven. Every built-in profile 3/4/5 target (accelerator, Android, board) and 40 mutations of each: proven exactly when `decode_native_target` accepts. 240 graphs with random, sometimes non-canonical trap payloads, mutated graph bytes, and extra references: identical outcomes, and each graph glued by XAX passes the bootstrap's checks. The M11 bundle's package, profile, trust policy, request, and snapshot are proven. Full suite: 1,064 passed, 4 skipped. |
| Limits | Accelerator fields on a non-accelerator architecture, ULEBs longer than five bytes, and graphs whose types the typing program does not prove (for example the M14 compiler's META types) still take the bootstrap path. The store verifier's own 1.9 MB store exceeds its input window and is verified by the bootstrap. Graph parsing still builds the Python lowering structures; only its checks moved. |

## ADR-150 — Self-hosting step S6c: every committed store decided by XAX; B1–B4 for the store verifier on RV64

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Windows.** The S3c graph decoder's output view grows from 4 MiB to 16 MiB (a stream word per body byte for the largest 1 MiB bodies), and the typing-and-facts input view, which the store verifier and the RISC-V backend share, grows from 16 MiB to 64 MiB. Both views are zero-filled on demand, so small inputs cost nothing more. The op-typing (2.8 MB) and store-verifier (2.0 MB) stores now stream and fit as one verifier object table. (2) **Backend arena.** The XAX RISC-V backend allocates each function's label slots first and reclaims the rest of its arena (liveness bitsets, edge moves, per-value tables) after the function is lowered, so the arena must hold one function's scratch rather than the whole program's. Its output layout is rebalanced inside the same 32 Mi words: words 1–5 Mi, jumps 5–7 Mi, ranges 7–11 Mi, metadata 11 Mi, stream 12–16 Mi, arena 16 Mi to the header. Writes to the internal stream past its end are dropped, and the front end then declines, so an overflow cannot corrupt the arena. (3) **B1–B4 for the store verifier.** gen1 (the backend run natively) compiles the verifier program for the RISC-V views profile (ADR-145). The verifier's RISC-V image, run in the RV64 emulator, verifies stores. Its output is read by the same `collect_verdicts` the native path uses. gen2 (the backend's own RISC-V image) compiles the verifier store again. |
| Rationale | S6 asks for the full verifier and then B1–B4 for the real compiler on at least one target. After S6b.4, every object kind had an XAX decision path, but the two largest helper stores did not fit the decoder and verifier windows, so the verifier could not verify itself, and the backend could not compile the verifier's 800 KB graph. With S6c, the XAX components that make up the working compiler's verification and code generation (store and graph decoders, CFG analysis, operation typing and facts, store verifier, RISC-V backend) are each compiled by XAX on RV64. The verifier that decides the production verdicts also gives them as a RISC-V program, its own store included. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_selfhost_stores.py`, `tests/test_xax_selfhost_closure.py`, `benchmarks/bench_selfhost_closure.py`, `selfhost_closure_evidence.json`). All 12 committed stores: the store and every object are decided by XAX (op typing 46/46 functions and graphs, store verifier 16/16), and the bootstrap runs no object check for them. B1: gen1 gives the bootstrap generator's exact image for CFG analysis (9,584 bytes), graph decoder (36,516), encoder (3,124), store decoder (16,212), op typing (4,701,852), store verifier (4,559,444), and the backend (2,094,244); gen1 compiles the verifier in 1.8 s against 7.5 s for the bootstrap. B2/B3: the emulated verifier image gives the native verdicts (store verdict, per-object verdicts, proven functions' graphs, group members) on all 12 stores, its own included (921 objects, at most 3·10⁹ emulated instructions, 34 s against 0.2 s natively), and on stores with mutated object bodies, which it declines exactly where the native verifier does. B4: gen2 compiling the verifier store under emulation reproduces the 4,559,444-byte image byte for byte (sha256 `49ad0a2b…`, at most 6.8·10¹⁰ instructions, 722 s). The ADR-145 backend fixed point was re-run on the changed backend: store 728,919 bytes, gen2 = bootstrap reference = gen3 (2,094,244 bytes, at most 2.5·10¹⁰ instructions). |
| Limits | One target (RV64, views profile, under Unicorn). BLAKE3 is not compiled this way: it uses `aggregate.make`/`aggregate.get`, which neither RISC-V generator lowers in this profile. The verifier's verdicts still drive a Python driver that builds the lowering structures and gives every rejection's exact diagnostic, and the x86-64 backend that compiles the helpers natively is still Python. Each B4 run takes minutes, so its tests are opt-in (`XAX_FIXED_POINT=1`) and the results are recorded as evidence. |

## ADR-151 — Aggregates in the RISC-V views profile: BLAKE3 compiles through both RISC-V generators

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The views profile (`riscv64-baremetal-views-v1`) adds `aggregate.make` and `aggregate.get` for tuples and arrays of 1–255 `bits<N <= 64>` fields, lowered identically by the bootstrap generator (`xax_riscv64.py`) and the XAX backend program (`xax_selfhost_riscv64_backend.py`). (1) **No machine value for an aggregate.** `aggregate.make` emits nothing; `aggregate.get` of a made aggregate is that field (an alias, like a borrowed view's result, ADR-145). (2) **Indirect result.** A function whose only non-proof result is an aggregate takes the address of a caller-owned result area as a hidden first argument (a0; LP64's indirect return) and stores field k as a zero-extended doubleword at offset 8k. The caller gives each such call its own area at the top of its frame, and `aggregate.get` of a call result loads the field from it. (3) **Stack arguments.** Machine arguments past a7 go in 8-byte slots at the caller's `sp`; the caller reserves the outgoing area at the bottom of its frame, and the callee reads them above its own frame. With no such calls, every frame is unchanged, so every earlier image is byte-identical. (4) **Rejection.** An aggregate that reaches a block parameter, an edge, a call argument, or a return it was not made for is rejected (`RISCV64-AGGREGATE-VALUE`, `RISCV64-AGGREGATE-USE`); the XAX program declines and the bootstrap gives the diagnostic. The instruction-set-neutral part of the bootstrap generator (closure, widths, the aggregate plan, view aliases, linear scan) moves to `xax_views_lowering.py` so the x86-64 generator can share it. |
| Rationale | ADR-150 left BLAKE3 out of the RV64 closure because its compression returns a 16-word tuple and takes 28 arguments. Lowering it once by inlining or scalar replacement would have hidden a missing calling convention; the LP64 indirect-return and stack-argument rules are the platform's own and cover any later aggregate result. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_riscv64.py`, `tests/test_xax_selfhost_riscv64_backend.py`, `tests/test_xax_selfhost_blake3.py`, `tests/test_xax_selfhost_closure.py`, `benchmarks/selfhost_closure_evidence.json`, `benchmarks/selfhost_fixed_point_evidence.json`). Tuples of 3, 12, and 255 fields and a 16-field array, returned through hidden areas with stack arguments, run in the RV64 emulator with the expected results, and both generators give the same image. BLAKE3: both generators give one 18,844-byte image; under emulation it matches the official vectors and the reference digests across chunk and tree boundaries, and hashes each of the 10 committed stores under 1 MiB to its production digest. gen2 (the backend's own RISC-V image) compiles the BLAKE3 store to gen1's image in 3 s of emulation, a default test. The changed backend still reaches its fixed point (store 902,679 bytes; gen2 = bootstrap reference = gen3, 2,810,964 bytes, at most 4.1·10¹⁰ emulated instructions), and gen2 still reproduces the verifier image (4,559,444 bytes, at most 7.6·10¹⁰ instructions). |
| Limits | Aggregates are only made values and call results; aggregate parameters, edges, and nested aggregates are not lowered. An image entry cannot return an aggregate (its result widths are scalar). The B4 verifier and backend runs stay opt-in (`XAX_FIXED_POINT=1`). Still one target (RV64 under Unicorn). |

## ADR-152 — Self-hosting step S7a: the x86-64 backend as XAX; B1–B4 natively on x86-64; production helpers compiled by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Profile.** `x86_64_views_target()` (identity `x86_64-linux-views-v1`, the existing Linux x86-64 machine tuple) carries the views operations (`views_operations()`: the RV64IM integer subset, checked view accesses, aggregates). It keeps the repository's x86-64 convention (Win64 registers): arguments in rcx, rdx, r8, r9, then stack slots above the caller's 32-byte shadow area; one result in rax; an aggregate result through a caller-owned area whose address is the hidden first argument and is returned in rax; rbx, rbp, rsi, rdi, r12–r15 hold values (the same linear scan as RISC-V); r10/r11 are scratch; a trap is `ud2`; code is position-independent with rel32 transfers and functions on 16-byte boundaries. (2) **Bootstrap reference.** `xax_x86_64_views.py` is a new, deliberately simple generator for that profile, sharing `xax_views_lowering.py` with the RISC-V one. (3) **XAX program.** `xax_selfhost_views_backend.py` now holds the instruction-set-neutral half of the XAX backends (front end, liveness, linear scan, edge copies, driver, native runner) with explicit hooks; the RISC-V program is that half plus its hooks and lowering, and its store is byte-identical to before the split. `xax_selfhost_x86_64_backend.py` is the same half plus x86-64 hooks and lowering, emitting one code byte per output word. (4) **Production.** Every native XAX helper (BLAKE3 hash, store and graph decoders, CFG analysis, operation typing and facts, store verifier, RISC-V encoder, both backends) is now lowered by the XAX x86-64 backend (`host_image`). Where it cannot run, or while its own image is being made, the bootstrap reference gives the same bytes, so an image never depends on which one ran. `xax_x86_64.compile_native` (the optimizing bootstrap backend) no longer lowers any XAX helper; it remains the backend for programs (Linux, PE, Android) and for the S0 compressor. |
| Rationale | ADR-150 left two gaps: the x86-64 backend that compiles the helpers was Python, and B1–B4 held on one target, RV64 under emulation. Reproducing the optimizing x86-64 backend byte for byte in XAX would have meant porting its inliner, range analysis, and allocator before anything else could move. A simple x86-64 profile whose bootstrap reference is small enough to mirror exactly gets the helpers onto an XAX-compiled path now, gives a second instruction set, and runs the closure natively instead of under an emulator. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_x86_64_views.py`, `tests/test_xax_selfhost_x86_64_backend.py`, `benchmarks/bench_selfhost_x86_64.py`, `benchmarks/selfhost_x86_64_evidence.json`). The bootstrap reference matches the reference executor on 320 random integer calls (traps included) and executes aggregates of 3–255 fields with stack arguments; `llvm-mc` decodes its code. B1: gen1 gives the bootstrap's image for all nine helper programs: CFG 9,642 bytes, graph decoder 47,417, encoder 3,894, store decoder 19,644, typing 3,396,677, store verifier 2,993,128, BLAKE3 20,661, RISC-V backend 1,764,222, and the x86-64 backend itself 1,835,317. B2/B3: the verifier image production now runs agrees with the bootstrap verifier on all 13 committed stores; gen2 compiles random programs as gen1 does. B4: gen2, run natively, compiles the backend store to itself (sha256 `95d6184c…`) in 0.95 s — a default test. Cost, MEASURED on the shared evidence host: the verifier image runs at 1.08–1.32× the time of the optimizing backend's image of the same store on the same inputs, and is 4.5× larger (2,993,128 against 659,575 bytes); warm-cache self-hosting suites take 1.14× as long, and cold ones 0.67× (the XAX backend compiles the helpers faster than the bootstrap does). |
| Limits | The profile is the views subset only: no floats, raw memory, atomics, foreign calls, or system calls, so it does not replace `xax_x86_64` for programs. Code quality is a first cut (no fall-through layout, caller-saved registers, or inlining). The S0 compressor still comes from `compile_native`. The Python driver still builds the object table, and declines still fall back to the bootstrap for the exact diagnostic. |

## ADR-153 — Android counter: descriptor ownership as a linear resource, durable writes, and a Java + NDK twin

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Descriptor ownership.** `xax_platform.posix_descriptor_api()` adds `descriptor`, a linear resource type (kind `0x102`, no release), the owning bionic `close(fd, descriptor, fs)` that consumes it, and `fdatasync(fd, fs)`. It is a separate package, so `posix_android_api()` and every artifact built from it are unchanged. No kernel concept is added: the token is an ordinary resource value, erased by the ABI. (2) **Counter callbacks** (`xax_android_counter.counter_native`) take the token with the descriptor that `detachFd` hands over and close through the owning `close`; a click calls `fdatasync` after `pwrite64` and before `close`. The DEX side is unchanged because the token is not a machine value. (3) **Twin.** `benchmarks/android_counter_twin/` is a conventional Java + NDK app with the same package, classes, native methods, state file, and behavior, built with Google's tools by the generalized `bench_android_ndk_twin.build_twin`. `bench_android_counter_twin.py` measures sizes now; with `--device` it cold-starts both apps (`am start -W` `TotalTime`, total PSS from `dumpsys meminfo`) in alternating install-once passes and checks each click path, and labels the result MEASURED only when the target is hardware. (4) **Device oracle.** `validate_counter_apk.sh` polls the UI for up to `XAX_UI_TIMEOUT` seconds instead of reading it once after one second. `android_counter_app.py --device` runs it on the `adb` target and records the target's identity and whether it is hardware; only an EXECUTED run of the committed APK is carried into later evidence. |
| Rationale | ADR-111 left the descriptor's ownership "by construction": nothing stopped a callback from leaking it or closing it twice, and a click's write could still be in the page cache when the process died. Both are now visible to verification or explicit in the graph, without a runtime. The Android row cannot reach R4 without an established baseline that has the same behavior; the minimal-Activity twin (ADR-105) has no state or I/O. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED/MEASURED (`tests/test_xax_android_counter.py`, `benchmarks/android_counter_evidence.json`, `benchmarks/android_counter_twin_evidence.json`). The verifier accepts one owning `close`, rejects a leaked descriptor (`XAX.RESOURCE.DROP`) and a second `close` (`XAX.RESOURCE.DUPLICATE`). The rebuilt APK is 20,649 bytes (native library 2,584, +88 for `fdatasync`); Google's tools pass, ART verifies both classes, and under Android's `linker64` and bionic the library still gives restore → 0, clicks → 1, 2, 3, restart → 3, click → 4, an 8-byte file holding 4, and every descriptor closed. Sizes against the twin (NDK r28c `clang -O2`, build-tools 36.1, `javac --release 11` + `d8`): APK 20,649 vs 28,892 bytes (0.715×), native library 2,584 vs 4,608 (0.561×, packed container and no `libdl.so`), DEX 2,936 vs 2,472 (1.188×, one DEX per class), manifest 0.652×. The two XAX native functions are 224 and 324 bytes against clang's 96 and 132 (the AArch64 frame path keeps every value in memory, ADR-110); ADR-154 later moved them to the register path (current sizes there). |
| Limits | The token is paired with the descriptor by the declarations, not proven: any `b32` can be closed with it. `fdatasync` makes the value durable, but one 8-byte `pwrite64` is not atomic on every filesystem (no write-then-rename). Start-up time and memory need an arm64 hardware device; until then the Android row has no performance or memory evidence. |

## ADR-154 — AArch64 register path for heap views and full-width heap loads and stores

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | On general AArch64 targets (those that `_general_aarch64_target` selects, ADR-110), the register-resident path also lowers `HEAP_VIEW`, `LOAD_BITS_LE`, and `STORE_BITS_LE`. A heap view is a `cbnz` over the allocator's pointer and a `brk` with the existing null-heap code, then the same register (or a copy while the raw pointer is still live): the frame path's trap, without the frame. A load or store through a pointer that is not an explicit stack object addresses `[base]` directly with the pointer's register. A function stays on the frame path unless every access is full width (the stored or loaded value's width equals the access size), so narrow and partial accesses are unchanged. Stack objects keep their SP-relative lowering. |
| Rationale | ADR-153's twin showed the counter's two native callbacks at 2.3× and 2.5× clang's size: a function with any heap access fell to the frame path, which keeps every value in memory. The register path already spills live values around calls, so heap accesses needed only an addressing form and the null trap. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_aarch64_heap_register_path.py`). A seeded corpus of 40 programs (64- and 32-bit) runs one to three allocate/use/free phases with bionic `malloc` and `free`, heap views, random full-width loads and stores, arithmetic, and `getpid` calls with the pointer and values live across them; all take the register path. Compiled into one packed Android library and run under Android's `linker64` and bionic, all 160 results equal a mirror computed while the programs are built. Mutations that halve the store width or invert the null check fail it. The ADR-110 corpus (288 results) is unchanged and passes. Of the committed artifacts only the counter changes: its callbacks shrink from 224 and 324 bytes to 128 and 168 (clang `-O2` twin: 96 and 132), the packed library from 2,584 to 2,328 bytes, and the APK from 20,649 to 20,393 bytes (0.706× the twin); Google's tools, ART's verifier, and the bionic run of the restore/click/restart sequence pass. |
| Limits | No address offsets, checked accesses, or narrow accesses on the register path; values are still spilled and reloaded around every call (no callee-saved registers). Correctness is validated under emulation only; no device timing. |

## ADR-155 — Android R3 from an x86_64 emulator with ARM translation

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) An Android Emulator run of an x86_64 `google_apis` system image whose ARM translation (`libndk_translation`) executes the APK's arm64 library counts as EXECUTED correctness evidence for the Android row, under the same rule as qemu runs (`XAX_SPEC.md` §21.2): never performance or memory evidence, and recorded with the target's identity and `hardware: false`. (2) On that basis the row's `practical_application` is EXECUTED and the row derives R3. The blocker list keeps the arm64-hardware run as outstanding. (3) The device oracle gains an opt-in tap retry (`XAX_TAP_RETRY`) and reads the state file directly on a rooted target, because `run-as` on a non-debuggable package prints its refusal on stdout, which the oracle previously compared as the stored value. |
| Rationale | The APK, its DEX, its manifest, its packed native library, and the framework calls (Activity lifecycle, `ParcelFileDescriptor`, view updates, input, process death and restart) are exactly what a device runs; only the arm64 instructions are translated instead of executed natively. That is stronger than the bionic-under-qemu evidence the row already had, which ran no framework at all, and it is the same correctness-only standing as the linux-aarch64 row's qemu runs. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`benchmarks/android_counter_evidence.json` `device`, `integration/android/validate_counter_apk.sh`). Target `google/sdk_gphone64_x86_64/emulator64_x86_64_arm64:12/SE1B.240122.005`, API 32, `ro.dalvik.vm.native.bridge=libndk_translation.so`, emulator 37.2.12 under software emulation (no KVM), with `ro.hw_timeout_multiplier=20`, a JDWP connection held on `system_server`, and error dialogs hidden (`compiler/integration/android/README.md`). The committed APK (SHA-256 `3f228494…`) passed every check: fresh install shows 0; taps show 1, 2, 3; after `force-stop` and relaunch it restores 3; a tap shows 4; the private state file holds 4. No tap retry was needed. |
| Limits | Not arm64 hardware (OI-44): the native library was translated, and the framework ran under a timeout multiplier. Android 11 (API 30) images could not launch the app on a host without KVM (fixed 10-second attach limit), and the API 33 image has no ARM translation. The libxposed modules and the platform-contract library have not run on the emulator. |

## ADR-156 — JVM linear memory: `jvm-classfile-memory-v1`, a generated memory package, and `jsonmin` on HotSpot (JVM R3)

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Profile.** `jvm_classfile_memory_target()` (identity `jvm-classfile-memory-v1`) is `jvm-classfile-v1` plus heap views, plain and checked loads and stores, address offsets, pointer casts, addresses, and rebases. v1 and every v1 artifact are unchanged. (2) **Representation.** Memory is one private static `byte[] M` of the generated class; a memory pointer lowers to an `int` offset into it, as wasm32 pointers are offsets into linear memory. Offsets below 16 are never storage. One-byte accesses are `baload`/`bastore`; wider ones call generated little-endian helpers (`xax$ld2/4/8`, `xax$st2/4/8`); floats go through `Float/Double` raw-bit conversions. (3) **Package.** Heap blocks and standard streams come from declarations of the reserved class `xax/jvm/Memory` under the existing `jvm-invokestatic` ABI (`jvm_memory_api()`), shaped like `xax_linux.LinuxApi` (`mmap_anonymous`, `munmap_view`, `read`, `write`, plus `System.exit`).
