The backend generates each member as a static method of the program's own class (UR-001): a bump allocator that grows `M` with `Arrays.copyOf` and returns zero-filled 16-aligned blocks (0 above 2^31 − 1 bytes), a no-op free, and `read`/`write` windows over `System.in`/`out`/`err`. No new foreign ABI, so the XAX-hosted verifier and its committed stores are untouched. (4) **Views given back.** A function that returns its borrowed views keeps a single machine result: the returned pointers are elided as on native targets (ADR-101), and the caller copies the lent pointer. (5) **Entry stack.** On this profile the generated `main` runs the entry on a thread with a 256 MiB stack (the class implements `Runnable`) and exits with status 1 if the entry ended by a trap. The launcher's default 1 MiB stack overflowed at 513 nested JSON levels, which the program legitimately recurses through. |
| Rationale | The JVM row stopped at R2 because the scalar subset could not hold a practical application: no XAX program with memory could run there. A byte array with integer offsets keeps every XAX memory rule (provenance, extents, checked accesses, rebases) exactly as the verifier states them, needs no object model, and reuses the program text: `jsonmin`'s parser and entry run unchanged, only the platform package differs. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED/MEASURED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_memory.py`, `tests/test_jsonmin.py::JsonminJvmTests`, `benchmarks/jvm_jsonmin_evidence.json`). 24 seeded programs over every access width (integers and exact floats, plain and checked, at random aligned offsets) match a byte-level mirror on HotSpot; a mutated load helper fails them. Out-of-view checked accesses, rebases outside the view, and a 2^31-byte request (a null block whose view traps) all trap. The v1 profile rejects memory and an unknown `xax/jvm/Memory` member rejects. `jsonmin` built with `build_jsonmin("jvm")` matches its reference contract on the 30 fixed cases (513-deep nesting included), 54 generated and mutated documents, and both capacity limits. On an 8 MiB document (fresh JVM per run, 7 interleaved runs, OpenJDK 21) it takes 0.228 s against 0.194 s for a line-for-line `javac` twin (1.178×), with the same peak RSS (1.00×) and a class file 8.9× larger (33,330-byte class, 33,692-byte JAR). The full suite still passes (1,106). |
| Limits | Freed blocks are never reused, and memory is capped at 2^31 − 1 bytes. No stack allocations, aggregates, sums, or indirect calls on the JVM yet. Every local is typed method-wide and multi-byte accesses are helper calls, hence the class size. There is no competitive verdict, so the JVM row is R3, not R4. The 256 MiB entry stack is a fixed profile property, not a program-declared bound. |

## ADR-157 — JVM code quality and the JVM runtime rule (JVM R4)

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Locals.** Liveness over the CFG, then greedy slot coloring per JVM type. Hints coalesce block parameters with their edge arguments, and copies with their operands; copies are pointer casts, same-representation zero extensions, heap-view pointers, and truncations of values whose known width already fits. Edge copies are parallel moves, with one temporary for a cycle. Entry parameters may reuse their slots. (2) **Frames.** Every frame lists the same locals. A slot that holds no value across a branch target (block live-ins and parameters, edge arguments, values live at a lowering's internal label) is Top and never initialized. (3) **Values.** Integer constants have no slot and are rematerialized at each use. Pure integer nodes over constants fold. A value read once and next stays on the operand stack (two-pass store elision; edge arguments and copy operands excluded). A value read again right after it is computed is `dup`ed before its store. (4) **Branches and compares.** Int compares fuse into `if_icmp*`, against zero into `if*`; unsigned 32-bit order flips the sign bit, folded on constants. Long equality is a plain `lcmp`. Materialized equality is branch-free. Empty forwarding blocks are threaded away. (5) **Intrinsics.** Rotates use `Integer/Long.rotateRight`. A rotation of a zero-extended value no wider than the amount is a left shift, and a 32-bit source then needs only `i2l`. A rotation read only through a narrow truncation is a right shift. `Integer.toUnsignedLong` widens u32. (6) **Memory.** Checked accesses with a 32-bit index call one generated member `xax$ix(i, max)` that traps or returns the index. One-byte accesses call generated `xax$ld1`, `xax$cld1`, and `xax$cst1` members, as a Java programmer's `at()` helpers; HotSpot inlines all of them. `M` starts at the sum of the program's constant-size allocation sites, so the common case never copies. (7) **Debug lines.** The `LineNumberTable` has one entry per node that can raise or call, de-duplicated; every pc still maps to its node through the artifact's semantic ranges. (8) **Runtime rule.** A JVM comparison follows §15.0 with the platform's own compilers, `javac` and `kotlinc`, as the baselines (`XAX_BENCHMARKS.md` §15.0a); `xax_replacement._runtime_rule_errors` checks for both. |
| Rationale | The JVM row stopped at R3 for code quality, not semantics. Every local was typed method-wide, constants and copies took slots, and each access paid inline checks. The fixes are the ones javac's own output shows, plus what an SSA graph allows: liveness, coalescing, folding, and branch fusion. Kotlin is the JVM's other established compiler, so it is the second baseline the multi-language rule asks for, in place of a non-C/C++ native toolchain. Its runtime-bundling JAR is how `kotlinc` deploys a program. |
| Evidence | MEASURED (`benchmarks/jvm_jsonmin_evidence.json`, `benchmarks/jvm_twin_evidence.json`, `XAX_BENCHMARKS.md` §15.17); EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm.py`, including a rotate-shift differential test; `tests/test_xax_jvm_memory.py`; `tests/test_jsonmin.py::JsonminJvmTests`; `tests/test_replacement_matrix.py`). `jsonmin` on 8 MiB, 15 interleaved rounds after 2 warmup rounds: XAX 0.1336 s, `javac` twin 0.1367 s (1.023×), `kotlinc` twin 0.1461 s (1.094×). XAX meets the primary target and was the fastest arm in all five full runs. Peak RSS is 0.89× javac's and 0.88× kotlinc's. The class went from 33,330 to 8,539 bytes. Collatz: kernel 0.93× `javac`, class 753 vs 760 bytes. A full-suite run caught one miscompile before commit (a subtraction whose stacked second operand was read first); operands are now reordered only for commutative operations, and `test_subtraction_of_a_value_left_on_the_stack` guards it. JVM row R4. |
| Limits | Code size: the `jsonmin` class is 2.29× javac's and 1.51× kotlinc's, and the JAR is 3.4× javac's (blockers in the matrix row). Measurements come from one shared host with one application and one kernel. The jsonmin wall time includes JVM start-up, which is common to all arms; the Collatz kernel is timed after warmup. JVM start-up and HotSpot defaults are not tuned for any arm. OI-35's closing criterion (a native-plus-bridge arm) is still unmet. |

## ADR-158 — JVM code size: expression trees, one name per value, and frames only at targets

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The JVM backend (`xax_jvm.py`) lowers more like javac. (1) **Expression trees.** A pure integer node read exactly once, by a later node or the terminator of its own block, gets no local: it is lowered where it is read, and liveness charges its operands to that point. (2) **Short-circuit branches.** A conditional branch on an OR/AND tree of compares jumps per compare, as `\|\|`/`&&` do; unsigned range checks fold the offset and the sign flip into one `iadd`. (3) **One name per value.** The parameters of a block entered by one edge alias that edge's arguments. A load from the same place as a load whose memory it consumes is that load's value, emitted once with one line entry. Copies and views a call gives back may share their operand's local even while both are live, and reads of a coalesced copy go to its operand. `pack(low(s), high(s))` is `s`. (4) **Dead code.** Block parameters that nothing reads take no locals and their edges copy nothing. Pure nodes nobody reads are not lowered, and unused call results are popped. (5) **Frames.** A stack-map frame exists only at a branch target or after an instruction that never falls through. Definite assignment over the CFG decides which typed locals need a zero store. The trap label has its own frame with no locals, so trap jumps impose nothing. (6) **Layout.** Blocks are emitted in fallthrough chains, and a return is preferred as a jump target. Byte-identical return blocks reached only by jumps merge. A local updated as `x = x + c` keeps its local (two-address coalescing) and is one `iinc`. (7) **Members and constants.** Packed 32-bit halves go through `xax$pk`. Checked byte accesses call members specialized by view bound, with the check inline. A checked access at a constant index inside its view cannot trap and has no line entry. A run of constant ASCII byte stores is one `String.getBytes(0, n, M, p + i)`. The memory package's `free` is the constant 0. The counting pass lowers into a scratch constant pool. The threaded launcher ends with `System.exit(0)` from `run` and `exit(1)` after `join`, without a status field. (8) **Locals.** Internal methods (not the entry) take one-slot parameters first. An entry parameter no branch target sees frees its slots for values of other types; if that would need a zero store before the parameter is read, the method is lowered again with the parameter kept. |
| Rationale | The user asked to reduce `jsonmin`'s class toward 1.05× the smallest twin class. The program stays the same graph as on Linux, so every change is in the lowering. Each one is either javac's own output shape (stack expressions, `\|\|`, `iinc`, frames only where needed) or a standard compiler step (copy propagation through block parameters, redundant-load elimination, dead-code elimination, tail merging). |
| Evidence | MEASURED (`benchmarks/jvm_jsonmin_evidence.json`, `benchmarks/jvm_twin_evidence.json`, `XAX_BENCHMARKS.md` §15.18); EXECUTED: `tests/test_xax_jvm.py` (new: short-circuit branches; subtraction with a stacked operand); `tests/test_xax_jvm_memory.py` (new: string runs, repeated loads, statically safe and trapping indices; a trap in the threaded entry exits with status 1); `tests/test_jsonmin.py::JsonminJvmTests`; the self-hosted encoder lowered to the JVM. **`jsonmin` class: 8,539 → 5,575 bytes**: 0.987× the `kotlinc` twin's classes (5,649) and 1.495× the `javac` twin's (3,730). XAX remains the fastest arm (javac 1.197×, kotlinc 1.251×) with the lowest peak RSS. Collatz: class 724 vs 760 bytes, kernel 0.91× `javac`. Full suite: 1,111 passed, 5 skipped. |
| Limits | **The 1.05×-of-javac target is not met.** What remains is the program's shape: the XAX parser is a few large functions with inlined helpers; every call returns a packed (pos, out) state that is tested and unpacked; and every byte access names its view and is checked. The javac twin instead calls `at()` and `copy()` on two global arrays. Each raising site keeps one line entry, so a trap still names its node (104 entries; javac's class has 98 for its statements). Changing the program's representation (state in fields, views as globals) would change the XAX program on every target, not the JVM lowering. |

## ADR-159 — JVM general profile: aggregates, sums, stack allocations, and indirect calls

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Profile.** `jvm_classfile_general_target()` (identity `jvm-classfile-general-v1`) is `jvm-classfile-memory-v1` plus `aggregate.make/get`, `sum.make/tag/get`, `stack.alloc/end`, `function.address`, and `call.indirect`. The v1 and memory profiles and their artifacts are unchanged. (2) **Aggregates and sums.** These are immutable flattened `long[]` values (`[J` locals, parameters, results, and block parameters). A scalar takes one element holding its raw bits; a nested aggregate is copied in (`System.arraycopy`) and copied out (`Arrays.copyOfRange`). A sum is its tag followed by the largest variant's slots, and `sum.get` of another variant traps. JVM object references cannot be stored in one (`JVM-AGGREGATE-SCALAR`). (3) **Stack allocations.** A shadow stack lives in the linear memory, `[16, 16 + 1 MiB)`, below the heap. A method with allocations takes a 16-aligned frame for all of them from the static field `S` on entry, trapping when the region is exhausted. Every allocation has a fixed offset in that frame and is zero-filled (`Arrays.fill`) each time it executes. Every return restores `S`, so frames follow activations: recursion is exact. (4) **Indirect calls.** `function.address` is the function's 1-based index in the table of address-taken functions. Each `call.indirect` contract gets a generated `xax$i<k>(index, args...)` dispatcher that invokes the addressed function with exactly the contract's types, and traps otherwise. |
| Rationale | These operations were the JVM's remaining gaps against the native general profiles. Objects are the platform's own representation for immutable composite values, and HotSpot's escape analysis can scalar-replace short-lived ones. A real per-activation stack makes recursion through stack storage and aggregates exact; the wasm lowering's static per-node storage rejects that. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_general.py`): every program runs on HotSpot and matches the reference executor. The programs cover nested tuples and arrays with float fields through calls and block parameters; sums made on three paths and dispatched on their tag, plus the wrong-variant trap; and function pointers chosen at run time, passed to another function, and called twice. Recursion keeps a value in each activation's own stack slot across the recursive call and returns an aggregate: depth 100 against the reference, depth 3,000 against the closed form. A process entry recursing to half the shadow stack exits 0, and twice it traps with status 1. The memory profile rejects these operations. `jsonmin` is unchanged (5,575-byte class). Full suite: 1,118 passed, 5 skipped. |
| Limits | No atomics, links (pointer values stored in memory), or raw loads on the JVM yet. The shadow stack is a fixed 1 MiB, and a stack allocation inside a loop reuses one frame offset per node, as on wasm. Aggregates allocate (C2 may scalar-replace them); there is no performance measurement for these operations yet. |

## ADR-160 — JVM general profile: links, raw loads, and atomics

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | `jvm-classfile-general-v1` also admits `link.make/follow/target`, `raw.load.bits.le`, and `atomic.load/store/rmw/cmpxchg/fence`. The profile's identity is unchanged and its operation list grows; it was introduced by ADR-159 and has no published artifact. (1) **Links.** A link is its record's `int` offset in the linear memory (0 is null, never storage). A link field is 8 bytes holding the offset zero-extended, the size every target uses. `link.follow` of null traps. (2) **Raw loads** are ordinary loads: the waived alignment and initialization checks need nothing over a zero-filled byte array. (3) **Atomics.** Exactly one XAX thread runs on a JVM profile: the entry thread, since the profiles create no threads and have no JVM-to-XAX callbacks. So each atomic operation is its sequential effect on the linear memory. Read-modify-write loads the old value and stores the exchanged or added one; compare-exchange stores only on a match and reports it. Orderings, scopes, and fences have nothing to order. A profile that admits a second XAX thread (callbacks from Java threads) must lower atomics to JVM atomics (`VarHandle` byte-array views) instead. |
| Rationale | These were the last operations the native general profiles have and the JVM lacked. Each lowering is exact under the profile's one-thread fact, which the spec now states as the condition for it. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_general.py`). Every atomic form at 32 and 64 bits matches the reference executor on 25 argument pairs each: store, add and exchange RMW, a succeeding and a failing compare-exchange, and load. Three records linked in stack storage are walked with `link.follow` and sum their keys; following a loaded null link traps. A raw load with the initialization check waived reads the zero-filled stack cell. Full suite: 1,121 passed, 5 skipped; `jsonmin` unchanged. |
| Limits | No JVM-to-XAX callbacks, so no second XAX thread exists yet; when callbacks come, atomics must change with them. Links name records in the linear memory only, not JVM objects. |

## ADR-161 — JVM callbacks: XAX functions as functional-interface objects

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Entry type.** `jvm_interface_entry_type(interface, "name(descriptor)")` is `ptr<opaque_identity<"code-entry:jvm-interface:" + interface + "." + name + descriptor>>`. It is the ADR-102 code-entry family with a parametrized JVM convention, so the convention stays part of the type. The verifier admits it for pure functions only (no proof parameters or results), the same rule as C entries. (2) **Lowering.** `function.address` at such a type is `new P(k)`: an instance of the program's own class, with `k` the function's table index (ADR-159). The class implements every interface used. Each interface method reads `k` and calls the matching function, and traps otherwise. One class serves all callbacks, so the JAR stays one class file. The backend checks that the function's machine signature equals the descriptor (a `Z` result is a `bits<1>` result; `JVM-ENTRY-SIGNATURE`). It also rejects a callback that can reach a stack allocation, because the shadow stack belongs to the entry thread (`JVM-ENTRY-SHADOW-STACK`), and a `Runnable.run()V` entry in a threaded process, whose launcher already implements it. (3) **Calling interfaces.** Two foreign ABIs are added: `jvm-invokeinterface` (receiver first; `invokeinterface` of an InterfaceMethodref) and `jvm-invokestatic-interface` (static methods declared on interfaces, such as `IntStream.rangeClosed`). (4) **Threads.** Java may call a callback on any thread. Callbacks are pure, so they never touch the linear memory, and ADR-160's sequential atomics stay exact. The spec now states that condition as "one thread with memory access". |
| Rationale | JDK APIs take behaviour as functional-interface objects (streams, comparators, executors). This is the JVM form of the C callbacks ADR-102 introduced, and it reuses that rule. Implementing the interfaces on the program's own class avoids generating and loading one adapter class per callback. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_callbacks.py`). The JDK drives `IntStream.rangeClosed(1, n).map(square).filter(isOdd).reduce(7, combine)` over three XAX functions (`IntUnaryOperator`, `IntPredicate` with a `bits<1>` result, `IntBinaryOperator`), matching a Python model for n up to 1,000. Three rejections are covered: an effectful callback (verifier), a mismatched interface descriptor, and a callback that reaches a stack allocation. The self-hosted typing store was regenerated for the extended ABI list. Full suite: 1,125 passed, 5 skipped. |
| Limits | Callbacks are pure, and they take and return only scalars; object parameters (for example `Comparator.compare(Object, Object)`) would need object construction or references in callbacks. There is no object, array, or string construction yet. A callback cannot use stack allocations. |

## ADR-162 — JVM object construction, arrays, string constants, and casts

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Seven typed foreign ABIs, lowered to the instruction they name, on every JVM profile: `jvm-new` (`new`, `dup`, `invokespecial <init>`; the result is the class), `jvm-newarray` (`newarray`/`anewarray`; library: element descriptor), `jvm-arrayload`/`jvm-arraystore`/`jvm-arraylength` (`<t>aload`/`<t>astore`/`arraylength`; library: array descriptor), `jvm-ldc` (a printable-ASCII `String` constant; name: the literal), and `jvm-checkcast` (library: the target class or array descriptor). Each declaration is checked against its descriptor like any JVM member. Narrow (`byte`, `short`) array loads are masked back to their XAX width. Java's own exceptions (bounds, casts, negative sizes) terminate the program like a trap, the existing rule for foreign members. Whether a declaration carries effect tokens is the program's choice, as for every foreign function. |
| Rationale | Objects could previously only arrive as foreign results, so XAX could not build a `StringBuilder`, an array for `Arrays.sort`, or the boxed values a `Comparator` sorts. Construction as typed declarations adds no operation or type form: the existing foreign-call machinery and descriptor checks cover it. With `jvm-checkcast`, ADR-161's callbacks can take objects. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_objects.py`), each against a Python model. An `int[]` is filled, sorted by `Arrays.sort`, and read back. A `StringBuilder` builds `"value=" + n`, checked through `length()` and `hashCode()`. An `Integer[]` is sorted descending by an XAX `Comparator` callback that casts and unboxes its `Object` arguments. An out-of-bounds load terminates with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException`. The self-hosted typing store was regenerated. Full suite: 1,129 passed, 5 skipped. |
| Limits | No field access on objects (instance fields, or static fields other than `jvm-getstatic`), no `instanceof` (both superseded by ADR-163), and no multi-dimensional `multianewarray`. Strings are printable ASCII literals. |

## ADR-163 — JVM instance fields and `instanceof`

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Three more typed foreign ABIs on every JVM profile: `jvm-getfield` (`getfield`; input: the object; result: the field's type), `jvm-putfield` (`putfield`; inputs: the object and the value; no result), and `jvm-instanceof` (`instanceof`; input: one reference; result: `bits 1`). Field declarations name the class as library and `field:descriptor` as name, as `jvm-getstatic` does. Narrow (`byte`, `short`) field reads are masked to their XAX width. A null object terminates the program like a trap (the `NullPointerException` rule for foreign members). |
| Rationale | ADR-162 left objects opaque except through methods; JDK value classes (`java.awt.Point`, `GridBagConstraints`, and similar) expose public instance fields, and a callback that takes `Object` needs a type test before a cast. Both reuse the existing descriptor checks. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_objects.py::test_instance_fields_and_instanceof`) against a Python model. A `java.awt.Point` is constructed, its `x` read, its `y` written, `translate` called, then it is cast to `Object` and tested: `instanceof java/awt/geom/Point2D` is true and `instanceof java/lang/String` is false. The self-hosted typing store was regenerated. Full suite: 1,130 passed, 5 skipped. |
| Limits | `putstatic` is not added: no `java.base` class has a public mutable static field to execute it against, and an untested lowering is not admitted (superseded by ADR-167). No `multianewarray` (superseded by ADR-164). |

## ADR-164 — JVM multi-dimensional arrays

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | One more typed foreign ABI on every JVM profile: `jvm-multianewarray`, lowered to `multianewarray`. Its library is the array descriptor, of rank 2 or more; its inputs are 1 to rank 32-bit lengths, outermost first (unlisted inner dimensions stay `null`, as in Java); its result is the array. Anything else rejects (`JVM-MULTIANEWARRAY`). |
| Rationale | ADR-162's `jvm-newarray` makes one dimension, so a Java `int[][]` needed a loop of `anewarray` and stores. Java APIs that take matrices (`Arrays.deepHashCode`, `deepToString`, image rasters) can now get one in one instruction. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_objects.py`) against a Python model. `new int[a][b]` is filled through a row and checked by lengths, the stored element, and `Arrays.deepHashCode`; `new long[b][a][]` has two of its three dimensions created. Rank-1 descriptors and too many lengths reject. The self-hosted typing store was regenerated. |
| Limits | Lengths are not checked by XAX: a negative length ends the program by Java's `NegativeArraySizeException`, the existing rule for Java exceptions. |

## ADR-165 — Native code plus a generated JVM bridge; OI-35 answered for the JVM

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Bridge arm.** `xax_jvm_bridge.compile_jni_bridge` compiles an entry for `x86_64-linux-elf-exec-v1` (syscalls only, position-independent code) and packages it as an x86-64 `ET_DYN` JNI library. The library has one export, `Java_<class>_<method>`, and no relocations, `DT_NEEDED`, or executable stack. The export is the existing SysV entry adapter (ADR-102). The managed side is a generated bridge class: a `public static native` method, a `<clinit>` that calls `System.loadLibrary`, and, for a process entry, a `main` that passes the returned status to `System.exit`. Both adapters are generated from the entry's signature (UR-001). The entry takes `JNIEnv*` and the `jclass` as two 64-bit parameters, then up to two integers, and returns at most one integer (`JNI-ENTRY-SIGNATURE`). No JNI function is called and no object crosses. `jsonmin` gains a `jni` build, which returns its status instead of calling `exit_group`. (2) **OI-35 answer.** Choose per workload, with direct emission as the default. Native code plus a bridge suits a host-specific compute kernel that exchanges only scalars and the process's own streams. Direct emission is portable to every JVM, ships one artifact, and is the only arm with Java objects, callbacks, and mapped stack traces. |
| Rationale | OI-35 asked for one executed application per strategy on one managed platform, measured. Direct emission already ran `jsonmin`; the bridge arm runs the same parser group through HotSpot. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_bridge.py`): `jsonmin` through the bridge equals `reference_jsonmin` on seven documents, including 512/513 nesting levels and 64 KiB. `javac`-compiled Java code calls a native XAX kernel with parameters. The output is deterministic. MEASURED (`benchmarks/jvm_strategies_evidence.json`, `XAX_BENCHMARKS.md` §15.19): native-bridge over direct is 0.68× start-up, 0.68× process time on 8 MiB, 0.64× peak RSS, 2.86× artifact bytes, and 0.81× generated-adapter bytes. |
| Limits | The bridge arm is x86-64 Linux only: an AArch64 or Windows JVM needs its own library. It is deployed as a JAR plus a `.so` on `java.library.path`. A trap in native code is a fault in a JNI frame, so HotSpot aborts with a crash report, not an exception and status 1. Each JNI call costs a transition, unmeasured here because `jsonmin` crosses once. CLR has neither arm (no CLI/IL container), so OI-35's CLR half stays unanswered. |

## ADR-166 — JVM class-file importer; OI-32 answered

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Importer.** `xax_jvm_import` reads class files from JDK `.jmod` modules or JARs. It parses them with the existing `parse_classfile` and reads the module's unqualified `exports` from `module-info`. It emits the same canonical `jvm-*` foreign declarations that were written by hand: `jvm-new`, `jvm-invokestatic`, `jvm-invokevirtual`, `jvm-invokeinterface`, `jvm-invokestatic-interface`, `jvm-getstatic`, `jvm-getfield`, and `jvm-putfield`. It loads no class and runs no Java. A request names a member exactly (`Owner.name(desc)`, `Owner.name:desc`, `Owner.name:desc=`) and resolves as the JVM resolves it: the class, then its superclasses, then its superinterfaces. It imports only a public member of a public class in an exported package. Anything else is refused with a reason (not found, not public, not exported, final, an abstract class's constructor, a static write, an inherited static interface method). (2) **Conservative contracts.** Facts a class file lacks are never assumed. Every member takes and returns the I/O effect, so calls stay ordered. References are nullable `jvm-ref` pointers. A functional-interface parameter imports as a plain reference unless the caller asks for callbacks; it then imports as the interface's single abstract method's `jvm_interface_entry_type` (ADR-161). Purity and other effects are curated facts the caller states (`pure`, `effects`). (3) **OI-32 answer.** The package format is the canonical foreign declarations themselves, with no wrapper format or second representation. Ownership, nullability, callbacks, threading, and error conventions are visible to the verifier through their types: effect tokens, nullable reference types, entry types for callbacks, and pure callbacks (ADR-161, so no cross-thread effects). Java exceptions and C errors keep their existing termination and return-value rules. |
| Rationale | OI-32 lacked three things for closure: an executed program over the class-file importer's output, an error rate against curated metadata, and the AI-token comparison. With the C header importer (ADR-127), two unrelated ecosystems now produce packages that executed programs consume. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_import.py`). A program over imported declarations only runs on HotSpot against a Python model: `StringBuilder`, `Integer`, `String`, an `IntStream` with an XAX callback, `java.awt.Point` fields from `java.desktop`, and `Math.floorMod` as pure. The hand-built `java_base_api` is reproduced byte for byte, and refusals and determinism are checked. MEASURED (`benchmarks/oi32_import_evidence.json`):<br>- **Coverage.** `java.base` has 7,554 classes; 1,411 are public in 54 exported packages. Of their 15,806 public and protected members, 14,973 import; 756 protected members and 77 abstract-class constructors are refused.<br>- **Latency and size.** Loading takes 0.38 s and importing 1.79 s (113 µs per request), producing 3.7 MB of declarations.<br>- **Error rate.** Every hand-built JDK declaration in the repository (30) and every hand-built C declaration the C importer reproduces (5) comes back byte for byte: 0/30 and 0/5.<br>- **Offline tokens.** An import request is 0.40× (JVM) and 0.23× (C) the tokens of the hand-built declaration (`o200k_base`: 0.397× and 0.229×). |
| Limits | Token counts are offline (`tiktoken`); no model trial ran, because R5 is deferred. Win32 metadata, .NET metadata, and Web IDL have no importer. Protected members are refused, because XAX cannot subclass. Static field writes were refused here; ADR-167 imports them as `jvm-putstatic`. Generic signatures import erased. A curated fact the caller does not state stays conservative, never inferred. |

## ADR-167 — JVM static field writes

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | One more typed foreign ABI on every JVM profile: `jvm-putstatic`, lowered to `putstatic`. Its library is the class, its name is `name:descriptor`, its only machine input is the value, and it has no machine result. The importer (ADR-166) imports a public, non-final static field's write request (`Owner.name:desc=`) as `jvm-putstatic` instead of refusing it. Static fields are shared mutable state, so the importer's default I/O effect orders each write before later reads and calls. Hand-built declarations should carry an effect for the same reason. |
| Rationale | ADR-163 left `putstatic` out because no `java.base` class has a public mutable static field to execute it against. A scan of every module of the installed JDK 21 finds none. So the executable target is a class on the class path, which is where such fields occur: application and library classes, not the JDK. `run_jvm_calls` takes extra class-path entries for this. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_jvm_objects.py`). The test compiles a helper class with `javac` (`xaxtest.Statics`: `int`, `long`, `byte`, and `String` static fields, plus a static `bump()`). An XAX program writes each field and reads it back. Java's own `bump()` observes the XAX write and increments it, and XAX then reads Java's write. A Python model covers six inputs, including 2^32 − 1 for the 64-bit square and the byte truncation. A write with two inputs rejects (`JVM-FOREIGN-ARITY`). The importer reproduces all nine hand-built declarations from a JAR of that class, byte for byte (`tests/test_xax_jvm_import.py`). `oi32_import_evidence.json` was re-run: still 0/30 errors and 14,973 imports, with its time re-measured. The self-hosted typing store was regenerated. |
| Limits | Final static fields stay read-only: a final field written outside its class's initializer is illegal, and the importer refuses it. A narrow (`byte`, `short`, `char`) write takes its XAX value as stored. |

## ADR-168 — Function-wide register allocation for Linux AArch64

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The Linux AArch64 profiles (`aarch64-linux-elf-exec-v1`, `-dynexec-v1`) lower a function with `xax_aarch64_regalloc` first. Android and board identities are untouched, so their artifacts stay byte-identical. A function with another operation or value form falls back to the ADR-110 register path, then to the ADR-123 frame path. (1) **Values.** Pointer casts, pointer addresses, heap views (after the null check), zero extensions, rebased pointers (after the check), and truncations proven lossless share one value. Constants become immediates or the zero register, are materialized at their use, or, when used in a loop and costing two or more instructions, are hoisted to the entry. Pure nodes nobody reads are dropped. (2) **Allocation.** Liveness is exact SSA dataflow. Each value's live ranges keep their holes, and a block parameter is defined at the end of each predecessor. Values are assigned hottest first (weight 10^loop depth per use, from dominator-based natural loops). A value live across a call takes x19–x29, any other x0–x13 or x19–x29; x14–x17 are per-node scratch. A register is free when no assigned value's ranges intersect; if none is, lighter conflicting values are evicted to frame slots, or the value itself is. A block parameter and its arguments prefer one register. (3) **Code.** Blocks are laid out in reverse postorder with the deepest-loop successor next. A compare consumed only by its block's branch fuses into `cmp`/`b.cond`. A jump to a block that is only a test runs the test in place. A branch edge's copies move before the test when nothing the test or the other path reads lives where they write; otherwise they go through an out-of-line stub. A checked access with a proven index (the shared range analysis, now `xax_ranges`, with an opt-in `or`/`xor` rule) has no check, and a proven `x * size` index becomes a scaled register offset. An OR of three or more `x == k` tests within 64 values becomes a flag-free bit test against a hoisted mask (`lsrv`, then `and` with `((x >> 6) - 1) >> 63`). `(a ^ 1) & b` of 0/1 values becomes `bic`. Failing checks branch to shared `brk` stubs with the frame path's codes. |
| Rationale | The frame path kept every value in memory: `filestat` ran 7.18× `gcc -O2` under qemu (§15.10). The ADR-110 register path covered too few operations for any hosted program. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_aarch64_linux_regalloc.py`). Seeded random programs over 8/16/32/33/64-bit values, with loops carrying up to 14 values, calls inside loops, and every compare predicate, match the reference executor in Unicorn. Further tests cover register pressure past the callee-saved set, the bit test and `bic`, and division traps. A local 1,200-run fuzz (six seeds) found two bugs, both fixed with regression tests: a five-scratch signed compare on odd widths, and the zero register read as SP in add/compare immediate forms. The existing Linux AArch64 execution tests (traps, rebase checks, heap views, `filestat`) pass on the new path. MEASURED-EMULATED (`benchmarks/linux_aarch64_filestat_evidence.json`, `XAX_BENCHMARKS.md` §15.20): `filestat` on 4 MiB under qemu-aarch64 is the fastest arm. clang `-O2` takes 1.082× XAX's time, rustc `-O3` 1.271×, and gcc `-O2` 1.304×, so XAX is 0.767× gcc, down from 7.18×. The executable is 4,688 bytes (was 14,024), 0.069× gcc's. |
| Limits | Emulated only (OI-44): qemu's costs differ from hardware's. A `ccmp` chain was slower under qemu than separate compares, which is why the bit test is flag-free. Only `filestat` among the repository's applications takes the path: `jsonmin` and `uniqcount` use recursion groups, borrowed-view returns, or aggregates, which still fall back. No floats or aggregates. A function needs at most 8 machine parameters, and its frame must fit in 4 KiB. |

## ADR-169 — ADR-168 allocation for the Android general profile; pair saves

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) The function-wide allocator (ADR-168) also lowers functions for `android-arm64-v8a-shared-v4`, the general Android profile. Earlier Android identities (v1–v3) keep their paths and bytes. Functions it does not cover fall back as before: those with JNI table loads (`target.op`), function addresses, indirect calls, atomics, or stack allocations. It never allocates x18, Android's platform register. (2) **Frames.** Saved registers sit at the bottom of the frame as `stp`/`ldp` pairs, the first pre-indexed so it also allocates the frame (`stp xA, xB, [sp, #-frame]!`, and the matching post-indexed `ldp` on return); spill slots follow. (3) A narrow entry parameter that moves register to register zero-extends in the move itself (`mov wD, wS`) when no other entry copy reads its destination or writes its source. (4) A call result nothing reads is neither copied nor masked. |
| Rationale | The counter app's JNI callbacks were 1.33× and 1.27× the NDK twin's (§15.15). The ADR-110/154 register path stores every value live across a call in the frame and reloads it, where clang keeps such values in callee-saved registers saved by `stp` pairs. |
| Evidence | MEASURED (`android_counter_twin_evidence.json`, §15.21). `xaxOnCreate` is 96 bytes against the twin's 96. Its own code is 92 bytes; the symbol size counts alignment padding up to the next function. `xaxOnClick` is 128 against 132. The native library is 2,248 bytes (0.488× the twin's 4,608) and the APK 20,313 (0.703×). EXECUTED with every v4 artifact regenerated by re-running it:<br>- the counter library under Android's `linker64` and bionic via qemu-aarch64 (`android_counter_evidence.json`: seen 0,1,2,3,3,4, every descriptor closed);<br>- all 31 bionic cases (`android_bionic_qemu_evidence.json`);<br>- the platform-contract probe on the Android 12L emulator (`android_platform_runtime_probe_evidence.json`: file, thread, and socket OK, arm64 code through ARM translation, `hardware: false`);<br>- ART verification and the official tools over every committed APK (21/21 each);<br>- the Linux differential corpus and the 1,200-run fuzz. |
| Limits | Size only: start-up and memory need arm64 hardware (OI-44). The emulator runs are correctness evidence. |

## ADR-170 — Native Windows R2: Win64 callbacks and linear thread handles

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | `win64-c` is also a foreign-entry ABI. A pure XAX function's address is its existing Win64 entry, so no adapter is emitted. `win32_thread_api` declares `CreateThread`, `WaitForSingleObject`, `GetExitCodeThread`, and `CloseHandle`; the thread handle carries one linear resource from creation through observation and is consumed by close. |
| Rationale | The PE backend, Win64 calling convention, import table, resource verifier, and function-address lowering already supplied every mechanism. Reusing them proves Windows callback and lifecycle interoperability without a new kernel operation or runtime. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Windows 11 (`compiler/benchmarks/windows_pe_hosted_evidence.json`, `tests/test_xax_pe.py`): a 1,536-byte PE imports the four thread calls plus `ExitProcess`; `CreateThread` calls a pure XAX function returning 37, the program waits, reads 37, closes the handle, and exits 39. The ordinary hosted fixture also re-executed 20/20 times from its current 2,048-byte PE. The matrix therefore advances from R1 to R2. |
| Limits | This proves the callback and thread-handle lifecycle used by the fixture, not arbitrary concurrent shared-memory semantics. Files, sockets, PE exports, TLS, unwind/PDB data, and a Windows-host multi-language performance comparison remain absent. |

## ADR-171 — Stable libxposed hook IDs belong to installation semantics

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Add an optional stable ID to the canonical hook-installation carrier. It is legal only with `retained-manual-unhook`. The hot-reload carrier keeps its transfer policy ID, but lowering/build closure require exact equality and never synthesize an installation ID. The legacy absent-ID encoding remains byte-for-byte canonical. |
| Rationale | Multiple retained hooks need a key on each installation before a build closure can prove uniqueness. Owning the ID only in the hot-reload policy made the installed hook's identity implicit and could not represent independently keyed installations. |
| Evidence | STRUCTURAL. Focused tests cover canonical round-trip, process-lifetime rejection, and hot-reload mismatch rejection. Regenerated `android_libxposed_hot_reload_evidence.json` records the new installation CID while the 34,347-byte APK and 2,920-byte DEX remain byte-identical. |
| Limits | The build closure still admits exactly one adapter/installation pair. Duplicate-ID rejection and ID-indexed replacement of multiple transferred handles are the next slice. Compatible libxposed runtime behavior remains UNEXECUTED. |

## ADR-172 — Android arm64 hardware evidence reaches R4 for the counter workload

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Accept the existing counter twin protocol as Android R4 evidence on physical arm64 hardware: identical app behavior, two alternating install orders, three warmups per arm per pass, 16 measured cold starts per arm, `am start -W` `TotalTime`, post-launch total PSS, and artifact sizes. Extend the twin builder only to select native Windows NDK/SDK executables; generated XAX code and workload semantics are unchanged. |
| Rationale | ADR-153 deliberately withheld startup and memory claims until arm64 hardware was available. The connected Pixel 8 Pro supplies that missing environment, and the existing Java + NDK twin is the platform-established implementation for the same lifecycle/JNI/file-I/O workload. |
| Evidence | MEASURED (`android_counter_twin_evidence.json`, `XAX_BENCHMARKS.md` §15.23). XAX median cold start is 227.0 ms versus 223.5 ms (`1.016×`, meets the <=1.05 primary target); median PSS is 112,862.0 versus 113,411.5 KiB; APK/native-library size ratios are 0.703/0.471. Both click paths pass and all 32 measured launches are tracked. The separate counter oracle executes `0,1,2,3`, restores `3` after process death, then reaches `4` on the same hardware (`android_counter_evidence.json`). The Android row advances R3→R4. |
| Limits | One app, device, OS build, and run. Variance is substantial (XAX 80.25 ms, twin 37.29 ms stdev), so broader device/workload coverage remains desirable. libxposed still lacks compatible framework execution. The file/thread/socket platform probe subsequently ran on the device (ADR-173). |

## ADR-173 — Android platform contracts execute on physical arm64 hardware

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Reuse the existing validation-only C loader and unchanged XAX-generated v4 shared object. Make the shell oracle select Windows NDK `clang.exe --target=aarch64-linux-android24`, preserve local Windows paths, and disable MSYS conversion only for adb remote paths. No target/compiler semantics change. |
| Rationale | ADR-155 proved file, pthread, and socket contracts only through ARM translation on an emulator. With physical arm64 connected and r28c installed, the same oracle can close that environment gap; duplicating it in a Windows-specific harness would add no evidence. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`android_platform_runtime_probe_evidence.json`): Pixel 8 Pro, API 37, native `arm64-v8a`, 17,496-byte shared object, result `XAX_PLATFORM_RUNTIME_OK file=1 thread=1 socket=1`. Imports are exactly close/connect/open/pthread_create/pthread_join/read/recv/send/socket/write. |
| Limits | The C process is validation-only and is not part of the XAX artifact. One device run does not prove every POSIX behavior or Android API. |

## ADR-174 — Bounded target-neutral semantic-edit evidence is not yet JVM R5

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Record the real-model comparison as MEASURED benchmark evidence and close OI-31, but keep the JVM matrix `ai_tokens` field PROTOTYPE and the row at R4. The run uses the existing five paired C/XAX structural edits, one fresh session per cell, fixed `gpt-5.6-luna`/low reasoning, and balanced alternating arm order. The target-neutral protocol can be reused for JVM R5, but §6.2/§15 require task classes this run does not cover. |
| Rationale | R5 measures AI tokens per successful semantic change, not backend runtime, so the target-neutral workspace evidence is relevant. Relevance is not completeness: promoting five local edits would contradict the benchmark policy requiring creation, control-flow, type change/repair, resource/effect repair, stale-root recovery, context scaling, cross-function/API change, and a large-application edit. |
| Evidence | MEASURED (`compiler/benchmarks/ai_native/jvm-r5-evidence.json`, `jvm-r5-results.csv`, `XAX_BENCHMARKS.md` §15.24). All ten cells pass in one turn with no repair: C totals 325,282 tokens; XAX totals 264,535, or 0.813× C (18.675% fewer). Median task cost is 64,857 versus 50,083 tokens. The CSV records model and reasoning metadata and has SHA-256 `893d7f44b74b73afdf4e43bb697a7bd0ca7f5e3189cc2a7c32e90b475d875363`. |
| Limits | This is not R5. Five small local edits, one model/setting, n=1 per cell, and a C-like rather than Java/Kotlin baseline. No significance claim. Creation, control-flow, repair, stale-root, cross-function/API, context-scaling, and large-application tasks remain outside this result. One preflight session whose sandbox denied all file discovery was excluded before the registered run; it produced no completed turn/token record and changed no file. |

## ADR-175 — Direct local semantic transactions clear the 50% aggregate token target

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Add `XAX-DIRECT` as a measured workflow arm. The task supplies the complete bounded semantic view, and one `xax.py apply CMD` invocation writes the transaction, verifies it, commits it, and checks the exact target. `apply` accepts quoted or split command arguments. The paired C arm uses the same controlled Codex client profile, inspects and edits only `program.c`, and runs its task-local exact-target checker. Keep JVM at R4 because this optimizes the existing five-edit slice without completing the required §6.2 corpus. |
| Rationale | ADR-174 spent most tokens on repeated agent context and discovery/verification round trips. XAX already owns a bounded semantic view and transactional verifier, so exposing them as one atomic operation removes those round trips without weakening correctness or changing the requested semantic edits. A separate arm preserves the historical protocol and evidence. |
| Evidence | MEASURED (`compiler/benchmarks/ai_native/jvm-r5-optimized-evidence.json`, `jvm-r5-optimized-results.csv`, `XAX_BENCHMARKS.md` §15.25). All ten registered cells passed in one turn with no repair under the same Codex CLI 0.160.0, `gpt-5.6-luna`, low-reasoning, isolated feature profile. C used 197,252 total tokens; XAX-DIRECT used 98,432, or 0.4990× C: **50.098% fewer**. Median task cost was 39,479 versus 19,704 tokens. The CSV SHA-256 is `574c1769f27968decd4fce71192ac4bff947927a5b69eead0fc3721fe60c5568`. |
| Limits | The 50% result is aggregate; individual cells are not each claimed to clear it. Five small local edits, one model/setting, and n=1 remain insufficient for JVM R5. This is a workflow result, not a syntax-density result: XAX receives its complete local semantic view and atomic transaction/check operation, while C uses source-file inspection, editing, and checking. Exploratory protocol-tuning sessions are excluded and remain visible in the local Codex logs. |

## ADR-176 — Expanded JVM R5 corpus remains incomplete and below the median gate

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Keep the JVM matrix row at R4 and `ai_tokens` at PROTOTYPE. Record the expanded 15-family Java/Kotlin/XAX attempt without promoting it. |
| Rationale | The completed cells do not clear the required 0.50 median ratio, and three fresh retry cells were stopped by the account usage limit before a model turn. Claiming R5 would require both complete successful coverage and the threshold. |
| Evidence | `compiler/benchmarks/ai_native/jvm-r5-full-evidence.json` and `jvm-r5-full-results.csv`: 42/45 cells passed; completed-cell medians are Java 50,342.5, Kotlin 50,737.5, and XAX 38,679 tokens, or 0.7683× the lowest textual median. |
| Limits | The three missing cells are jvm-06 Java/Kotlin creation and jvm-07 XAX control-flow. Retry them under the same fixed profile after the usage window resets; do not alter the corpus or client profile. |

## ADR-177 — Evidence and canonical-state integrity before S7b

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Restore a trustworthy baseline after the independent audit (2026-10-06) before S7b. (1) Canonical stores must regenerate byte-identically on every host and on repeated builds in one process: decline-site codes baked into the facts engine and store verifier are numbered per build, not per process, and `tests/test_store_regeneration.py` (STORE_REGENERATION) runs everywhere, separate from NATIVE_EXECUTION tests. `xax_op_typing.xax` is regenerated: root `4681e6c50bd88ba3d96ab87ddc45ea294a8a9ae3b0658868c8f0b77beb0383ce`, SHA-256 `c3395d39e7f3556910ea1483c59cbc29a8ef3e0a20fee2892b49d4b9f8f99c02`, 2,840,749 bytes. (2) B levels have one executable derivation, `xax_selfhost.bootstrap_status`; `m14_selfhost_evidence.json` and the generated `<!-- xax-status:bootstrap -->` blocks consume it. Each M14 service is hosted only when the XAX graph performs it rather than a host META primitive, so the M14 semantic-image wrapper holds B2–B4 and not B5/B6; no whole-compiler B level is established. S-step fixed points are "component fixed points", never B1–B4. (3) The seed is pinned (`SEED_SHA256`, `SEED_SIZE` in `generate_m14.py`); routine generation reads and checks it and never writes it; `generate_m14.py rotate-seed` is the only writer and packs fixed zip metadata; a rotation is a reviewed change. (4) `xax_native.AUTHORITY` records, per component, requested and actual authority, fallback reason, semantic root, and native image digest; `XAX_REQUIRE_NATIVE=1` turns any unrequested fallback into an error, and self-hosting evidence generators set it. Per-input declines of the store verifier are counted. (5) Native images run from W^X mappings; the on-disk image cache stores authenticated entries (key over the cache schema, store, entry, target profile, generator sources, and backend store; length and SHA-256 of the code) and is ignored when its directory is shared or foreign-owned. (6) The wheel carries every module and the canonical stores (`share/xax/bootstrap`, found by `xax_native.bootstrap_dir`); `tests/test_wheel_install.py` installs it alone and checks. (7) Matrix validator v2: documents, sources, scripts, and images alone never support EXECUTED/MEASURED/PROVEN; MEASURED needs a recorded .json/.csv; MEASURED performance needs raw samples; an R4 runtime verdict is recomputed from per-arm `wall_seconds_samples` with a named host, an XAX arm, and at least two baselines under §15.0/§15.0a, and published ratios must match. (8) Linux ELF, Windows PE32+, browser pages, and SPIR-V modules are produced by `xax_build.build` with provenance (container lowering identity over the target profile identity). |
| Consequences | Linux x86-64 R4→R3 (summary-only runtime evidence; `performance` PROTOTYPE until re-measured with raw samples) and Android arm64 R4→R3 (one Java + NDK baseline; per-pass medians 0.84× and 1.20×). The bare-metal row cites its board execution evidence instead of `XAX_STATE.md` alone. Typing-store native and emulated evidence is marked `native_rerun_required` (OI-45). Production still depends on Python; the bootstrap seed is a Python zipapp and requires Python. |
| Rationale | The audit found a stale authoritative store, B6 asserted against the repository's own derivation, a routine command that rewrote the "immutable" seed, silent host-dependent authority, an unauthenticated executable cache, an installed package without the stores or four modules, and R4 verdicts that could not be recomputed. Each fix removes the mechanism that let the defect exist rather than editing the affected value. |
| Evidence | `compiler/benchmarks/audit_remediation_evidence.json` (`bench_audit_remediation.py`), `tests/test_audit_remediation.py`, `tests/test_store_regeneration.py`, `tests/test_wheel_install.py`, `tests/test_hosted_build_provenance.py`. Executed on Windows 11 x86-64; native-image behaviour (W^X mapping, strict mode on a native host, cache reuse) is UNVERIFIED until run on Linux x86-64. |

## ADR-178 — Vector is the reference runtime for libxposed API-102 modules

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The Android hook contract is XAX -> libxposed API-102 module -> Vector -> ART. Generated modules keep linking only the public `io.github.libxposed.api` surface; Vector (https://github.com/JingMatrix/Vector) is the tested reference framework, replacing "a stand-in framework now, LSPosed eventually". Pinned in `compiler/integration/android/vector/vector_runtime_pin.json`: Vector v2.2 (tag object `8b8fa04d`, commit `88f8e1faa8b4e7ce20aefabe9c295cd746ea038e`), its libxposed-api submodule `39cac0845771547c9c67a3e3ce255af110a54a0e` (tag 102.0.0 plus one documentation commit) and libxposed-service `3318940876192e29cf6ab07637e899e22a87ebf0` (tag 102.0.0), and the API-102 member table parsed from the SHA-256-pinned `api-102.0.0` AAR whose Java sources are byte-identical to Vector's pin. Two contract changes follow from reading Vector and that API revision: (1) a module must declare at least one Java entry (the API says so at `39cac084`; Vector discovers APKs only by `java_init.list`, rejects modules without Java entries, and never opens native entries itself, so `native_init` runs only when the Java entry loads the library); the native fixture now carries the generated `XposedModule`. (2) A stable hook ID (`HookBuilder.setId`, API-102-only) requires `minApiVersion=102` (`ANDROID-APK-LIBXPOSED-HOOK-ID-API`); Vector loads by `targetApiVersion` alone, so the compiler must not advertise API-101 compatibility it lacks. Everything else was already API-102 and is unchanged: `XposedModule` with a public no-argument constructor, `onModuleLoaded`/`onPackageReady` overrides, `HookBuilder`/`Chain`/`HookHandle` descriptors, `PROTECTIVE`, `PROP_CAP_REMOTE = 1 << 1`, the hot-reload callbacks (the saved target `ClassLoader` passes Vector's old-classloader check; the new generation calls no native code, which matches Vector not replaying `onModuleLoaded`), `native_init` naming, and 16 KiB-aligned stored libraries. |
| Rationale | Vector is the runtime the target is meant for, and LSPosed is no longer the reference. Testing against a stand-in framework proved generated code paths on ART, not framework behaviour. Reading Vector's loader, module manager, hook registry, hot-reload path and native API exposed two places where the compiler accepted modules Vector would reject or misjudge. |
| Evidence | Layer 1, STRUCTURAL: `tests/test_xax_vector_contract.py` - all 13 committed module fixtures pass `xax_vector.check_vector_module_apk` (Vector's rules plus exact-descriptor resolution against the pinned table), with per-fixture descriptor sets, API-102 floor, and legacy/implementation/bundled-API rejection. Layers 2-3: ART verify, ART execute (now 13/13 including the native fixture) and official-tools evidence were regenerated by running the tools. Layer 4: `vector_harness.py` (27 checks over 14 signed profiles from `bench_android_vector_profiles.py`, Vector's CLI for enable/scope, logcat, `/proc` maps, JDWP inspection of the generated module in the target; `jdwp.py` exercised against a host JVM) - UNEXECUTED: `android_vector_runtime_evidence.json` is the plan, because no rooted arm64 device with Zygisk and Vector is available here. |
| Consequences | Android stays R3; the matrix states the framework requirement and the UNEXECUTED Vector harness as the blocker. The Gradle `libxposed_fixture` is documented as a packaging/dlopen oracle, not a loadable module. Auto hot reload needs a `versionCode` change (Vector's staleness test), so the harness's second generations bump it. Remote-preference values are written by the module app through libxposed-service, which generated modules do not include; the harness checks capability and default reads only. |
| Limits | No Vector execution has happened. `package_callbacks` and `deopt_order` depend on wait-for-debugger preceding the package-ready phase and are INCONCLUSIVE when it does not. Other libxposed API-102 frameworks are expected to work but are untested. |

## ADR-179 — Self-hosting step S7b.1: the x86-64 views lowering decides target legality and writes the exact diagnostic

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Record.** A backend program that rejects writes one diagnostic record into its output view and sets the status word to `REJECT` (2). The record is the stable payload of `Diagnostic`: code, entity, rule, expected, and actual, each a typed value (INT, packed-ASCII STR, LIST, NONE, CID as four big-endian words, TERMINATOR). The builders and the renderer live in `xax_selfhost_diagnostics.py`. `decode_diagnostic` only renders: packed bytes become text, CID words become hex, a terminator code becomes its `TerminatorKind`. It decides nothing. (2) **Legality pass.** With a diagnostic family (`X86_64.DIAGNOSTICS = "X86_64_VIEWS"`), the shared views builders decide every check of the bootstrap lowering, in the bootstrap's order, so the first rejection is the bootstrap's. The order: closure support for operations and terminators (in the bootstrap's depth-first order); per function, entry first and then by CID, `result_fields`, both `Aggregates` loops, `machine_width` over parameters and non-elided results, then the lowering's own checks in block order (`SINGLE-RESULT`, `CHECKED-ACCESS`, `OP-LOWERED`); and finally the entry interface's widths. The pass runs inside `_translate` on its existing analysis (value bases, extents, aliases, made aggregates, aggregate values), before the stream is written. The rule table `VIEWS_RULES` is the single source of codes and rules; one XAX function, `diagnostic`, writes each code and rule text once. `LOWERED_OPERATIONS` in `xax_x86_64_views.py` is the single list of lowered operations. (3) **Production.** `compile_with_xax` raises the `XaxError` the program wrote; no Python check decides an x86-64 views rejection on Linux x86-64. Off Linux x86-64, and for inputs the program declines for capacity, the bootstrap generator still decides, recorded in `xax_native.AUTHORITY`. (4) **RISC-V unchanged.** The RISC-V program has no diagnostic family, so `_legal` emits exactly `_ok` there and its store is byte-identical. |
| Rationale | S7b names the exact rejection diagnostics as the next authority to move. Writing them from a dedicated legality pass, rather than threading diagnostics through the code generator's declines, keeps check order identical to the bootstrap by construction (separation of concerns). It also leaves the generator's remaining declines as internal capacity limits. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_selfhost_diagnostics.py`): 14 rejection programs, each accepted by the store verifier, exercise every reachable rule (OP-TARGET-SUPPORTED with closure order, TERMINATOR-TARGET-SUPPORTED, VALUE-BITS for parameters, results, and the entry interface, AGGREGATE-RESULT, AGGREGATE-VALUE, AGGREGATE-USE for call arguments and returned call aggregates, SINGLE-RESULT for calls, returns, and phase order, and OP-LOWERED). `backend="xax"` fails rather than fall back, and its `Diagnostic` equals the bootstrap's in every field and in repr. Accepted programs still lower byte-identically: `test_xax_selfhost_x86_64_backend.py` passes, including every helper image and the native gen2 fixed point. Store: `xax_x86_64_backend.xax` root `0890ba48a696391bc3db7b517b035bbd5ae4198b2986dbd0504b53ffcec78dc5`, SHA-256 `bde66d740f35bfb837eb7a7b471dff804091d22a7d5e3a1818f7c579c232b5ec`, 1,369,383 bytes. |
| Limits | CHECKED-ACCESS is implemented but unreachable through verified stores: the verifier rejects the access sizes and pointers it would cover. The entry-kind and target-identity checks, and target decoding, still run in the Python driver before the program (S10). RISC-V diagnostics are S7b.3. The object table and image record are S7b.2. The legality pass makes the backend store larger: 1,369,383 bytes against S7a's 974,862. |

## ADR-180 — The compiler migration ladder after S7b; rejections are part of migration; SH reporting labels

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Inventory.** `compiler/migration/python_authority_inventory.json` classifies every module under `compiler/src` (A authoritative compiler logic, B production orchestration/tooling, C reference, D test/benchmark tooling, E host integration, F historical/bootstrap seed, G generated/non-authoritative). Each A/B component records its Python authority, any XAX-hosted counterpart, target dependency, prerequisite, differential oracle, fixed-point relevance, runtime relevance, and recommended S-step. `tests/test_python_authority_inventory.py` fails when a module is unclassified or a component is undescribed. Progress is counted in authoritative decisions moved, not Python lines removed. (2) **Rejections migrate too.** `XAX_SPEC.md` §16.5 gains condition 5: a component is migrated only when the XAX component also decides its rejections and produces the exact stable diagnostic. S3–S6 and S7a are therefore migrated **for acceptance only**; their rejections still decline to the bootstrap. The S7b record (ADR-179) is the mechanism for the remaining components. (3) **Ladder after S7b**, the smallest dependency order that reaches a full closure on Linux x86-64: S7b (views lowering: diagnostics ✓ ADR-179 for x86-64; then the object table and image assembly; then the RISC-V family) → S8 verifier totality: store decoder, graph decoder, typing/facts, and store verifier reject with the bootstrap's exact diagnostics → S9 canonical store writing in XAX, so a compiler root can be produced without Python → S10 the driver as an XAX program executed as a native Linux x86-64 process: file I/O through the explicit Linux platform package, target-package interpretation, verify → lower → assemble → write → S11 the ELF64 container for views images, so that program is a standalone executable → S12 general x86-64 program lowering (the optimizing backend's decisions) for ordinary programs → S13 build/package/provenance and the minimal command surface (`verify`, `build`, `inspect`) → S14 full-compiler generations C1 → C2 → C3 on Linux x86-64, with a positive/negative equivalence suite and generation identity records (full-compiler B2–B4) → S15 compiler evolution through semantic transactions (B6), other targets through target packages, and a Python-free seed. (4) **SH labels.** Migration status is reported as SH0–SH8 (SH0 mainly Python; SH1 substantial production components XAX; SH2 complete authoritative semantics in XAX; SH3 recursive self-build; SH4 cross-generation semantic equivalence; SH5 deterministic fixed point; SH6 production toolchain closure; SH7 no maintained Python compiler for ordinary evolution; SH8 Python-free audited seed and release path). They are reporting labels, not normative levels, and are distinct from B0–B6 and R0–R6. Current: **SH1**. |
| Rationale | S7b was the last step on the roadmap, but most compiler authority is still Python: every rejection, the store writer, the driver, the program backends, and build orchestration. A ladder that stopped at S7b would invite a premature completion claim. The order puts the self-build path first (the helpers are views-profile programs, so the views backend plus an ELF container plus syscalls is the shortest closure). The optimizing program backends and other targets follow, because the first full closure does not need them. Condition 5 makes the mission's rule explicit: a verifier that accepts in XAX but needs Python to reject is not migrated. |
| Evidence | STRUCTURAL: the inventory and its test. 35 modules are class A, 5 B, 6 C, 4 D, 1 E, 2 F, and 14 G; 26 components are described. |
| Consequences | Roadmap S-table rows S8–S15 are added as planned (UNIMPLEMENTED). Status documents say "migrated for acceptance" where condition 5 does not hold. `FULL_PRODUCTION_SELF_HOSTED` remains NO. |
| Limits | The S8–S15 order is a plan and may be re-cut by evidence (ADR required). The SH labels are not adopted into the specification. |

## ADR-181 — Self-hosting step S7b.2: the views programs build their own object table and image record

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Input.** Both views backend programs (RISC-V and x86-64) take the store's objects in store order, then the bound target. Each object is its kind, its references and its own identity as CID words, and its payload: for a graph fragment the S3c XAX graph-decoder stream, else its body bytes, with no selection by kind. `xax_riscv64._object_table` only copies these words. (2) **Decisions moved into XAX.** A new program function, `find`, bisects the CID-ordered store objects (then the appended target) to locate a CID. The front end uses it to decide whether the bound target is a store object or the appended record, to find the entry function, and to resolve every reference of every object into an index table (`G_REFS`). Python previously decided all three, and also chose which kinds carry a payload. (3) **Image record.** After patching, the program writes, in layout order, each function's CID and its code range (start and end, the end being the next function's start or the code size). `collect_program_output` returns those CIDs and ranges. `xax_riscv64.image_record` renders them, scaling RISC-V instruction words to bytes; it no longer looks up the object table or derives function ends. (4) **Shared.** The change is in the instruction-set-neutral half, so both programs and their stores change together. |
| Rationale | S7b names the object table and image assembly as the driver's remaining lowering structures. The Python side still made decisions there: reference resolution, target placement, entry lookup, payload selection, and function extents. Passing identities as CIDs and resolving them in XAX moves those decisions without duplicating the S3/S3c decoders, whose outputs the programs already read. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED: `test_xax_selfhost_x86_64_backend.py`, `test_xax_selfhost_riscv64_backend.py`, and `test_xax_selfhost_diagnostics.py` pass (20 tests, 217 subtests). That covers byte-identical images for every helper on both backends, random programs, aggregates with stack arguments, the native x86-64 gen2 fixed point, and every rejection diagnostic. Both target placements are exercised: `program_store` holds the bound target, while helper stores lowered for the views profiles append it. Stores: `xax_x86_64_backend.xax` root `f7bdaa521d5bf65f4dbae7a3a6c98a712f9a1f6610b4cac8eb978f1e17395e55`, SHA-256 `6bd710548f0d590a3f34e78d7c274850d186b98ff70d97d9b652541b4016007c`, 1,384,123 bytes; `xax_riscv64_backend.xax` root `1c9245150f9090e11fc3d507573527244be82cff69117a1d3ff29e057a5519ad`, SHA-256 `9cd0e3249c07a024f5a2132cc4802243a5c5280512086611edecf60a257506eb`, 917,236 bytes. `selfhost_closure_evidence.json` and `selfhost_x86_64_evidence.json` were regenerated under `XAX_REQUIRE_NATIVE=1`: every gen1 image equals its reference, and every emulated verdict equals the native one. |
| Limits | The graph-decoder streams are still produced by a separate XAX program, with Python moving words between the two programs; one XAX driver joining them is S10. The entry-kind and target-profile checks and target decoding stay in the Python driver (S10). Off Linux x86-64 the bootstrap generators still decide everything. |

## ADR-182 — Self-hosting step S7b.3: the RISC-V views lowering decides target legality too; S7b complete

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **RISC-V family.** `RISCV64` opts in (`DIAGNOSTICS = "RISCV64"`, `LOWERED_NAME = "riscv64 integer subset"`), so the RISC-V program runs the same legality pass and writes the same record as x86-64 (ADR-179). `xax_riscv64._compile_with_xax` raises the diagnostic the program wrote. (2) **One lowered set.** Both bootstrap generators lower the same operations, so `LOWERED_OPERATIONS` now lives in the shared `xax_views_lowering.py`; `xax_x86_64_views` re-exports it. (3) **Deferred entry check.** The bootstrap checks the entry interface's widths after it finishes the code, and the RISC-V generator can reject at finishing time (`RISCV64-FAR-RANGE`, `RISCV64-JAL-RANGE`). So that check is now deferred in both programs: `_legal(..., deferred=True)` records only the first failure and lowering continues. `_program` rejects with that record only after layout and patching succeed. If patching declines on a range, the program declines, and the bootstrap reports the range rejection it makes first. |
| Rationale | S7b's diagnostics are complete only when both views families decide their own rejections. Reusing the same pass (open/closed: a family opts in with three attributes) moves the RISC-V decisions without new mechanism. Deferring the entry check keeps the first-rejection order exact for both instruction sets. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED: `tests/test_xax_selfhost_diagnostics.py` runs all 14 rejection programs against both families (28 differential cases, every field and repr equal) and checks that production rejections record `actual_authority: xax`. Accepted programs, every helper image, and the component fixed points are unchanged byte for byte (`test_xax_selfhost_riscv64_backend.py`, `test_xax_selfhost_fixed_point.py`, `test_xax_selfhost_x86_64_backend.py`). Stores: `xax_x86_64_backend.xax` root `dcf2f58560cf1929d977812a988990c0ae7337ffaaf245bebde3f18acaadcaba`, SHA-256 `c2d955322ebbba7879d1699df312fe6ba0d8ddd9096035fb02f0c545cf6edf2e`, 1,385,516 bytes; `xax_riscv64_backend.xax` root `4f2432196b27ec6ac4c9c905f9a854702de9398a2dc40d3bdb678631029cbfff`, SHA-256 `22d1b686a4021fe378c6205d7e68fe024bc93c717a53d96141e812418a931df9`, 1,309,426 bytes (S7b.2: 917,236). Native x86-64 and emulated RV64 component evidence was regenerated under `XAX_REQUIRE_NATIVE=1`. |
| Consequences | S7b is complete for the views profiles on Linux x86-64: their legality, diagnostics, object table, and image record are XAX decisions there. The views lowering components satisfy `XAX_SPEC.md` §16.5 condition 5, except for range rejections, which the RISC-V program declines and the bootstrap decides. Next is S8 (verifier rejections). |
| Limits | Range rejections (`FAR-RANGE`/`JAL-RANGE`) still come from the RISC-V bootstrap emitter, and only images over ±1 MiB reach them. CHECKED-ACCESS stays unreachable through verified stores. The entry-kind and target-profile checks stay in the Python driver (S10). |

## ADR-183 — Self-hosting step S8a: the store container's rejections decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Every container check, in the bootstrap's order.** The XAX store decoder (`xax_selfhost_store.py`) now makes every check `StoreReader` makes on a container, in the same order, and writes the exact diagnostic: truncations (`XAX.CANON.TRUNCATED` under `SER-BOUNDS`, `SER-RECORD-LENGTH`, and `SER-INDEX-LENGTH`), ULEB overflow, unterminated, and non-minimal (with the minimal re-encoding), magic, major, hash suite, feature flags, record length and order, non-semantic body, trailing bytes, and order, trailer, trailing store bytes, the digest, index entries, index count, the index-to-records match, and root presence. Entities are rendered from a kind and a number (`store`, `index`, `record:{n}`, `metadata:{n}`). (2) **Order around the digest.** The bootstrap checks the trailer and the exact end, then the digest, then parses and compares the index, then checks the root; the program now does the same. Previously it compared the index while parsing it, before the digest. (3) **Digest in two passes.** With `verify_digest`, the program first reports where the digest ends (`NEED_DIGEST`). The caller hashes that prefix with the XAX hash (S2) and runs the program again with the digest placed after the data (`MODE_VERIFY`); the program then makes the comparison and every later check itself. (4) **70-bit values.** A ULEB carries up to 70 bits. The decoder now keeps the low 64 bits and the high bits, so values the bootstrap prints in diagnostics are exact (record type `WIDE`). (5) **One site table.** `SITES` holds every rejection's code, rule, and value forms. A failing check branches, with its values, to one shared diagnostic block that writes each site's text once. The record format gains `WIDE`, `FORMAT` (a template the program chooses, rendered on the host), `HEX`, and `TUPLE`. |
| Rationale | S8 makes the verifier total (`XAX_SPEC.md` §16.5 condition 5). The store container comes first: every compiler input passes through it, and its rules are fully syntactic. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_selfhost_store.py`). 31 constructed stores, one per rule and including the 70-bit paths, yield the bootstrap's diagnostic in every field and repr, covering all 16 container diagnostic codes. 1,200 random mutations of the committed stores (digest verification on for three in four) are decided identically, acceptance and index included. Production `StoreReader` raises the program's diagnostic, with `actual_authority: xax`. Store: `xax_store_decoder.xax` root `b832e2f552c2fe8d6525910b7f365606177294f76a8fecd330038a5fb459d51c`, SHA-256 `1f0a22e062b6ea78d639c83ecdfd5b871653aee310ebfc2570750c5b32fbe896`, 212,454 bytes (S3b: 29,910). |
| Limits | Still deferred to the bootstrap, which then decides acceptance: a minor version of 2^32 or more (out words are 32 bits), a store with more than 116,507 records, and an index mismatch whose two lists exceed the diagnostic region (more than about 32,000 records). Object-envelope rejections (`decode_object` through `StoreReader.get`) are S8b. The digest bytes are moved between the hash and decoder programs by Python, which compares nothing. |

## ADR-184 — Self-hosting step S8b.1: object-envelope rejections and the CID comparison decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Object mode.** The container decoder program gains two modes, `MODE_OBJECT_HASH` and `MODE_OBJECT_VERIFY`, whose data is one record envelope. It makes every `decode_object` check in its order: the stored CID in bounds; the kind (supported, so 1–11; the expected value is the list of `Kind` members, record type `KIND`); schema version 1; each reference in bounds, then the whole table strictly ascending (the diagnostic quotes every reference); the body in bounds; and the exact end (`SER-OBJECT-BODY-LENGTH`). ULEB errors are reported on the object's entity, `object:<CID hex>`. (2) **CID in two passes.** The program reports the span after the stored CID; the caller hashes the semantic domain plus that span with the XAX hash; the program compares it with the stored CID (`ID-CID-INTEGRITY`). Valid envelopes have minimal ULEBs, so that span is the canonical content. (3) **Production.** For every store the XAX decoder accepted, `StoreReader.get` decides each object through the object mode (`_decode_object_native`), raising the program's diagnostic or building the object from its fields. It replaces `_object_from_parse`, which compared CIDs in Python and handed malformed envelopes to the bootstrap decoder. Only the envelope is copied to the program, so readers of different stores do not reload whole stores. (4) **Sites.** Five sites join the S8a table: `KIND`, `VERSION`, `REFERENCE_TABLE`, `TRAILING_OBJECT`, and `CID_MISMATCH`, plus an `object` entity rendered from the 32 CID bytes. |
| Rationale | Objects are decoded lazily on `get`, so their rejections surface there rather than at store load. Deciding them, and the CID comparison, in XAX completes the decoding half of S8b. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_selfhost_store.py`): 13 valid containers, each holding one malformed object (kinds 0, 12, and 70-bit, an unterminated kind, versions 2 and 70-bit, truncated, unsorted, and duplicate references, a truncated body, a non-minimal body length, trailing bytes, a CID mismatch), raise the bootstrap's diagnostic in every field and repr. The existing differential, which compares every object of 600 mutated stores between the production and bootstrap readers, passes on the new path. Store: `xax_store_decoder.xax` root `75490eae0938ad1eb9d86ce912fecdfede3ec8a303c129ed9970da85dd328f7f`, SHA-256 `d46676a34abbca614324b3ca61b92fc4cec3f234836230ed43f0992016c48108`, 273,370 bytes. |
| Limits | `XAX.IDENTITY.OBJECT_MISSING` (a CID absent from the index) and `XAX.CANON.INDEX_LENGTH` (unreachable once S8a has matched the index to the records) are still decided in Python. Object-backed readers built with `from_objects` never decode envelopes. Graph-body rejections are S8b.2. |

## ADR-185 — Self-hosting step S8b.2: graph-body syntax rejections decided by XAX; one site mechanism for decoders

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Shared mechanism.** The S8a site machinery becomes a mixin, `_SiteDiagnostics` (`xax_selfhost_store.py`). It holds the conditions, the record writers, the value-form grammar (text, integer, `int`, `wide`, `hex`, `format`, `list`, `terminator`, `kind`, `minimal`), `fail_unless`, the shared diagnostic block, and the diagnostic `Cursor` (`uleb_d`, `take`). A decoder supplies its site table, entities, block state, word writer, and reject epilogue. `CURSOR_SITES` (the three ULEB rules and truncation) is defined once and shared. The container decoder's store is unchanged in meaning by the refactor. (2) **Graph decoder.** `_GraphDecoder` adopts the mixin with its own sites: `ENTRY_BLOCK`, `REF_INDEX`, `VALUE_TAG`, `TERMINATOR`, `TRAILING` (`GRAPH-BODY`), the ULEB rules, and the trap payload's truncation. Values are decided before they enter the stream: block count and entry, reference indices, value tags, and terminator kinds are read with all 70 bits, and a value at or above 2^35 is rejected exactly instead of deferred; an over-large count runs until an element fails, as the bootstrap's loop does. Only accepted values too wide for the stream (operation codes, value fields, attributes, edge targets) still defer. The graph's CID follows the body in the input, so the program renders its own entity. (3) **Order with resolution.** On a rejection the program also reports the stream prefix that precedes it. `_parse_graph_uncached` walks that prefix first (reference resolution, type verification, and trap payloads, still Python, S8c), exactly where the bootstrap checks them, then raises the program's diagnostic. A resolution failure earlier in the body therefore still wins. |
| Rationale | S8b's second half: graph bodies are the largest syntactic structure the verifier decodes. Reusing the site mechanism keeps one implementation for both decoders (open/closed, DRY). The prefix walk keeps the interleaving of syntax and resolution exact without moving resolution yet. |
