| Evidence | EXECUTED (`tests/test_xax_selfhost_graph.py`): 18 constructed bodies, one per syntax rule and including 70-bit values and over-large counts, are rejected by the XAX decoder itself (status REJECT, never deferred) with the bootstrap's diagnostic in every field and repr, and the production path raises the same. A missing type before a bad terminator yields the bootstrap's resolution diagnostic. 1,500 mutated bodies of real graphs give the bootstrap's parse or diagnostic. Stores: `xax_graph_decoder.xax` root `05ba650dc678a359093ac0f4d3e1b2520ddfd6737954e04622e22634baba04a6`, SHA-256 `3da3fed07bec537038e245ebeaf0db7c6a54d6e94ee0c3c6cfa7850109866e62`, 148,177 bytes (was 71,275); `xax_store_decoder.xax` root `5f3e5a1c28e99f4deb350fff40e179294c3d5e6910a6cb71c5b2c77f8c6ae0bc`, SHA-256 `72c503c558a7e1d1c9c454d9a9484358ee11085c7d95372b634245b82ad4bc6c`, 274,243 bytes. |
| Limits | Reference resolution, type verification, and trap-payload canonicality inside a graph are still decided in Python while it walks the stream (S8c). Bodies over 1 MiB less 32 bytes, and the deferred wide values above, go to the bootstrap parser. |

## ADR-186 — Snapshot-bound local mutations and reproducible JVM token trials

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Put the local mutation adapter in normal compiler tooling (`xax_local_protocol.LocalMutationSession`), shared by the direct benchmark arms. Derive omitted old attributes from the queried snapshot, submit one exact batch, and retain ordinary verification and the atomic generation check. Keep historical ADR-176 data separate. |
| Rationale | Avoid redundant inspection, file writes, verification round trips, and shell argument repairs without introducing task-aware operations or new kernel semantics. A stale session never silently refreshes. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED: `compiler/tests/test_xax_local_protocol.py`; workspace regression suite. The repeated runner records raw event digests, all completed-turn usage including cached input, fresh attempts, and failed-attempt costs. Real-model outcomes are recorded separately in the new local-results evidence. |
| Limits | JVM remains R4. No runtime backend or performance claim changes. R5 requires complete repeated Java/Kotlin/XAX trials, at most 0.50 of the lowest textual median, and equivalent tasks. The existing creation scaffold is an explicit equivalence limitation, never an automatic R5 promotion. |

## ADR-187 — Host-applied model responses and a controlled JVM comparison

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Ordinary clients apply a final model request through `LocalMutationSession.commit` and request another model response only on rejection. Add task-independent snapshot projection and applicable carrier help, a bounded construction-tool adapter over `GraphBuilder`, and atomic deletion of entire dead pure dependency chains. Compare Java/Kotlin final patches and XAX final semantic requests through the same host-check lifecycle. |
| Rationale | A successful mutation needs no model acknowledgement of a tool's PASS. Removing that round trip is available to normal XAX clients. A valid comparison also needs empty construction in every arm, real application semantics, actual stale conflicts, and stable common instructions. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED: `test_xax_local_protocol.py` and `test_jvm_r5_response.py`. All 15 semantic targets verify; 14 also execute on the JVM. The resource contract uses verifier/exact-target checks. Changed Java/Kotlin targets compile and execute. Raw real-model trials live under `compiler/benchmarks/ai_native/runs-jvm-r5-response-v2`, with source snapshots, instruction-context hashes and all response/repair costs. |
| Method | Same fixed model/reasoning/client flags for all three arms. Textual edits read only their named program file and return a normal patch; XAX receives a bounded projection and returns a normal mutation batch. Creation starts empty in all arms. The large synthetic application contains 160 real helpers plus its target and assertion. No answer or reference command enters a model prompt. |
| Limits | JVM remains R4 until complete repeated token evidence and corpus review justify R5. Exact-root/normalized-source oracles are intentionally restrictive; the large fixture is synthetic. No runtime-backend performance improvement is claimed. Earlier profiles retain their failures, the interrupted bound-profile attempt has unknown final usage, and the first response preflight had unequal plugin instruction catalogs; neither can establish R5. |

## ADR-188 — Exact compound edits reconstructed from a local snapshot

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Normal `LocalMutationSession` clients may set an edge argument, result type or function signature without restating the old value. The adapter derives it from the captured snapshot and expands the request into existing exact workspace mutations. `prune-dead` expands an unused pure root and its dead pure dependencies into an atomic deletion set; dependencies with surviving uses remain. |
| Rationale | Repeating known old fields increases response cost and creates avoidable parsing and ordering failures. Reconstruction belongs in ordinary tooling, with no task identity or expected target available to the adapter. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED: `compiler/tests/test_xax_local_protocol.py` and `compiler/tests/test_jvm_r5_response.py` compare the expanded edits with the existing exact targets. They also reject stale generations, missing exposure, live roots and cross-function values, and preserve shared live dependencies. The affected validation reports 140 tests and 167 subtests passing on the Windows/JDK 17 host. |
| Limits | This changes bootstrap tooling and transaction expansion, not kernel semantics or JVM code generation. Real-model costs remain separate, pinned profiles. Numeric token results require complete repeated trials and a corpus review before any R5 promotion. |

## ADR-189 — Compact mutation aliases and compiled checks for redundant literal conversions

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Add ordinary compact verb aliases, retaining long forms and identical transaction expansion. Shorten applicable help while explicitly preserving pre-batch snapshot semantics and multi-result indexing. Correct the textual checker to admit redundant Kotlin literal conversions only when the rest of the normalized source matches and the compiled JVM instructions, resolved operands and method descriptors match the reference. |
| Rationale | Token savings must come from normal tooling and survive real reasoning/repair costs. An explicit `7.toShort()` must not be rejected merely because the reference relies on contextual literal coercion. A conversion selecting a different overload remains a semantic change and must reject. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED: 142 affected tests and 167 subtests pass on Windows/JDK 17, including exact transaction/target equivalence for aliases, accepted redundant coercion and rejected overload selection. Response-v4 retains 137 attempts, 134/135 successful cells and a partial ratio of 0.501623; its overly strict source checker prevented one Kotlin cell from completing. Its independent audit reports no usage/digest errors. Response-v5 is a new frozen comparison; no old rows are reclassified or imported. |
| Limits | Offline prompt shrinkage is not a successful-task token result. The host-projected graph versus named-file inspection workflow and synthetic/resource corpus limits remain explicit. No JVM runtime or R5 promotion follows from these changes alone. |

## ADR-190 — Lossless result-type defaults and a fresh host-pinned JVM trial

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Declare a uniform single node-result type once per projected function, retaining exact signatures, parameter types and mixed/multiple result lists. Start response-v6 with the current host; pin Python, Java, javac, javap and Kotlin versions before inference and reject missing/broken checker tools. |
| Rationale | Repeated type annotations transfer no new semantic information. Response-v5 cannot be resumed as complete evidence: its transferred Java creation attempt has no final model usage, and the current CLI/checker host differs. Preserve every historical artifact and do not assume the interrupted attempt cost zero. |
| Evidence | The continuation audit reproduces 60/135 successful v5 cells at a partial ratio of 0.5003204395 and identifies the unrecorded attempt. New regression checks cover uniform/mixed/multiple result views and checker-version drift. Fresh v6 results and validation are recorded separately. |
| Limits | Shorter prompts do not establish successful-task savings. Complete three-trial inference, independent raw-usage audit and corpus review remain required. No JVM backend-speed or replacement-level claim follows from this change. |

## ADR-191 — Bound mutation fields and complete negative JVM token evidence

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Ordinary snapshot sessions may bind a caller-selected mutation kind and exposed node before inference. The model supplies every remaining value/index/destination. Submission rejects extra commands, wrong arity, unexposed nodes and changed aliases, and expands through the existing exact transaction verifier. Retain full batches for compound edits and mutations with no remaining fields. |
| Rationale | Protocol §8.2 already permits omission of uniquely bound fields. Repeating the chosen kind/target and the full mutation menu wastes tokens and allows unnecessary replacement sequences. This is ordinary tooling with no task identity or expected target available to the adapter. |
| Evidence | Response-v6 is complete negative evidence: 142 attempts, 135/135 successful cells, medians Java 21,565, Kotlin 21,557, XAX 10,784; ratio 0.5002551375. Independent audit has zero errors and includes every failed cost. Bound-request validation passes 144 tests and 167 subtests; representative prompts save 35–70 offline tokens. Response-v7 is a fresh frozen three-trial comparison, without imported rows. |
| Limits | Shorter prompts alone do not establish a token gate. The bound kind/target versus textual named-file workflow is explicit context asymmetry. Strict source targets rejected Kotlin UShort spelling where the required API uses Short. Synthetic application and abstract resource limits remain. JVM stays R4 pending complete evidence and corpus review. |

## ADR-192 — Immutable bindings and minimum scalar query context

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Fix the mutation kind/target for the lifetime of a bound session; a different binding needs a new session. Serialize binding establishment under the existing workspace lock. Bound integer constant/arithmetic setters may query only the selected node's old value/operation and width. Preserve the full selected view for operand, movement and edge choices. |
| Rationale | A response omitting its target must not be redirected by an intervening rebind. Constant/operation setters cannot modify operands, uses or other nodes, so that unchanged context does not help determine their remaining field. |
| Evidence | 145 affected tests and 167 subtests pass, including rejection of retargeting, alias drift, stale generations and identical restored roots; bound edits reach the ordinary exact targets. Representative scalar prompts are 64–65 offline tokens. Response-v7 stopped safely after 11 successful cells, with all costs known and zero independent audit errors; its CSV and snapshots are retained. Response-v8 is entirely fresh. |
| Limits | Binding is caller-selected and the model still supplies every remaining semantic field. Numeric evidence requires the full repeated corpus and an independent audit. Context asymmetry, strict textual targets, synthetic helpers and abstract resources still prevent automatic R5 promotion. |

## ADR-193 — Accept matching bound commands and reuse ordinary repair

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | A bound response may include its ordinary command kind and target only when both exactly match the immutable binding. Keep movement in the established full-batch form. After any rejected bound response, use the existing ordinary full-batch repair path. |
| Rationale | Field-only formatting caused avoidable model repairs. Reuse the ordinary parser and verifier without relaxing target selection, freshness or exact semantic checks. |
| Evidence | 146 tests and 167 subtests pass. Response-v8 stopped safely between cells at 71 successful cells in 74 attempts; all costs are known and its independent audit reports zero errors. CSV and source snapshots remain separate. Response-v9 starts 135 fresh cells with the same model, reasoning, toolchain and accounting rules. |
| Limits | The stopped partial v8 ratio is not a numeric gate result. A complete independently audited profile is required; corpus/context review still controls R5 promotion. |

## ADR-194 — Complete response-v9 meets the median token gate

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Record response-v9 as complete numeric gate evidence and stop token tuning. Keep JVM R4/PROTOTYPE until corpus/context equivalence review supports R5. Preserve every prior profile and failed cost. |
| Evidence | 135/135 successful cells, three trials per 15-family Java/Kotlin/XAX cell, 142 attempts including seven failures. Medians: Java 21,564; Kotlin 21,566; XAX 10,748. XAX/lowest textual median = 0.4984232980894083, meeting 0.50. Independent audit: zero errors, no live-source drift, complete usage and common instruction hashes. 146 tests and 167 subtests pass. |
| Method | Immutable normal session bindings, minimum scalar context, lossless result-type defaults, matching full commands and ordinary batch repair. All remaining semantic fields are model-supplied; exact verification, freshness and target checks are retained. |
| Limits | The median clears the gate by 34 tokens; aggregate ratio is 0.747651, and repair variability remains. This single fixed-profile run gives no significance claim. Semantic context versus named-file inspection, strict source targets, synthetic helpers and abstract resource contracts require separate review. No runtime improvement or automatic R5 promotion is claimed. |

## ADR-195 — Corpus review rejects response-v9 as R5 evidence; same-prefill response-v10

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Response-v9 (ADR-194) is not valid R5 evidence and JVM stays R4. Its numeric result is retained unchanged as historical, context-asymmetric evidence. The current profile is response-v10, a same-prefill comparison: every arm receives its whole context inline in one request with no tool use. Textual arms get their named program; for jvm-15 they get the lines naming the target, the textual counterpart of the XAX named-role view. XAX gets its ordinary view and role names with no out-of-band mutation kind or target. Textual results pass on the exact normalized source or on equal compiled JVM instructions that differ from the initial program. The owner set the R5 acceptance threshold: 0.50× remains the target and up to 0.55× is accepted. Evidence reports `TARGET_MET`, `ACCEPTED_WITHIN_TOLERANCE`, or `NOT_R5`. |
| Review | (1) Every v9 textual edit cell read its file through a tool call (`context_reads=1`, cached input 2×9,984), so it made two client requests, while XAX made one with its view inline. The 0.498× ratio is about one request against two. In the control family jvm-06 (creation), no arm reads anything, and XAX's median was 10,722 against Java's 10,652 (1.007×). (2) Bound XAX requests received the caller-selected mutation kind and target, which the textual arms had to locate. (3) XAX passed on semantic-root equality, but textual arms needed one exact source text, so equivalent textual edits failed and were retried. (4) For jvm-15, the host handed XAX only the target and assertion functions, while the textual arms scanned a 160-helper file. (5) jvm-09 XAX resource effects are proof values erased on the JVM, while the textual arms execute a runtime tracker. This limit is retained and documented. |
| Evidence | Exploratory v10 pilot `compiler/benchmarks/ai_native/jvm-r5-response-v10-pilot-results.csv` (SHA-256 f4839408a24567cd66a20a8014375fd334a9522d2322a90bb0f0a7f051f66c23): jvm-01 and jvm-15, one trial each, six attempts, all passing. Java 10,753 / 10,723; Kotlin 10,738 / 10,747; XAX 21,965 (one repair) / 10,834. The repair-free cell is 1.010× the lowest textual cost. A probe with one-line base instructions still used 6,932 input tokens for a one-word reply, compared with 10,474 under the default instructions: the client's tool schemas and framing set a floor common to every arm. |
| Consequence | Under a fair single-request comparison in this client, even a zero-cost XAX payload stays near 0.93× and cannot reach 0.55×. The full 135-cell v10 run was not executed because it would spend about 1.5M tokens on a predetermined miss. OI-46 records the measurement question. |
| Limits | The two-task pilot is not a gate result. Removing the shared client overhead requires a client or API without fixed tool schemas, used identically by every arm; that change is a measurement-design decision, not a protocol optimization. |

## ADR-196 — Minimal-client same-prefill JVM run (response-v12): XAX ≈ 1.0× textual; R5 unmet

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Option (a) of OI-46, with the owner's model choice. All arms use the pinned Codex 0.160.0 client with every configurable optional tool, skill and instruction block removed. Base instructions are one line, and the model is `gpt-6-luna` (low reasoning, the catalog's "fast and affordable" model). The remaining per-request floor F is calibrated in-profile. It is 3,501 input tokens, identical in all three samples. The gate ratio subtracts F once per client request from every arm, every failed attempt still counts, and the raw ratio is reported alongside. Two normal-adapter fixes preceded the run. First, edit forms are listed one per line and labelled as placeholders, because the model copied `const N INTEGER` literally and read inline alternatives as edits. Second, callee parameters in cross-function views are aliased (`P0`) instead of leaking a raw handle (`F2.B0.P0`), and the help states that `sig` retypes entry parameters. Each source change started a new profile. v10 (stopped after the help defect) and v11 (stopped for the alias defect, 113/135 cells) are retained unchanged. |
| Evidence | `compiler/benchmarks/ai_native/jvm-r5-response-v12-results.csv` (SHA-256 c39f72bedb1e7162938ab1d642669be7c0fe9846fd4bad4c794c332c94cc6bac), its evidence JSON and `jvm-r5-response-v12-audit.json` (zero errors, no source drift). One full pass plus one refill pass: 151 attempts, 123/135 cells, 28 failed attempts (XAX 16, Kotlin 10, Java 2). Medians including failed costs: Java 3,710, Kotlin 3,702, XAX 3,701. Raw ratio 0.99973, floor-adjusted ratio 200/201 = 0.99502. Aggregate recorded tokens: Java 200,712, Kotlin 283,035, XAX 322,843 (1.61× Java). |
| Result | R5 is unmet by a wide margin (target 0.50, acceptance 0.55), and JVM stays R4. On these single-function edits the semantic view plus edit list is about the same size as the inline Java or Kotlin program plus a patch, and the model is less reliable on XAX than on Java. Persistent XAX failures were jvm-09 (no-op operand edit), jvm-15 (did not update the assertion constant) and jvm-07/jvm-14 (wrong edge, extra parameter retype). The run is incomplete (12 cells never passed), so no gate verdict is possible. The measured medians would need to fall by about half for the gate to pass. |
| Limits | Kotlin jvm-12/jvm-14 failures come partly from corpus wording: "u8 → u16" invites `UShort`, while the target is `Short`. The gate uses the lower textual median, so this does not decide the ratio. jvm-09 XAX resource effects remain proof-only on the JVM. Floor subtraction depends on the one-character calibration prompt and is applied identically to all arms. One host, one model and one client. The v11 Kotlin jvm-12 trial-3 attempt was interrupted by session teardown after two complete traces. It was recorded with both costs, and its round-2 host check was replayed from the retained trace. |

## ADR-197 — Vector runtime executed on hardware; hot-reload register fix; heap-dump oracle; device ART route

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) Run the ADR-178 Vector harness on a rooted Pixel 8 Pro (Android 17/API 37, KernelSU 3.1.2, Zygisk Next 1.5.0, Vector v2.2 3080). (2) **Fix:** the generated `onHotReloading` emitted `invoke-interface {v0, v2}` (third unit `0x0020`) for `HotReloadingParam.setSavedInstanceState(ClassLoader)`, making the saved `ClassLoader` the receiver. The correct encoding is `{v2, v0}` (`0x0002`). (3) **Explicit debuggable semantics:** `AndroidManifestSpec.debuggable` (default off) emits `android:debuggable` on `<application>`. When off, neither the identity nor the binary manifest changes, so every existing manifest CID and APK is byte-identical. The harness targets set it; the modules under test do not. (4) **Heap-dump oracle:** attaching JDWP to any Vector-hosted process aborts ART on this build (`oat_file.cc:2594 Check failed: method_index < num_methods_ (method_index=65535)` in `Instrumentation::UpdateEntrypointsForDebuggable` while the agent attaches), including a module that installs no hook, while the same target without a module attaches normally. The harness therefore defaults to `--oracle heap`: `am dumpheap` (in-process, no JVMTI agent) plus a minimal HPROF reader (`integration/android/vector/hprof.py`). It decides class-loaded, instance, field, hook-record and hot-reload-generation checks. Checks that need a breakpoint or an invocation stay UNEXECUTED; JDWP remains selectable with `--oracle jdwp`. (5) **Device ART route:** `XAX_ART_DEVICE=1` runs `bench_android_art_verify` and `bench_android_art_execute` with the device's own `dex2oat64`/`oatdump`/`dalvikvm64` and boot image, as root over adb (`benchmarks/android_art_device.py`). The libxposed API comes from the pinned AAR (`XAX_LIBXPOSED_AAR`, SHA-256 checked). `bench_android_official_tools` uses the existing host-portable `_sdk` lookup. |
| Rationale | Only layer 4 claims framework behaviour, and it found a defect that layers 1-3 could not: ART verification defers interface-receiver checks, and the stand-in framework never calls `onHotReloading`. A heap dump observes the generated module in the target without the debugger path that this ART build cannot survive under Vector. Re-running layers 2-3 on the device replaces a qemu run of Android 14 tools with the hardware's own ART. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on hardware: `compiler/benchmarks/android_vector_runtime_evidence.json` has 18 PASS, 0 FAIL and 9 UNEXECUTED across 14 profiles, and the target survived every profile. PASS: discovery, module_prop, java_init, native_init, scope, instantiated, hook_installed, pass_through, result/argument/combined replacement, retained_handle, stable_hook_id, hot_reload_callbacks, saved_state, replace_hook, stable_id_validation, process_survives. Before the fix, Vector logged `onHotReloading of xax.generated threw java.lang.IncompatibleClassChangeError` and kept one generation. After it, Vector logs `Hot reloaded xax.generated`. The new generation holds the target `ClassLoader`, and the framework record behind its handle carries ID `xax.primary` and the new-generation `XaxHooker`. With a mismatched ID, the new generation keeps no handle and the old record keeps the old hooker. Device ART: `android_art_verify_evidence.json` 21/21 Verified with the negative control rejected (`NotReady`); `android_art_execute_evidence.json` 13/13. `android_official_tools_evidence.json` 21/21 (build-tools 36.0.0 on Windows). `tests/test_xax_libxposed.py` pins the corrected operand; `tests/test_xax_vector_harness.py` checks the committed run and the debuggable default. |
| Consequences | The Android row's Vector blocker narrows to the nine breakpoint/invocation checks: package_callbacks, class_loader, intercepted, deopt_order, manual_unhook, module_services, remote_preferences, remote_files, protective. `android_libxposed_hot_reload_fixture.apk` and the Vector profile hashes changed; no other committed APK did. ADR-178's "no Vector execution has happened" is superseded. |
| Limits | One device, one Vector build. The heap oracle reads Vector's obfuscated HookHandle record structurally (reachable strings and `XaxHooker` instances, two levels deep), not by field name. The JDWP abort is an ART/Vector interaction on this build, recorded rather than worked around. The ART execute layer still uses the recording stand-in framework and does not call the hot-reload callbacks. |

## ADR-198 — Android counter R4 restored: three arms, rotated passes, equal AOT compilation

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Re-measure the Android counter under §15.0 with two baselines. The first is `clang_ndk_java`, the ADR-153 Java + NDK twin, as the C/C++ arm. The second is `java_d8`, a new pure Java twin (`benchmarks/android_counter_java_twin/`), as the arm outside C/C++. `bench_android_counter_twin.py --device` now runs four passes with the arm order rotating by one, reinstalls each arm per pass, and compiles it with `cmd package compile -f -m speed` (`XAX_TWIN_COMPILER_FILTER`) so no arm depends on background dexopt. It records per-pass medians and XAX's per-pass ratio, and writes `host` and `results` with raw `wall_seconds_samples` for the matrix validator. `build_twin`/`_measure` accept a twin without native code. |
| Rationale | ADR-177 withdrew R4 for two reasons: one baseline arm, and a pooled median hiding a 0.84×/1.20× pass effect. A Java twin is the platform-native non-C baseline (§15.0 names Kotlin/Java for Android). Two-arm alternation cannot separate arm from order with three arms; rotation does. Uncontrolled post-install compilation state was the most plausible source of the pass effect, so it is fixed and equal for every arm. |
| Evidence | MEASURED on Pixel 8 Pro hardware (Android 17/API 37, AC power, Battery Saver off), `compiler/benchmarks/android_counter_twin_evidence.json`. Median `TotalTime` over 16 tracked cold starts per arm: XAX 277.0 ms (1.007×), Java + NDK 275.0 ms (fastest), Java 286.5 ms (1.042×). Per-pass XAX/fastest ratios are 1.00, 1.053, 1.06 and 1.00. Median PSS: 107,168.0 / 107,161.5 / 107,050.0 KiB. `xax_replacement.validate` is clean and derives R4 from the raw samples. §15.29 has the full record. |
| Consequences | The Android arm64 row returns to R4 for this application on this device. The Android half of OI-45 is closed; the Linux R4 re-measurements remain. |
| Limits | One app, one device and one run. All arms slow by about 100–150 ms from pass 1 to pass 4 (device state drift), which is larger than the pooled XAX margin; two passes sit at 1.05–1.06, inside the 1.10 band but not under 1.05. Four starts per arm per pass. No Kotlin arm (the Kotlin runtime would add size; it is not required by §15.0 once a Java arm exists). The `speed` filter is equal for all arms but is not the default install-time state. |

## ADR-199 — General Android managed classes replace per-API managed carriers

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Add one identity-only carrier, `android-managed-class-v1` (`xax_android_managed.py`). It declares a DEX class (descriptor, superclass, interfaces) and the methods the platform calls; each forwards to an XAX JNI export. The carrier is the managed half of the Android ABI: lifecycle overrides, listener and callback interfaces (`SurfaceHolder.Callback`, `View.OnTouchListener`, `MediaCodec.Callback`, ...) and value-returning callbacks such as `onKeyDown` are all instances of it, so new Android APIs need no new carrier. `DexForwardingOverride` gains a range form (non-void results, `long` parameters, five or more registers); the original 35c form is kept for void methods with at most four registers, so every existing DEX is byte-identical. The build adds a managed-class APK profile (v3 or general v4 target) that checks each export's non-proof ABI against the Java descriptor. `float`/`double` stay rejected until JNI has an exact float carrier. |
| Rationale | AutoHead (an Android Auto head-unit test project) needs Activity pause/resume, SurfaceHolder lifetime, touch and key input, permission results, MediaCodec and AudioTrack callbacks, and Bluetooth/Wi-Fi Direct listeners. One carrier per API, like the existing UI/Surface/receiver/service carriers, would grow a DSL per platform feature, which the mission forbids. The DEX bridge was already general; only its exposure was per-feature. The old 35c path also claimed five registers but packed a fifth into the wrong field, a latent overflow fixed by capping the compact form at four. |
| Evidence | STRUCTURAL: `tests/test_xax_android_managed.py` (canonical round trip, rejections, compact/range/wide/boolean forms, super-before-native order, signature check accepting the exact ABI and rejecting a wrong width, a missing argument, a wrong class and a missing result). ART: the fixture's three classes (`MainActivity`, `SurfaceCallback` with `surfaceChanged(SurfaceHolder,III)V`, `WideProbe.stamp(J)J`) are `Verified` by the device's own `dex2oat64`. EXECUTED on Pixel 8 Pro / API 37 (`compiler/benchmarks/android_managed_evidence.json`): the APK built only from managed-class carriers resumes. Its XAX `onKeyDown` returns `keyCode == VOLUME_UP` as a `jboolean`, which consumes VOLUME_UP (media volume unchanged). A control build whose XAX result is false lets PhoneWindow change the volume. Three HOME/relaunch cycles keep one process with `call_super` lifecycle overrides, and no fatal exception occurs. Existing APKs are unchanged (Android/DEX/build tests: 275 passed). |
| Consequences | The managed side of any Android SDK callback is now declarative and general. The remaining AutoHead gaps are on the native side: calling SDK members from XAX through JNI (plans exist in `xax_jni` but have not run on a device), `float` JNI values, NDK media/audio imports, and permissions in the manifest. Existing per-feature carriers remain for their committed artifacts; new work should use managed classes. |
| Limits | Invalid attempts are recorded: one run with the phone locked (no Activity could resume) and one BACK-key oracle without a control. BACK cannot show a boolean result because `Activity.onKeyDown` only starts tracking and the back action runs in `onKeyUp`; the control showed no difference, so that oracle was replaced by the volume-key oracle. The managed-class profile does not yet compose services/receivers/application classes in the manifest. JNI short names forbid overloads within one class. |


## 2026-10-07: platform-carrier-replacement-v1

Use exact identity-only TARGET replacement with generation-bound workspace
handles to maintain declarative platform packages. Reconstruct supported package,
request and snapshot ancestors with their canonical constructors; never rewrite
raw bodies, signed assertions or provenance. Privately lower affected Android
APK requests before publication. Stale replacements require fresh queries rather
than automatic rebase. This bounded general service closes AutoHead's maintenance
blocker without adding an application syntax, machine-target mutation or runtime
feature. Tests and contract: compiler/integration/android/CARRIER_TRANSACTIONS.md.

## ADR-197 — Multi-file JVM corpus (OI-46 option b): XAX 1.68× the IDE-excerpt baseline, 0.35× whole files

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Option (b) of OI-46. `compiler/benchmarks/jvm_r5_multifile.py` generates the Java, Kotlin and XAX programs for each task from one spec. Each program has five classes: helper chains, a 16-bit function, and task functions. There are three families: mf-01, a cross-file 8-bit to 16-bit API change of a callee and its three callers; mf-02, an operation change in a 40-method class plus its dependent assertion; and mf-03, a constant change whose effect reaches two assertions through a call chain. XAX receives the target and its transitive callers through the workspace `callers` query. Each textual language has two workflows that receive the same call hierarchy: whole files, which is what an agent reads, and an IDE-style excerpt of exactly the hierarchy's methods. Following the R5 rules, the gate compares XAX with the lowest of the four textual medians. Client, model, calibration and floor subtraction are as in ADR-196. The Kotlin jvm-12/14 wording now says "8-bit/16-bit integer" instead of "u8/u16". |
| Normal-protocol changes | (1) Workspace correctness fix: an edited function whose unedited callee depended on another edited function kept calling the stale callee. Edited and rebuilt functions now share one dependency order, and `tests/test_xax_workspace.py` has a regression test. (2) `type OLD_TYPE NEW_TYPE` (shown functions) and `type F OLD_TYPE NEW_TYPE` (one function) expand exactly into result-type and signature mutations. `type P NEW` on an entry parameter is that function's signature-parameter change. (3) Edits the batch already implies are dropped: exact duplicates, and signature edits of one function that agree field by field. Conflicts still reject. (4) Node diagnostics name the handle's kind ("Units.scale is a function; use sig …"). The trial adapter aliases integer types by shape (`b8`, `b16`) and shown functions by the task's names. A trailing `;` stays malformed, because an existing negative vector treats it as possible truncation. |
| Evidence | `compiler/benchmarks/ai_native/jvm-r5-multifile-v5-results.csv` (SHA-256 9414ad6a56deac5ef95939b6de419e0bdb033be60c535142360dd04a39f8c0aa) and its evidence JSON. Model `gpt-6-luna` (low), client floor 3,501. One full pass plus one refill: 48 attempts, 42/45 cells. Floor-adjusted medians: Kotlin excerpt 286, Java excerpt 306, XAX 481, Kotlin files 1,315, Java files 1,391. XAX / lowest textual = **1.682** (raw 1.051). XAX is 0.35× the whole-file workflows. Recorded totals: Java excerpt 34,122, Kotlin excerpt 33,919, XAX 62,310. Failed attempts: XAX 6, all textual arms 0. Every XAX mf-02 attempt published a verified wrong target: the model changed the operation but never the assertion constant (19 → 11), so the run is incomplete. Profiles v1–v4 were stopped after protocol defects surfaced and are retained unchanged. |
| Result | R5 is unmet, and JVM stays R4. XAX's semantic neighborhood beats whole-file textual context by about 3×. Against an IDE-style excerpt of the same call hierarchy, XAX loses, for two reasons. Each request carries about 120 tokens of edit-form help, and the model already knows Java and Kotlin syntax, so the textual arms pay no such cost. The model is also less reliable on XAX (6 failed attempts against 0). The owner's preference that XAX be the lowest arm is not met. |
| Limits | Synthetic helper chains, three families, one model and one host. There is no independent audit module for this runner yet; the evidence JSON is recomputed from the CSV by `summarize`. The R5 rules require the excerpt workflows. Without them, XAX would compare at 0.35×, but that would drop a valid lower-token competitor. |

## ADR-200 — Shared edit grammar replaces per-request edit-form help

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | ADR-197 left a gap: about 120 tokens of edit-form help in every XAX request, which the textual arms don't pay. This is changed in the normal protocol, not in a benchmark adapter. `xax_local_protocol.edit_grammar(compact=...)` is the complete carrier grammar (13 forms, four notes and the batch rule; 1,016 characters in compact form). It depends only on the protocol version: no snapshot, task, generation or handle. `edit_grammar_id(...)` is its content identity (`xax-edit-` plus 12 hex digits of SHA-256). A client sends the grammar once per conversation or session as shared context, then calls `session.instructions(shared=ID)`, which returns no per-request help. A mismatched ID rejects, so a client holding an older grammar is told its grammar is stale. Without `shared`, `instructions()` keeps the ADR-196 per-neighborhood help, so existing clients are unchanged. |
| Invariants | Acceptance does not change: exposure, generation, exact old fields, verification and staleness checks are identical, and a form that does not apply to the shown handles rejects with the ordinary diagnostic. The shared grammar covers every form that `instructions()` advertises, which `tests/test_xax_local_protocol.py` checks for both the compact and long spellings. The grammar contains no task identity or expected edit. |
| Evidence | Unit tests only. There is no model measurement yet. |
| Limits | The token saving only appears where shared context is cheaper than repeating it: across several requests in one conversation, or through prompt caching. In the ADR-196/197 one-request-per-cell profiles, every request would still carry the grammar once, and the full grammar is larger than the filtered per-request help. Measuring it needs a new profile. In that profile the grammar is a pinned shared-context block, counted as transmitted (cached tokens reported separately), and the owner decides how it is accounted against textual arms that need no grammar. `xax_local_protocol.py` is in the pinned `SOURCES`, so v5 and v12 cannot be continued under this source; their rows stay as recorded. |

## ADR-201 — Multi-file profile v6: the XAX arm holds the shared edit grammar

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | This wires ADR-200 into a new `benchmarks.run_jvm_r5_multifile` profile, v6 (`jvm-r5-multifile-v6-results.csv`, `runs-jvm-r5-multifile-v6`). The XAX arm's client base instructions are the common one-line `BASE` followed by `edit_grammar()`. The XAX prompt calls `instructions(shared=edit_grammar_id())`, so it carries no per-request edit help, and repairs work the same way. Textual arms, the client flags, the model (`gpt-6-luna`, low) and the corpus are unchanged. The manifest pins `xax_base_instructions` and `shared_grammar_id`. |
| Accounting | The common floor F is calibrated as before. A second calibration (`calibration-xax.json`) runs the same one-character request under the XAX base instructions, and G = F_xax − F is the grammar's input cost per request. **The gate subtracts only F from every arm**, so XAX pays G in every request it makes, which is what the client actually transmits. Two informational ratios bracket the owner's open accounting decision: G counted once per attempt (one conversation that holds the grammar), and G excluded (fully cached or amortized shared context). They never set the status. Cached input tokens stay in the CSV. |
| Evidence | Unit tests only: `tests/test_jvm_r5_multifile.py` checks that the XAX prompt has no edit help, that its base instructions end with the current grammar, and the bracket arithmetic. No inference has been run. |
| Limits | One request per response round and one client process per round, so the gate cannot show a saving from sharing unless the client's prompt cache discounts it, and recorded input tokens include cached tokens. The informational ratios are not R5 evidence. Profile v5 and its rows stay as recorded. |

## ADR-202 — Exact JNI `jfloat`/`jdouble` carriers and typed-record `jvalue` packs

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | `xax_jni` maps `F`/`D` to the existing IEEE `f32`/`f64` kernel types (JNI 1.6 defines `jfloat`/`jdouble` as binary32/binary64), so `Call*FloatMethodA`/`Call*DoubleMethodA` results, `Get/Set{Float,Double}Field` and `android-managed-class-v1` callbacks with `F`/`D` parameters or results are exact. The AAPCS64 backend already passes floats in V registers. Argument packs keep their two existing forms byte for byte (homogeneous `Z/B/C/S/I/J`; `jlong` plus strong references). Every other shape — `jfloat`, `jdouble`, or narrow integers mixed with other slots — uses a new `typed_record` pack: one record whose fields are each slot's exact value (or `jvalue.l` reference word) followed by explicit zero padding to 8 bytes, addressed through the ADR-097 record-field rule. No conversion or bit cast is added to the kernel. |
| Rationale | Touch coordinates (`MotionEvent.getX(I)F`), axis values (rotary input), audio volume and `MediaCodec.queueInputBuffer(IIIJI)V` were unreachable. The kernel already had floats; only the JNI package refused them. |
| Evidence | STRUCTURAL + verified lowering: `compiler/tests/test_xax_jni_float.py` verifies and deterministically lowers to Android arm64 four stack-pack calls (`getX(I)F`, `setAlpha(F)V`, `queueInputBuffer(IIIJI)V`, `getAxisValue(II)F`) and checks the record layouts cover each 8-byte slot. A `jint` passed where the descriptor says `jfloat` rejects (`MEMORY-STORE-TYPE`). Managed `(FD)F` callbacks emit DEX and the export check accepts exactly `(JNIEnv*, this, f32, f64) -> f32`. No device execution. |
| Consequences | ADR-199's float exclusion and the spec's "mixed narrow integers MUST reject" rule are superseded. Existing JNI fixtures, packs and APKs keep their identities. |
| Limits | No ART run of a float JNI call yet. Weak-global references still reject in packs. |

## ADR-203 — Android platform declarations: permissions, features, foreground types and managed components

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | A new identity-only carrier, `android-platform-declarations-v1` (`xax_manifest`), lists `uses-permission` (optional `maxSdkVersion`, `neverForLocation`), `uses-feature` (`required`), and Service/BroadcastReceiver components with `foregroundServiceType` flags and intent actions. The managed-class APK profile accepts 0..1 of it; other profiles reject it. The emitted manifest adds the four new attribute IDs to the resource map only when used, so every existing manifest is byte-identical. Build-time rules: a foreground type needs `FOREGROUND_SERVICE` and, at `targetSdk >= 34`, its per-type permission and declarable prerequisite (`RECORD_AUDIO` for microphone, `CAMERA`, a location permission, one connected-device permission); `BOOT_COMPLETED` needs `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED`; a declared component must be a managed class extending `Service`/`BroadcastReceiver`; a `maxSdkVersion` below `minSdk` is reported. `specialUse` is not offered (it needs a `<property>`). |
| Rationale | ADR-199 left the managed profile without permissions or extra components, which blocked services, boot receivers, Bluetooth, Wi-Fi, audio capture and foreground work. |
| Evidence | STRUCTURAL: `compiler/tests/test_xax_android_platform.py` (canonical round trip, field rejections, exact binary XML attributes and types, unchanged bytes without declarations, rule positives/negatives, a deterministic managed APK registering a boot receiver, and three rejected builds). |
| Limits | Runtime grants are not manifest facts. The emitted manifest has not been installed on a device. |

## ADR-204 — Android capability contracts: NDK ownership, POSIX async I/O and SDK permission rules

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) `xax_platform.posix_async_api`: sockets, `accept4`, `timerfd_*` and `eventfd` hand over the ADR-153 linear descriptor token; `close`/`close_socket` consume it. `poll` gives readiness with a deadline, `eventfd` cancellation, creation flags give nonblocking mode without the variadic `fcntl`, `getsockopt(SO_ERROR)` completes a nonblocking connect, pthread mutex/condition calls cover worker synchronization, and `rename`/`fsync`/`unlink` cover atomic file replacement. (2) `xax_android_platform` adds NDK contracts with linear tokens: `ANativeWindow` (from a Java Surface, borrowed by geometry calls, released once), `AMediaCodec`/`AMediaFormat`, and AAudio with the direction in the token type (an input stream can only `read`, an output stream only `write`). (3) A table of exact SDK members per capability (runtime permissions, foreground service, Bluetooth adapter/paired devices/discovery, RFCOMM client, SDP-registering RFCOMM server, network callbacks and requests, Wi-Fi Direct, local-only hotspot, `SSLEngine` TLS, Java MediaCodec, SurfaceHolder, AudioTrack, AudioRecord, audio focus, touch, keys) with permission rules by target and device level, acquire/release pairs, and minimum API. The managed-APK build now always checks: each JNI member or NDK symbol reachable from the exports has its permissions declared (with `maxSdkVersion` covering the levels that need it), its minimum API is within `minSdk`, an acquiring member's release member is reachable, a managed class that extends or implements a listed callback type implements every abstract method, and `Activity` lifecycle overrides call super. |
| Rationale | Most AutoHead rows were "unsupported" because no contract stated ownership, permissions or callback obligations. Contracts and build rules are the platform-package layer; nothing enters the kernel, and unused contracts add no code. |
| Evidence | STRUCTURAL + verified lowering: `compiler/tests/test_xax_android_platform.py` — a codec created, started and deleted verifies; leaking it (`XAX.RESOURCE.DROP`) or deleting it twice rejects; an output `write` on an input stream rejects; the window geometry borrow verifies; an unclosed socket rejects and a closed one verifies; timerfd create/close verifies; the codec function lowers to an Android ELF whose only `DT_NEEDED` is `libmediandk.so` with exactly its three imports; reachable `AudioRecord.<init>` needs `RECORD_AUDIO` and its `release`; Bluetooth rules differ by target/min SDK; reachable AAudio input needs `RECORD_AUDIO`; incomplete `SurfaceHolder.Callback` and a missing super call are reported. The existing managed fixture still builds unchanged. |
| Limits | No device execution of any new contract. The release-pair check is reachability, not path-sensitive pairing (JNI references have no capability-specific token yet). NDK media input buffers are not bounds-proven views and releasing a NULL `ANativeWindow` is the caller's branch (OI-47). TLS has contracts but no handshake evidence. The table is not an import of the whole SDK; members outside it get no permission check. |

## ADR-205 — Emulated execution of exact JNI float calls and typed-record packs

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | Add host-emulated execution evidence for ADR-202 instead of waiting for a device: the compiled Android arm64 function text runs in Unicorn's AArch64 CPU against a mock `JNIEnv` whose table slot points at a stub that captures `x0..x3` and the `jvalue[]` bytes and returns a value in `s0`. The emulator is a test tool, not part of the artifact. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED under Unicorn (not hardware, OI-44): `compiler/tests/test_xax_jni_float.py::test_emulated_*`. `MotionEvent.getX(I)F` passes env/receiver/method ID unchanged, packs the `jint` with zero padding and returns the stub's 1.5f from `s0`; `View.setAlpha(F)V` stores 0.25f as its exact IEEE bits plus zero padding; `MediaCodec.queueInputBuffer(IIIJI)V` produces the exact 40-byte `jvalue[]` layout. |
| Limits | No ART; the stub stands in for the JNI implementation. Device execution remains open. |

## ADR-206 — Emulated execution of NDK and POSIX contract programs; AAudio handles are words

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | `compiler/benchmarks/android_elf_emulator.py` maps an XAX Android arm64 shared object's `PT_LOAD` segments in Unicorn, points every `R_AARCH64_GLOB_DAT` import slot at a recording stub, and runs one export. AAudio returns its builder and stream through out-parameters, so `aaudio_api` now types those handles as 64-bit words loaded from caller stack cells (`ptr<b64>`, space 1); the direction-typed linear tokens still carry the object type and direction. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED under Unicorn (not hardware, OI-44): `compiler/tests/test_xax_android_platform_emulated.py`. A codec program calls create → start → stop → delete through `libmediandk.so` slots with the handle the platform returned; an AAudio output program builds, configures (48 kHz, stereo), opens, starts, writes 240 frames with a 10 ms timeout, stops and closes, passing the handles the stubs wrote into its stack cells; an owned socket is created nonblocking, shut down and closed with the same descriptor. |
| Limits | Stubs stand in for Android's libraries; ordering and argument flow are proven, platform behaviour is not. |

## ADR-207 — Replacement levels v3: R4 performance leadership, R5 autonomous maintenance, R6 XAX-only application

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) R4 is *performance leadership*: XAX's median execution time is at most 0.9999× the median of the fastest valid **non-XAX** implementation of the identical workload on the same hardware (XAX never in the denominator), compared unrounded, and a one-sided Mann–Whitney test on the raw `wall_seconds_samples` of XAX and that competitor gives p < 0.05. Runtime, memory, and binary size stay MEASURED for every arm. The 1.05×/1.10× bands of ADR-147/157 confer no level. (2) R5 is *autonomous maintenance* (the former R6). (3) R6 is a *proven 100% XAX-developed application*: designed, implemented, tested, built, deployed, and maintained with XAX as the sole authoritative language for application-owned logic, tests, and build definitions, with reproducible provenance, an auditable graph and change history, executed functional tests, and one verified XAX-only maintenance/release cycle. New matrix field `xax_only_application` (EXECUTED/PROVEN) gates it. (4) The former R5 (AI tokens ≤ 0.50× the lowest textual baseline) becomes a separately scheduled future AI-efficiency milestone outside R0–R6; the `ai_tokens` field is historical and gates no level; no token trials run in R4–R6 work. Validator `xax-replacement-validator-v3`. |
| Rationale | The upgrade brief sets R4 to leadership, not parity, and reprioritizes the roadmap to R4 → R5 → R6. A 0.0001 margin is below the old 0.005 ratio tolerance and three-decimal summaries, so the comparison is exact and a noise test guards against claiming a difference the samples cannot support. |
| Evidence | MEASURED recomputation: `compiler/tests/test_replacement_matrix.py`. JVM `jsonmin` (`compiler/benchmarks/jvm_jsonmin_evidence.json`): XAX median 0.1222 s vs `javac` 0.1463 s = 0.835x, p ≈ 3e-5 over 15 samples per arm, so JVM keeps R4 (one workload, one host; re-run at 0.884x in §15.31). Android counter cold start (`compiler/benchmarks/android_counter_twin_evidence.json`): 1.007273x the fastest non-XAX arm (`clang_ndk_java`), so `android-arm64` drops from R4 to R3. No row has R5 or R6 evidence. Published ratios are checked at their stored precision; `linux_harness` now records raw samples and `time_ratio_vs_fastest_competitor`. |
| Consequences | ADR-147/157's thresholds and ADR-195's 0.55× R5 acceptance are superseded as level gates; their records stay historical. The matrix cited gitignored JVM run manifests; those citations were dropped and the committed `jvm-r5-optimized-results.csv` regained the CRLF bytes its recorded digest binds. |
| Limits | A single workload never establishes universal leadership; a row's R4 is scoped to its cited workloads. The Mann–Whitney normal approximation assumes independent samples from interleaved runs. |

## ADR-208 — Predicate tables on the x86-64 register path; Linux raw-sample re-run

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | When a boolean value or branch condition is computed from one value whose proven upper bound is below 256, using at least three compare/arithmetic nodes (`int.compare`, add/sub/mul wrap, and/or/xor, zero-extend, truncate) whose results have no other use, the register path reads it from a byte table instead: the compiler evaluates the expression exactly for every value of the subject, places the table after the function's code, and emits `lea r11, [rip + table]` plus one `movzx` (value) or `cmp byte` (branch). Tests shared with other code are walked through and kept. A rotated-loop alignment tweak was measured, gave nothing, and was dropped. `jsonmin`'s XAX string loop takes the C twin's shape. |
| Rationale | `filestat`'s per-byte loop is uop-throughput bound: removing the three-instruction clamp of the ADR-131 bit test alone made it 10% faster. Table lookup is exact by construction (it is the expression's value table), adds no kernel operation, and costs at most 256 bytes per distinct table. |
| Evidence | MEASURED (§15.30): `filestat` 0.881962x and `jsonmin` 0.967682x clang -O2, both significant; `chains` 0.983564x rustc -O3, p = 0.29. EXECUTED: `compiler/tests/test_x86_64_optimizations.py::PredicateTableTests` runs three predicates (range union, signed compare with mask, wrapping product) in value and branch form over all 256 bytes against Python references and checks the table bytes are in the image; the existing membership, jsonmin, and regalloc differential tests pass. |
| Consequences | Linux x86-64 `performance` is MEASURED again (OI-45 closed); the row stays R3 because `chains` does not lead. `jsonmin`'s stripped binary grew to 1.21x clang's. AArch64 and the views backends are unchanged. |
| Limits | One shared host; three workloads. Subjects must be proven below 256; wider subjects keep the bit test. |

## ADR-209 — Recursion-group members are editable; first R5 maintenance record (JVM `jsonmin`)

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) A workspace transaction may edit a recursion-group member. The member's graph is mutated as before; the group is rebuilt with it and put back in canonical member order (renumbering `call.group_member` indices when an edit moves the order); every member function identity of the old group is replaced; their users are rebuilt by the ordinary affected-user walk. An edited member that calls a rebuilt non-member function still rejects (`WORKSPACE-RECURSION-EDIT-CALLEES`). (2) `compiler/benchmarks/bench_r5_maintenance.py` records one maintenance cycle of the JVM row's R4 application: query (callees, function nodes, one bounded projection), one local mutation bound to the queried generation, verified commit, JAR rebuild, contract tests at the new behaviour, a before/after benchmark, and a canonical save. |
| Rationale | The first real maintenance request on `jsonmin` (raise the nesting limit from 512 to 1024) touched a group member, and the workspace rejected it (`GRAPH-GROUP-CALL-CONTEXT`): it rebuilt the member as a standalone function. Recursive programs are ordinary; R5 cannot exclude them. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED: `compiler/benchmarks/r5_jvm_jsonmin_maintenance_evidence.json`. The agent (Claude, in the Claude Code session that added this ADR) read the projection lines `N3 constant 512` and `N4 int.compare P0 N3` and wrote `const N3 1024` (13 bytes). The commit moved generation 0 → 1 with a 19-byte transaction: 8 objects touched, 119 reused. The rebuilt JAR matches `reference_jsonmin` at depth 1024 on 16 cases (3 change behaviour: depth 513–1024 now accepted), and the original JAR still matches the 512 contract. Before/after medians on a 4 MiB document, 15 fresh-JVM runs each: 101.8 / 104.4 ms (p = 0.23, no measurable change). `compiler/tests/test_workspace_recursion_edit.py` commits 38 member edits of a mutually recursive pair (some move the canonical order) and executes each; it also replays the R5 edit and checks the committed root. |
| Consequences | The JVM row derives R5. |
| Limits | One cycle, one application, one constant edit, by the session's agent; the harness replays that edit, so the record is reproducible but the agent's choice is not re-made. Edits to a group member that calls a rebuilt function, and edits that change the group's member set, are not supported. |

## ADR-210 — General construction carrier; first XAX-only application (`xb64`)

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) `xax_construct.construct` decodes one `xax-construct-v1` request (types, multi-block functions, Linux platform entities, a package with build entries) into a verified build snapshot store. It is a tool-call carrier like `construct_program`, not source: names are request-local, the store is authoritative, and later changes go through workspace transactions. No kernel operation was added; shifts are `mul`/`udiv` by powers of two. (2) `xb64`, a base64 encoder compatible with GNU `base64` (76-column lines, streaming through 48 KiB chunks), was constructed by the agent in one request: four functions plus an XAX `selftest` entry (RFC 4648 vectors and both wrap edges). Its package declares `app` and `test` build entries; both artifacts come from the canonical build service with provenance. Release 2 (PEM-style 64-column lines) is one 64-byte workspace transaction that changes the width and the dependent XAX test expectations together. |
| Rationale | R6 needs an application whose logic, tests, and build definitions are all XAX. Until now every application graph was produced by Python builder code, and `construct_program` built only straight-line integer functions. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED (`compiler/benchmarks/r6_xb64_evidence.json`, `bench_r6_xb64.py`): the request reconstructs the committed `xb64-gen0.xax` byte for byte; the transaction gives `xb64-gen1.xax` (10 objects touched, 89 reused); every app and test build is reproducible; both selftests exit 0; the deployed executables match coreutils `base64 -w 76` / `-w 64` on 40/40 inputs each (0 B to 1 MiB, chunk and wrap boundaries); the width-only edit fails the XAX selftest (exit 8). `compiler/tests/test_xax_construct.py` covers the carrier's rejections and replays both generations. |
| Consequences | `linux-x86_64` cites `xax_only_application` but stays R3: levels are cumulative and its R4 (`chains`) does not hold. The ADR-125 recursion carriers are not in the carrier yet. |
| Limits | One small application on one platform package (Linux x86-64). A short write is reported as exit status 1 rather than retried (no dynamic pointer offsets in the carrier's Linux surface). The x86-64 backend's four-argument, one-result call limit made the program pack value pairs into 64-bit words. The test build request is derived from the package's declared entry by the harness. The construction request was written by the session's agent; no independent agent has repeated the cycle. |

## ADR-211 — Next-iteration prefetch and edge sinking on the x86-64 register path

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) `xax_prefetch.prefetch_next_iteration` (lowering view; canonical program unchanged): in a loop body that loads `h = P[g(f(x))]` from a single block parameter `x` through pure arithmetic and then branches into an inner loop, append a copy of the index expression with `x` replaced by the value the body passes on in `x`'s position (the guess for the next iteration), one more load of `P` at that index, and mark it; the backend follows that load with `prefetcht0 [Q + h' * s]` for every inner-loop array `Q` read at `block-parameter * s`. The appended load must be proven in bounds by value ranges, division is only by nonzero constants, and the load joins the region's memory-token chain. (2) Edge sinking: an add/sub of an immediate whose only use is one argument of its block's conditional-branch edge is emitted on that edge as `lea` (a move with an offset in the parallel-move solver), so a loop's exit-only `found + 1` leaves the loop. |
| Rationale | `chains` is a dependent walk of cache misses; XAX and rustc ran the same loop at the same speed. A C prototype showed prefetching the next lookup's first node saves about 11%. Prefetches have no architectural effect and never fault; a wrong guess only wastes a hint. |
| Evidence | MEASURED (§15.32): `chains` 0.943346x rustc -O3 (p = 0.019), `filestat` 0.896075x clang -O2 (p = 2.5e-5); `jsonmin` 1.029472x clang -O2 (p = 0.63) with a binary byte-identical to the one that measured 0.967682x in §15.30. EXECUTED: `compiler/tests/test_x86_64_optimizations.py::EdgeSinkAndPrefetchTests` (32/64-bit wrap through a sunk `lea`; the chains view gets exactly one mark with scales 4 and 8 and a plain loop gets none; a small chains program with prefetches matches an independent Python reference). |
| Consequences | Linux x86-64 stays R3 in this run: `jsonmin`'s lead is within run-to-run variation on this host. The same binary passing in one run and failing in the next is reported, not re-run until it passes. |
| Limits | The prefetch pattern is one shape (outer loop, pure index recurrence, inner walk over entry-block arrays); it is a heuristic, exact by construction. One shared host. |

## ADR-212 — Single-edge parameters in the lowering view; jsonmin reads in 1 MiB windows; Linux x86-64 reaches R4

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) Lowering-view simplification `_local_parameters` (`xax_inline`): in a block entered by exactly one edge from another block, a value that edge passes as an argument is read through the block's own parameter, so it is not also live across the edge. (2) `jsonmin`'s read window grows from 64 KiB to 1 MiB on every platform (the C twin requests the whole remaining capacity per `read`; 130 reads become 10). A trial that ordered block parameters by call crossing was measured, gave no gain, and was dropped. |
| Rationale | `jsonmin`'s string loop read the byte through its predecessor's parameter while also receiving it as its own, costing a register and two moves per character. Its earlier margin over clang was inside run-to-run variation (ADR-211). |
| Evidence | MEASURED (§15.33): `filestat` 0.851851x clang -O2 (p = 3.4e-7), `jsonmin` 0.959479x clang -O2 (p = 0.031), `chains` 0.911278x rustc -O3 (p = 0.0094); the validator derives R4 for `linux-x86_64`. `jsonmin` instructions 154.0 M → 151.0 M (callgrind). JVM `jsonmin` re-run: 0.865214x javac (p = 0.0012), JVM stays R5; the R5 record was re-recorded against the changed program (16/16 cases, after/before 0.961). EXECUTED: `compiler/tests/test_x86_64_optimizations.py::LoweringViewTests::test_single_edge_block_reads_its_own_parameter`; the full suite passes except the host wheel tests. |
| Consequences | `linux-x86_64` is R4. It is not R5 or R6 yet: R5 needs an executed maintenance record for this row; `xb64` (ADR-210) already supplies R6 evidence. |
| Limits | One shared host, one run per workload; `jsonmin`'s margin (p = 0.031) is small and its previous binary measured both 0.968x and 1.029x, so R4 on this row is not robust to host variation. |

## ADR-213 — R5 maintenance record for Linux x86-64; the row derives R6

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | `bench_r5_maintenance` takes a platform row (`--row=jvm|linux-x86_64`) and runs the same cycle on the Linux x86-64 build of `jsonmin`: queries, the projection, one local mutation bound to the queried generation, verified commit, ELF rebuild, contract tests, interleaved before/after benchmark, canonical save. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED: `compiler/benchmarks/r5_linux_jsonmin_maintenance_evidence.json`. The Linux projection shows the same `N3 constant 512` / `N4 int.compare P0 N3` lines the agent read for the JVM (ADR-209), and the same 13-byte edit `const N3 1024` commits as a 19-byte transaction (8 objects touched, 119 reused); 16/16 contract cases pass (3 change behaviour as intended); after/before median 1.019 (no measurable change). With R4 (ADR-212) and the R6 evidence of `xb64` (ADR-210), the validator derives R6 for `linux-x86_64`. |
| Limits | The Linux edit is the agent's JVM edit applied to the Linux store after checking the Linux projection, not a separately reasoned change. R4 rests on one run with a small `jsonmin` margin (ADR-212). R5 and R6 each rest on one cycle by the session's agent. |

## ADR-214 — S8c.1: the XAX typing program rejects integer-family nodes

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The typing program (`xax_op_typing.xax`, builder `xax_selfhost_typing.py`) gives a fourth node verdict, `REJECTED` (3), for the integer families (`add/sub/mul.wrap`, `bit.and/or/xor`, `udiv`, `urem`, `int.truncate`, `int.zero_extend`, `rotate.right`). For each such node it writes a four-word record `[site, a, b, c]` at `DIAGNOSTICS + 4 * node` (`DIAGNOSTICS = OUT_WORDS / 4`, below the type tables and above every verdict). The site is the bootstrap's first failing check, in its order: arity (`GRAPH-OP-ARITY`, `INT-WIDTH-CONTRACT`, `INT-ROTATE-CONTRACT`), then the operand/result type (`GRAPH-OP-TYPE`, `INT-ROTATE-TYPE`), then the width or amount (`INT-TRUNCATE-NARROWS`, `INT-ZERO-EXTEND-WIDENS`, `INT-ROTATE-AMOUNT`). The payload holds the counts, type indices, or widths the diagnostic quotes. A type the program cannot decode as `bits` (the bootstrap raises its own decode diagnostic) or a clamped attribute leaves the node `NOT_PROVEN`, so the bootstrap decides it as before. `verify_store` raises the program's diagnostic at the node's own position in the bootstrap's pass, so an earlier failure still wins; the host only renders fixed code and rule strings and maps type indices to CIDs (`marshal(..., cids=)`, `rejection`). |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64: `tests/test_xax_selfhost_typing.py` checks every site against the bootstrap's exact diagnostic (code, rule, entity, expected, actual), and the 2,000-node random corpus checks that every node XAX rejects (more than 40) fails with the bootstrap's diagnostic. The store was regenerated with `write_typing_store`. |
| Limits | S8c.1 covers the integer families only. Float, compare, aggregate, sum, resource, meta, constant, and call typing rejections, the facts engine's declines, and store-verifier object rejections are still decided in Python (S8c.2 onward). Off Linux x86-64 the bootstrap decides every node. |

## ADR-215 — S8c.2: float and integer-compare typing rejections decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The ADR-214 mechanism extends to `float.add/sub/mul/div`, `float.compare`, `uint/sint.to_float`, `float.to_uint/sint.trunc`, `float.convert`, and `int.compare`. Each family lists the bootstrap's checks in order (arity, operand agreement, result type, width limit, comparison kind, link equality); a check whose bootstrap form would first raise a type-decode diagnostic, or would quote a clamped attribute, has site 0 and leaves the node to the bootstrap. Rendering is table-driven (`_SITES` in `xax_selfhost_typing.py`): fixed code and rule strings, the enum value lists, and type indices mapped to CIDs. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64: one constructed case per new rule (15) matches the bootstrap's exact diagnostic, the 2,000-node corpus checks every XAX rejection, and an ad-hoc 6,000-node run over four other seeds found 687 XAX rejections and no mismatch. |
| Limits | Aggregate, sum, resource/effect, meta, constant, and call typing rejections, the facts engine's declines, and object verification remain Python (S8c.3 onward). |

## ADR-216 — S8c.3: aggregate and sum typing rejections decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | `aggregate.make/get` and `sum.make/tag/get` join the ADR-214 mechanism. A family's operand or result type is decided only when the program decoded it (its `AGGREGATE` table entry); a type whose single-byte form is known and is neither tuple nor array is rejected by form (`AGGREGATE-*-TYPE`); every other undecoded type, and a clamped index, stays with the bootstrap. Once a family's earlier checks pass, its last check fails exactly when the node's rule does, so the program reuses the rule's own flag. Element lists in the diagnostics are rendered from the program's tables (`NativeTyping.rejection` reads `AGGREGATE`/`COUNT`/`ITEMS`), and a make's operand types from the input stream at the node's position, never by decoding types in Python. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64: one constructed case per rule (12 new), the 2,000-node corpus, and an ad-hoc 6,000-node run over four seeds (973 XAX rejections, no mismatch). The x86-64 and RV64 self-hosting evidence files were re-run natively against the new store. |
| Limits | Resource/effect, meta, constant, and call typing rejections, the facts engine's declines, and object verification remain Python (S8c.4 onward). |

## ADR-217 — S8c.4: meta-operation typing rejections decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The fourteen meta operations join the ADR-214 mechanism with `_verify_meta_node`'s order: arity (`META-OP-ARITY`, one site per operation so the renderer knows the expected counts), each operand's kind in position order (`META-OPERAND-TYPE`), the result rule (`META-RESULT-TYPE`, `META-VERIFY-RESULT`, `META-TARGET-SUPPORT-RESULT`), then `meta.target_supports`'s operation code (`META-TARGET-OPERATION`). `_is_opaque` is false for a type whose body does not start with form 5, which is a rejection; a form-5 type the program did not decode, a `bits` operand or result it did not decode, and a clamped operation code stay with the bootstrap. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64: one constructed case per rule (6 new), the 2,000-node corpus, and the four-seed 6,000-node run (1,291 XAX rejections, no mismatch). Both self-hosting evidence files re-run natively against the new store. |
| Limits | Resource/effect, constant, and call typing rejections, the facts engine's declines, and object verification remain Python (S8c.5 onward). |

## ADR-218 — S8c.5: resource/effect typing rejections; the rejection pass becomes its own function; views-backend regions resized

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | (1) **Resource and effect operations** join the ADR-214 mechanism in `_verify_resource_effect_node`'s order: attributes, `effect.step`'s matching operand/result types, each operand's effect form, domain-instance uniqueness, then per operation the counts, the effect continuation, the resource form, and the operation's flag or type rule. `effect(cid)`/`resource(cid)` reject a type whose body does not start with form 3/4 and defer one of that form the program did not decode. The diagnostics' CID tuples, `_EffectType` values, flags, and transition lists are rendered from the input stream and the program's tables. A node with several attributes, or a clamped one, stays with the bootstrap (its diagnostic quotes the whole tuple). (2) **Rejection pass.** Adding this code to the node loop made the typing program's code too large for the XAX backends, so the rejection logic now runs in a separate function: after the node verdicts, main calls the pass with the facts engine's signature; it walks the node stream again and turns each `NOT_PROVEN` node whose first failing check it identifies into `REJECTED` with its record. (3) **Views-backend regions.** The XAX backends still declined: the facts engine's liveness bitsets (5 x blocks x values / 64, about 12M words) plus about 3.6M words of whole-program front-end data no longer fit the 16M-word arena. From measured use (3.5M code words, 19k jumps, 179k ranges), the regions are now code 5M words, jumps 0.5M, ranges 2M, stream 4M (unchanged), and the arena about 19M. Both backend stores were rebuilt. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64: 15 constructed resource/effect cases (7 rules) plus a direct node check for the attribute site match the bootstrap exactly; the four-seed 6,000-node run finds 1,635 XAX rejections and no mismatch, with the typing image lowered by the XAX x86-64 backend (`XAX_REQUIRE_NATIVE=1`). Both backends lower the new typing store byte-identically to the reference generators, and both self-hosting evidence files were re-run natively. |
| Limits | Constant and call typing rejections, the facts engine's declines, and object verification remain Python (S8c.6 onward). The arena margin is about 2M words; a larger program needs a larger output view or a smaller liveness representation. |

## ADR-219 — S8c.6: constant and direct-call target rejections decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The rejection pass decides `GRAPH-CONSTANT-TARGET` and `GRAPH-CALL-TARGET` (no entity, or an entity of another kind, named from the stream's kind word) and `GRAPH-CONSTANT-CONTRACT` (operands, or a result other than the constant's type, rendered from the stream). A constant object the program does not accept stays with the bootstrap, whose `_decode_constant` raises the object's own diagnostic. `marshal` now leaves out a constant or call node whose entity cannot be indexed, so a missing entity word always means no entity. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64 with the natively lowered typing image: four constructed cases (both target forms, both contract forms) match the bootstrap exactly; the four-seed 6,000-node run finds 1,650 XAX rejections and no mismatch. Both self-hosting evidence files re-run natively. |
| Limits | Call-contract mismatches (`GRAPH-CALL-CONTRACT`) and the call branch's later checks, the facts engine's declines, and object verification remain Python. |

## ADR-220 — S8c.7: direct-call contract rejections decided by XAX

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | For a direct call whose callee is a graph-fragment function, the rejection pass decodes the callee's interface as `_decode_function_interface` does (graph reference, parameter and return type references, exact end, every type one the program decoded) and writes it to a list area after the diagnostic records (`[n, parameters..., m, returns...]`). When it decodes and differs from the node's types, the node is rejected with `GRAPH-CALL-CONTRACT`, rendered from that list and the input stream. Recursion-group member callees, types the program did not decode, and lists that would not fit below the type tables stay with the bootstrap. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64 with the natively lowered typing image: constructed mismatches against every fragment callee in the test pool match the bootstrap exactly; 216 random call nodes over seven seeds give 51 XAX rejections and no mismatch; the four-seed 6,000-node run finds 1,687 rejections and no mismatch. Both self-hosting evidence files re-run natively. |
| Limits | Group-member call contracts and the call branch's later checks (resource call contracts, view effects), the facts engine's declines, and object verification remain Python. |

## ADR-221 — S8c.8: the XAX facts engine decides stack-memory rejections

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The facts engine (`xax_selfhost_facts.py`) gains `_reject`: a check that is exactly one of the bootstrap's memory checks, in its order, records on failure the site (`MEMORY_SITES`), where it failed (pass, block, node, or the node count for the terminator), and a payload, unless an earlier check already failed. Exact sites: pointer provenance and lifetime, the operation contract, element size, alignment and bounds of plain accesses, read/write permission, frontier presence, linearity (with the consuming operation, now tracked per value as `ECOP`) and provenance, uninitialized loads, address-offset bounds, stack-end owner presence and liveness, stack-allocation alignment, and the end-of-function lifetime leak, for `stack.alloc`, `stack.end`, `address.offset`, `load.bits.le`, and `store.bits.le`. `verify_store` runs its fact passes as before when the engine declines, but at the recorded point it raises the engine's diagnostic instead of running the bootstrap check. If the passes end without reaching that point, it still raises it. A diagnostic that would quote a call-result storage, and every other engine check, still only declines. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64 with the natively lowered typing image: the stack-program corpus (300 programs; use after end, double end, leak, forked frontier, out of bounds, misalignment, uninitialized load, wrong size, read-only store, end in loop, under-aligned allocation) matches the bootstrap's full diagnostic (code, rule, entity, expected, actual), and more than 90% of its rejections are decided by the engine. An ad-hoc run over eight further seeds (1,200 stack programs, plus view programs) gives 805 engine-decided rejections, 19 still decided by the bootstrap (heap-view and checked-access rules), and no mismatch. Both self-hosting evidence files re-run natively. |
| Limits | Heap views, checked accesses, rebase windows, foreign and view-passing calls, atomics, links, and type and contract checks inside the memory operations still decline to the bootstrap. Object verification remains Python. |

## ADR-222 — Component-store verification is memoized in the native image cache

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | `xax_native.verify_component_store(reader, name)` replaces the unconditional `verify_store` in the loaders of the committed compiler-component stores (hash, store decoder, graph decoder, CFG, typing, store verifier, RISC-V encoder, x86-64 views backend). The first process to verify given store bytes with a given verifier writes `verified-<name>-<key>.bin` (magic plus key) into `XAX_NATIVE_CACHE`. Later processes that find the exact record skip re-verification. The key is sha256 over a schema tag, `verifier_identity()` (every compiler module — `xax_*.py` and `blake3.py` — and canonical bootstrap store, by name and content hash), and the store bytes. Any compiler change therefore invalidates every record (XAX_SPEC §13.3: conservative over-invalidation, never under-invalidation). A failed verification writes nothing. Without a private cache directory, or with `XAX_NATIVE_REVERIFY=1`, the store is verified every time. Memoized loads appear in `xax_native.AUTHORITY` as `verification:<name>`. |
| Trust | The record adds no authority: `cache_dir()` returns only a private, owner-only directory that already supplies the native machine-code images this process maps and executes. Anyone able to forge a record could already replace the code. |
| Evidence | MEASURED on the reference Linux x86-64 host, first `construct` in a fresh process with a populated image cache: 21.9 s before, 2.7 s after (the rest is the native store verifier's table setup and BLAKE3 CID checks). `tests/test_verified_component_store.py` checks first verification, reuse, binding to the store bytes, name and verifier identity, corrupt records, the opt-out, refusal of a group-writable cache, and that a failed verification writes no record. Full suite (Linux x86-64, `-n 4`): 1353 passed, 53 skipped. |
| Limits | The 3.4 s remainder and the cold case (an empty cache still lowers every image) are unchanged. Evidence that must show the XAX verifier running on component stores should set `XAX_NATIVE_REVERIFY=1`. |

## ADR-223 — `linux.startup.*` entities in `xax-construct-v1`

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The construction carrier names the `linux-x86_64-startup-v1` reads (ADR-094) as `linux.startup.<name>`: `argc`, `arg_length`, `arg_copy`, `envc`, `env_length`, `env_copy`, `auxv_value`. Unknown names reject. The carrier adds no semantics: the entities are the existing `linux_startup_api()` declarations, still valid only in the process entry function (`LINUX-STARTUP-PROCESS-ENTRY` at build). Requests that do not use them produce byte-identical stores, so the xb64 generation-0 reconstruction is unchanged. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64: a constructed program echoes argv[1] (clamped to its 64-byte view) and exits with argc; a startup read in a non-entry function fails the build with `XAX.NATIVE.STARTUP` (`tests/test_xax_construct.py`). |
| Limits | Linux x86-64 only, like the carrier. |

## ADR-224 — `linux-x86_64-process-v1`: the host-facing process contract

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | `xax_linux.process_contract()` publishes, as versioned data, what a host may rely on when it runs a Linux x86-64 XAX executable: the container and empty runtime, the entry contract, inputs (fd 0; argv/env/auxv only through startup reads), outputs (fds 1 and 2), termination (explicit `exit_group`, status modulo 256; a failed check or an entry return executes `ud2`, giving SIGILL), and system calls (exactly the declared imports reached). It describes existing behaviour (ABI §19.4) and adds no runner: `run_linux_executable` stays a test harness, and confinement stays the host's concern. Changing a clause requires a new identity. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED: `tests/test_xax_linux_process_contract.py` checks every clause against built executables (exit statuses 0, 7, 255, 265 → 9; SIGILL on entry return and on an out-of-bounds checked load chosen at run time; fds 1 and 2; entry-contract rejection). |
| Limits | Static and dynamic Linux x86-64 profiles. Other platforms' process lifecycles keep their own sections (OI-33). |

## ADR-225 — `xax-host-contract-v1`: a versioned integration surface

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | `xax_contract` lists the module names external integrations may depend on (construction, workspace and local edits, build and provenance, target facts, Linux APIs and the process contract, compiler identity, and the authority record and component verification) and the formats they produce. It carries `HOST_CONTRACT = "xax-host-contract-v1"` and `HOST_CONTRACT_MINOR` (additions bump the minor revision; removals or meaning changes need a new identity). The `xax-compiler` distribution version keeps its meaning (none), and no release tag is created. |
| Evidence | `tests/test_xax_contract.py`: every listed name exists, and the format identities equal the constants of the modules that define them. First consumer: XAX-MCP (`MrxSiN/XAX-MCP`), which checks `missing()` at startup. |
| Limits | The contract covers names and formats, not internal signatures beyond those its tests exercise. Interfaces outside the list may change without notice. |

## ADR-226 — S8c.9: checked accesses and access type checks decided by the facts engine

| Field | Record |
|---|---|
| Decision | The ADR-221 mechanism extends to `checked.load/store.bits.le` (pointer provenance and lifetime, contract, element size, the bootstrap's alignment-1 rule, the 32-bit offset, permission, value type, frontier, effect continuation, and the whole-view initialization of checked loads) and to the type and continuation checks of plain loads and stores (`MEMORY-LOAD-TYPE`, `MEMORY-STORE-TYPE`, `MEMORY-EFFECT-TYPE`). Type CIDs are rendered from the typing stream's indices. An offset type the program did not decode as `bits` still declines, and a record the host cannot render makes `memory_rejection` return None, so the bootstrap decides. |
| Evidence | EXECUTED on Linux x86-64 with the natively lowered typing image: one constructed program per new rule (8) matches the bootstrap's full diagnostic and is decided by the engine; the eight-seed corpus run gives 820 engine-decided rejections out of 824, with no mismatch. Both self-hosting evidence files re-run natively. |
| Limits | Still declined to the bootstrap: heap-view construction, rebase windows, returned views, foreign and view-passing calls, atomics, links, and stored-pointer provenance. Object verification remains Python. |
